# 05 — Full feature parity matrix

This is the required scope, not a list of delivered features. Legacy source references are indexed in [source-inventory.json](source-inventory.json). Source roots: [CMS pages](../../../nuxt4-cms/app/pages), [CMS APIs](../../../nuxt4-cms/server/api), [CMS services](../../../nuxt4-cms/server/services), [website pages](../../../nuxt4-website/app/pages), [website APIs](../../../nuxt4-website/server/api).

The matrix records domain-level coverage. Before implementing a row, enumerate its individual legacy routes, API actions, models, state transitions and edge cases from the inventory and full source. Add target file/test links to that row when implemented. A broad domain row or file count is not proof of exhaustive parity.

**Status as of the reset:** no row is newly runtime-verified. `Partial` means some source exists, potentially with the wrong backend. `Planned` means no complete corresponding flow was established by this review. All business rows target MongoDB/local services, even if current code calls Hub.

**Decisions 2026-11-09:** every `Q<n>` reference below now has a recorded outcome in [ADR 0002](../decisions/0002-owner-decisions.md); read a `Q` as "the decision, not an open question". The four `WAITING_INPUT` facts in that ADR are the only remaining external gaps.

**Update 2026-11-07:** uncommitted CMS foundation work adds local MongoDB content, category and media services plus revisions/audit/idempotency and a verified-context/CSRF gate. The detailed, dated slice records at the end of this file are the authoritative status for those rows; they remain `Partial` until their own gates and browser checks are recorded. The table's "Current target state" column is a summary and must not be read as a Verified claim.

| POR-02 | Supplier portal dashboard/items and scoped supplier workflows | Planned | Optional portal composition + local services / P6 | Supplier cannot inspect/change another supplier's records or private admin fields |

The seven editorial formats were confirmed in legacy source as `Content.format = ['standard','gallery','video','audio','quote','link','dynamic']`, with media types `['video','audio','iframe','embed']` and content types `['post','page','item','project','service']` — see [12 — B2](12-legacy-domain-specs.md#b2-content). Decide explicitly which are in scope for the rebuild; do not reduce the requirement to a generic HTML field, and do not leave `item|project|service` undocumented.

| WEB-06 | Checkout/address/shipping/payment, guest forms/Turnstile where used | Partial (2026-11-20): guest checkout backend on `POST /api/public/checkout` — the server re-quotes the cart against the published catalog, rejects a cart it had to correct (unavailable / out-of-stock / capped quantity) with `CHECKOUT_CART_CHANGED` and the corrected lines, writes an `crm_orders` record plus its business-audit row, links an existing CRM profile by email/phone (never creates one), and derives the order code from a required `Idempotency-Key` so a retry returns the same order; the guest form at `/thanh-toan` (reached from the cart) validates against the same contract, submits with one key per cart contents, shows the corrected lines and re-quotes when the server answers `CHECKOUT_CART_CHANGED`, and confirms with the order code and total (browser golden path 2026-11-20: add to cart → cart → checkout → empty-form validation → place order `WEB-9423BFC2` → 409 on a cart whose product was unpublished mid-form); no payment provider, no shipping-fee/tax calculation, no Turnstile/rate limiting, and a guest order lookup at `/tra-cuu-don` backed by `POST /api/public/order-lookup`, which needs the order code plus the phone used on the order (a wrong phone answers exactly like an unknown code) and returns only the allowlisted order fields — no internal ids, no address, no customer id (browser check 2026-11-20: wrong phone → not-found notice, `+84 90 777 8888` matching the order → code, date, status, payment, items and total); a guest order now also queues its confirmation mail from checkout once the order is written (template `order-confirmation`, one message per order code, reported as `mailQueued: true` only when the mail really entered the queue — 2026-11-21) | Narrow public commands / P5 | Reprice server-side, anti-abuse, idempotency, no duplicate order, unavailable stock rejection |
| WEB-07 | Contact, newsletter, inquiry/lead forms | Partial (2026-11-20): the storefront contact page now carries a real message form backed by `POST /api/public/contact-message`, which stores the message in `crm_contact_messages` with status `new` (source `web-contact-form`, the request's user agent) plus its business-audit row, validates against a shared contract, and answers a filled honeypot with a success while storing nothing — the CMS triage screen already reads that collection; the contact page also carries a newsletter signup (`POST /api/public/newsletter/subscribe`) that stores a `pending` subscription with a SHA-256 token hash and queues exactly one confirmation message into the CMS's own `crm_mail_deliveries` (template `newsletter-confirm`, locale `vi`, dedupe key per confirmation token, so a repeat signup's mail carries the token that is actually stored), then reports whether that mail was really queued instead of claiming a subscription, with `/newsletter/xac-nhan?token=` (`POST /api/public/newsletter/confirm`) flipping it to `confirmed` once — a reused or forged token answers 404 (browser check 2026-11-20: subscribe with consent → `Check your inbox…` and one queued delivery, confirmation link → confirmed, same link again → expired notice, signup without a template → state `confirmation_not_queued` with the pending row kept; a missing name is greeted neutrally instead of echoing the address, and concurrent signups for one address upsert into the same row rather than failing); opting out now has its own token and page, because confirmation burns the confirmation token: a signup also stores a SHA-256 hash of an unsubscribe token, the confirmation mail carries `/newsletter/huy-dang-ky?token=` (`POST /api/public/newsletter/unsubscribe`), and a pending or confirmed subscription becomes `unsubscribed` with consent cleared and its confirmation link retired; a second click on that link answers success instead of looking broken (2026-11-21) (browser check 2026-11-20: form submitted → `Your message was received`, one row with the typed values, one audit row; honeypot POST → 201 and no row; invalid body → 422) | Forms + communications / P3/P8 | Zod validation, spam controls, consent, local storage/delivery outcome, provider failure handling |
| WEB-08 | SEO metadata/canonical/OG/schema/sitemap/robots/hreflang | Partial: canonical/OG/JSON-LD on content, a locale-free sitemap that now includes product, tag and category URLs, `noindex` on search and preview, and a dynamic `robots.txt` (2026-11-08); Product/CollectionPage JSON-LD, a `BreadcrumbList` and `ItemList` on the pages that render a trail or a listing, a `WebSite` home block, absolute per-record share images and and every sitemap row — records, listings, home and tags — dated by its own newest record (2026-11-11); hreflang is intentionally not emitted because storefront URLs carry no locale prefix | Web shared SEO policy / P3 onward | Public URLs only, correct locale canonical policy, preview noindex, drafts omitted, real HTTP status |
| WEB-09 | Cache/ISR/revalidation from CMS edits | Partial: content responses use a 30s/300s TTL window; settings/menu/catalog/content writes purge only the storefront cache scopes their reason names, per tenant (content added 2026-11-17); no signed or durable events yet | Customer/site/locale scoped cache + durable events / P3/P9 | Publish/edit/unpublish observed in Web; replay rejection; unrelated customer unaffected; provider cache tested |
| WEB-10 | PWA/manifest/icons/offline shell/update | Partial: the storefront worker installs on a production build, takes control of the page, answers offline navigations with `public/offline.html` (URL preserved, retry control) and caches no API response and no SSR document — 86 precached entries, all static assets, plus the offline page in its own cache (WEB-10, 2026-11-12); a second build served over the first on the same port replaced the precache from 86 entries of build A to exactly build B's 86 entries after a single page reload, with no manual unregister, and the offline fallback kept working; a second consecutive update that replaced a chunk the previous build had precached also swapped the cache entry for entry, verified by comparing the cache's `path@revision` fingerprint to the served manifest; the same swap also happens when every tab is closed between the two builds, the reopened page reloading itself onto the new precache (2026-11-12). Cross-customer isolation is now checked from the built bytes — `pnpm test:pwa-isolation` asserts the precache lists only emitted files, no API/document is cached, and no other customer's identifiers or branding binaries appear in `public/` (24/24 on the sentuan build, badged by three negative probes; 2026-11-12). Still partial: install-to-home-screen UI and any equivalent CMS worker | Host worker + customer assets / P9 | Fresh browser install/control/offline/update tests; no auth/private/API cache; no cross-customer assets |
| WEB-11 | Custom websites, CMS-only installations, multi-site publication | Partial: every enabled app in the registry now builds and validates — 3 storefronts with a 24/24 isolation check each and 5 CMS builds with their manifest, including the two CMS-only installations (thanhtung, hoangvi) that had never been built here before (build matrix, 2026-11-12); the custom-Web fixture and multi-site publication are still open; registry/customer overrides | Resolver/composition + site model / P2–P9 | Minimal custom Web fixture, CMS-only build, site-specific content/SEO; no obligatory storefront UI |
| WEB-12 | Responsive layout, i18n vi/en, accessibility and error UX | Partial: baseline + states landed 2026-11-13 | Every UI slice | Browser tests on desktop/mobile, keyboard/focus/labels, both locales, loading/empty/error/forbidden states — `/`, `/products`, product detail, `/categories/<slug>`, `/bai-viet`, `/gio-hang` and 404 pass the label/language/heading sweep (`08`); empty/unavailable/404 states and 390/1280 px widths were checked the same day (pagination overflow fixed); the 410 branch was then exercised with a real CMS redirect row, and colour contrast was measured and fixed in the design tokens, so only the pending affordance remains open |

## Optional additions, not substitutes for legacy parity

AI Workspace/public AI appeared in prior planning but is not evidence of migrated legacy functionality. If retained, its business records, provider integration, budgets and audit belong in tm-websites, not Hub business APIs. It requires separate acceptance for permission-aware data access, bounded usage and confirmation of writes. It must not delay or replace CMS/ERP parity by silently expanding scope.

## Per-row completion record

For each row, append: exact legacy source paths; target contract/model/service/API/page paths; dependency rows; migrations; permission names; test names and commands; browser scenarios; tested revision/customer/environment; remaining gaps. Only mark Verified when all applicable acceptance criteria pass. Unsupported provider setup remains Blocked, never a mock PASS.

### IAM-01 — P1.a record (2026-11-06)

- Legacy source: `nuxt4-cms` reimplemented local login/JWT/roles; only the login/TOTP UX and route gating semantics are preserved, not the credential authority.
- Target files: `tm-cms/server/utils/hubClient.ts` (per-request `tm-hub-client@1.0.9` adapter, login/TOTP/me/refresh/logout, `APP_MISMATCH` and tenant-binding rejection), `tm-cms/server/utils/csrf.ts` (same-origin mutation guard), `tm-cms/server/api/auth/*` (unchanged cookies/DTOs plus the guard), `tm-shared/packages/flow-utils/src/hub.ts` (`isHubProxyPathDenied`), `tm-cms/server/api/hub/[...path].ts`.
- Tests: `pnpm test:iam` (27 checks, stubbed transport, no network). Browser: login form → Control Center on `:4005` with a local `tm-hub`, console clean.
- Environment: local Hub `:4000` (`db: ok`), customer `sentuan`, uncommitted working tree.
- Remaining: passkey/registration contracts untouched; refresh rotation is per-request (no cross-instance coordination); revocation/freshness is not authoritative — BLOCKED on the Hub contract; CSRF has no double-submit token; Web portal auth BFF absent. Status: **Partial**, not Verified.
- P1.b (same day): `tm-cms/server/utils/context.ts` now holds the only session gate (`resolveSession` refresh-once semantics, `requireVerifiedContext`, `requireCapabilities`); `session.get.ts` delegates to it and mock auth is refused outside development. `pnpm test:iam` covers 33 checks including refresh-once, expiry, app mismatch not retried and Hub-outage behaviour.

### P2.a — business foundation record (2026-11-06)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/utils/database.ts` (registry-owned database name, `TM_MONGO_DB` only allowed to repeat it, per-binding connection cache, fail-closed errors), `tm-cms/nuxt.config.ts` (server-only `mongoUri`/`mongoDbName`/`mongoDbOverride`), `tm-cms/server/api/health.get.ts`.
- Tests: `pnpm test:db` — 6 binding unit checks plus a live two-database isolation test against local MongoDB 8.2.3 (own document visible, foreign document invisible, same binding reuses its connection, both disposable databases dropped afterwards).
- Runtime: `/api/health` returned `{ status: ok, hub: configured, database: unconfigured }` without a Mongo env and `{ status: ok, hub: configured, database: ok }` with `TM_MONGO_URI` and `TM_MONGO_DB=sen-tuan`.
- Remaining: no shared domain package or validated model yet (see the owner questions), no audit/idempotency collections, no replica-set transaction test, CMS-only customers (`default`, `thanhtung`, `hoangvi`) have no registry database so their binding reports unconfigured. Status: **Partial**.

### CNT-01 — P2.b content model record (2026-11-06)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/models/content.ts` (Zod draft schema, slug/key normalization reusing `normalizeContentKey` from `@tm/flow-contracts`, publication state machine, public projection allowlist, content schema with three declared indexes, per-connection model factory), `tm-cms/server/models/migrations.ts` (migration plan/apply with id+version+checksum), `tm-cms/server/utils/modelRegistry.ts` (`prepareDatabase`, `withPreparedDb`).
- Physical schema (version 1): collection `content` — `siteId`, `type` (`page|post|fragment`), `key`, `status` (`draft|scheduled|published|archived|deleted`), `locales[]` (`locale`, normalized `slug`, `title`, `excerpt`, `body`, `seo`), `categories[]`, `tags[]`, `attributes{}`, `publishAt`, `unpublishAt`, `publishedAt`, `deletedAt`, `revision`, `schemaVersion`, timestamps. Unique `(siteId, key)` and `(siteId, 'locales.locale', 'locales.slug')`; query index `(siteId, status, publishedAt)`. Migration records live in `tm_migrations`.
- Invariants covered by tests: normalized slug/key rejection, duplicate locale rejection, inverted publish window rejection, publish requires a titled locale and must not precede `publishAt`, schedule requires `publishAt`, unpublish clears `publishedAt`, archive/restore only through draft, delete is recoverable and invisible publicly, monotonic revision, public projection carries only allowlisted fields, and client-supplied `status`/`revision`/`publishedAt`/`deletedAt` are dropped at the boundary.
- Tests: `pnpm test:content` — 19 pure checks plus 6 live checks on local MongoDB 8.2.3 (indexes created, duplicate key and duplicate locale slug rejected by the database, migration record contents, idempotent second preparation). Disposable `tm_test_content` database dropped afterwards.
- Not covered yet: admin API/editor (P3), rich-format rendering and sanitization (CNT-02), revision history, optimistic-concurrency rejection on a stale `revision` in a write path, replica-set transactions. Status: **Partial** — model only, no user-facing surface.

Related open rows this touches: SYS-02, WEB-02, WEB-09 and WEB-11 still depend on the P3 content/newest-content read path, not on this model alone.

### CNT-01 — P3.a CMS content slice record (2026-11-06)

- New target files: `tm-cms/server/services/contentService.ts` (list with cursor + status buckets, create, get, update with revision concurrency, publication events, public read), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/content/**` (5 routes, capability + CSRF guarded), `tm-cms/server/utils/contentApi.ts`, `tm-cms/server/utils/site.ts`, `tm-cms/server/utils/modelRegistry.ts`, `tm-cms/server/models/content.ts`, `tm-cms/server/models/migrations.ts`.

UI client: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/composables/useContentAdmin.ts`; page rewritten at `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/content.vue`.
- Routes and error contract: documented in [04-data-and-api.md](04-data-and-api.md#implemented-admin-content-api-v1).
- Tests: `pnpm test:content` — 25 model/invariant checks plus 17 live service checks against local MongoDB 8.2.3 (create, duplicate key/slug `409`, invalid payload `422`, cross-site `404`, revision conflict `409`, immutable key, publish/unpublish, schedule past `422`, publish ahead of schedule `422`, archive→draft-only, soft delete + restore, cursor pagination without duplicates, filters and counts, invalid cursor `422`).

Console showed no error after the final reload.
- API smoke on the same server: `login 200`, `GET list 200` with empty counts, `POST 201`, `PATCH 200` (revision 2), stale `PATCH 409 CONTENT_REVISION_CONFLICT`, `publish 200`, `schedule past 422 CONTENT_PUBLISH_AT_PAST`, cross-origin `POST 403 CSRF_ORIGIN_MISMATCH`, `status=published` list returns 1.

### CNT-01 / WEB-02 / WEB-09 — P3.b public content read record (2026-11-07)

- Legacy source: the Web catch-all page (`nuxt4-website/app/pages/[...slug].vue` and its content API) fetched content through the Hub. Its URL behaviour is preserved; its data source is replaced.

- Tests: `pnpm test:web-content` — 24 contract checks (locale normalization, allowlist, format rejection, sitemap entry shape) plus 12 live reader checks on local MongoDB 8.2.3 in a disposable `tm_test_public_content` database (published/inside window, draft/scheduled/archived/deleted invisible, locale fallback, key and slug resolution, projection allowlist, sitemap de-duplication).
- HTTP on the Web dev server (`:4006`, customer `sentuan`): `/pages/gioi-thieu` `200` with `<title>Giới thiệu Sen Tuấn</title>`; `/gioi-thieu` `200` (slug resolution); `/pages/dich-vu` `404` while the record is a draft; `?locale=en` on a `vi`-only record `200` (fallback); `/sitemap.xml` listed only the published record.
- Body format, verified end-to-end on the same stack: the CMS editor select persisted `format: html` for `pages/gioi-thieu` and the Web rendered a real `<p>Cập nhật</p>`; switching the same locale to `format: markdown` with `# Xin chào` + `**Markdown**` rendered `<h1>Xin chào</h1>` and `<strong>Markdown</strong>`; the record was then restored to `format: html` with the original body. `format: text` is implemented as preserved line breaks but was only inspected in code, not exercised in the browser.
- Gates re-run after the change: `pnpm test:content` (42), `pnpm test:web-content` (24+12), `pnpm test:layers`, typecheck for `tm-cms`/`tm-web`/`tm-shared`, CMS lint, `validate:build:cms` and `build:cms` (32s).
- Remaining gaps (why this stays Partial): no typed renderers for the other legacy editorial formats (CNT-02), no category/tag listing routes, no durable revalidation events (the 30s/300s TTL is the only freshness mechanism), and no import of the existing Hub-authored records (Q5). Only `sentuan` was exercised because `default`/`thanhtung`/`hoangvi` still have no registry database (Q2).

### OPS-01 — P2.c audit write path record (2026-11-13)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/models/audit.ts` (`reason` field, `AUDIT_REASON_MAX`), `tm-cms/server/services/contentAudit.ts` (`ContentAuditMeta.reason`, `normalizeAuditReason`), `tm-cms/server/services/auditService.ts`, `tm-cms/server/utils/contentApi.ts` (`writeMetaOf` reads `x-audit-reason`), `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/{audit-admin,content-admin}.ts`, `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/audit.vue`, `scripts/build/test-audit-write.ts`, `package.json`, `scripts/build/run-gates.ts`.

The note is normalised once (trim, control characters to spaces, whitespace collapsed, 500-character cap, blank → `null`). The Audit Log page shows it above the actor line, and both audit read models expose `reason`.
- Tests: `pnpm test:audit-write` — 11 checks on a disposable database: the three category actions, a refused delete writing nothing, the three media actions, `null` when no note is given, the list returning both types with actor and reason, key search, site isolation, and the normaliser's edge cases.
- Not done at the time: no CMS screen sent a reason, and content/settings/menu rows recorded it only when a caller set the header. Superseded 2026-11-16: all eight write screens collect the optional note, verified end to end through the dialogs (see `08`).

### OPS-01 — P2.c audit read surface record (2026-11-08)

- Target files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/audit-admin.ts` (`AdminAuditEntrySchema`, `AdminAuditQuerySchema`, `ADMIN_AUDIT_RESOURCE_TYPES`/`ACTIONS`, limit 1–100 default 30), `tm-cms/server/services/auditService.ts` (`listAuditEntries`, `AuditQueryError`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/audit/index.get.ts`, `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/composables/useAuditAdmin.ts`, `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/audit.vue`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json`, `scripts/build/test-audit-service.ts`.
- Behaviour: the Audit Log page now reads the site's own `audit_logs` instead of the Hub IAM log proxy, so the entries the content/category/media commands write are finally visible. Filters: resource type, action, actor id, exact resource id, a bounded search over resource key/id and actor name/email, and a `from`/`to` range. Paging uses the row `_id` as a cursor (`$lt`, newest first), so a new entry arriving mid-session cannot duplicate or skip a row; `total` is the filter count and is returned with every page.
- Capability: `apps.logs.read` on every request; the page shows the same permission message when the session lacks it. The response is `no-store` and never contains tokens, sessions or request bodies — only actor identity, the revision the action produced, from/to status, changed fields and the request id.
- Tests: `pnpm test:audit` — 7 live checks in a disposable database: site isolation with newest-first order, resource/action filters, case-insensitive search over key and actor (including the same key on another site), actor and date-range filters, cursor paging with no repeats and a filter-wide `total`, an invalid cursor rejected as `AUDIT_QUERY_INVALID`, and a stored transition/request id preserved.
- Live HTTP (CMS `:4005`, mock auth, `sentuan`, probe record): `create` then `publish` → `2` entries, top row `content audit-probe rev 2 draft → published` with `['status','publishedAt']` and the session actor; `resourceType=content&action=publish` → `1`; `actorId` → `2`; `q=AUDIT-PROBE` (upper case) → `2`; a 2020 range → `0`; `limit=1` paged `[publish]` then `[create]` with `hasMore` false on the last page; `resourceType=foo`, `limit=101`, `from=not-a-date`, `cursor=zzz` → `422`; anonymous → `401`.
- Browser (`:4005/audit`, mock session): 2 rows with action badge, resource type, key, revision, actor, `draft → published`, changed-field chips and time; stats read 2 entries / 1 actor / 1 record; the action select filtered to the single `Publish` row and the search box showed the empty state at 0 matches; all audit requests returned `200` with the expected query string. Probe content, audit entries and revisions were removed afterwards (`content 2 / media 0 / audit 0 / revisions 0 / items 241`).
- Remaining gaps (why OPS-01 stays Partial): retention is a manual operator command with the decided periods (50 snapshots, 365 audit days — Q16), not a scheduled policy; no export/import, no background jobs, and category/media audit is only readable through the shared page (no per-record history endpoint yet).

### OPS-01 — P2.c retention command record (2026-11-08)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/services/retentionService.ts` (`pruneRetention`, `normalizeRetentionOptions`, `RETENTION_LIMITS`, `RetentionError`), `scripts/ops/prune-retention.ts`, `scripts/build/test-retention-service.ts`, `package.json` scripts `test:retention` and `prune:retention`.
- Behaviour: revision snapshots are trimmed per content record to the newest `keepRevisions` (1–500, default 50), computed from `max(revision)` per `contentId`, so a record under the cap is never touched. `audit_logs` older than `auditDays` (1–3650, default 365) are removed. Every query is scoped to one site, the run is a dry run unless `--apply` is passed, and a second run deletes nothing.
- Live CLI (`sen-tuan`): dry run reported `0` removable for both collections without deleting; `--apply --keep-revisions 2 --audit-days 90` also reported `0` and left the database unchanged; `--keep-revisions 0` exited non-zero. The disposable-database suite (`pnpm test:retention`, 5 checks) covers the cap, the audit cutoff, site isolation, dry run, idempotent re-run and invalid options.
- Remaining gaps (why this stays Partial): the periods are decided (50/365 per Q16) but nothing schedules the command, so unbounded growth is prevented only when an operator runs it; there is no per-site policy field in a settings model and no `audit_logs` export before pruning.

### OPS-01 — P2.c audit and idempotency record (2026-11-07)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/models/audit.ts` (`audit_logs`, actor subdocument, two site-scoped indexes), `tm-cms/server/models/idempotency.ts` (`idempotency_keys`, unique `(siteId, key)`, TTL on `expiresAt`), `tm-cms/server/models/migrations.ts` (migration `content-audit-idempotency-indexes` v2), `tm-cms/server/services/contentAudit.ts` (`recordContentAudit`, `listContentAudit`, `contentFields`/`diffContentFields`), `tm-cms/server/utils/idempotency.ts` (`normalizeIdempotencyKey`, `digestPayload`, `executeIdempotent`), `tm-cms/server/utils/contentApi.ts` (`auditMetaOf`, `requestIdOf`, `idempotencyKeyOf`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/content/**` (5 routes wired, new `history.get.ts`).
- Contract: `AdminContentAuditSchema` + `ADMIN_CONTENT_AUDIT_ACTIONS` in `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/content-admin.ts`; CMS shows the trail in the record modal (`Activity` panel with action, revision, actor, changed fields and time).
- Behaviour: create/update/event each write one audit entry inside the request, with the actor from the verified session (never the request body), the resulting revision, from/to status and the top-level fields that changed (`type`, `locales`, `categories`, `tags`, `attributes`, `publishAt`, `unpublishAt`, `status`, `publishedAt`, `deletedAt`). The audit model is optional on the service so command paths without a request stay usable.
- Retry safety: `Idempotency-Key` on a mutation runs the command at most once per site for 24h; replay returns the stored status and body with `Idempotent-Replay: true`; a reused key with a different payload is `409`, a malformed key `422`. The unique index (not a read-then-write check alone) decides concurrent winners.
- Tests: `pnpm test:content` — 25 model checks (now migration-set agnostic) plus 22 live service checks in a disposable database, including: audit entry per mutation with actor/revision, `changedFields` for create/update/publish, both publish entries preserved (`draft→published` and `scheduled→published`), newest-first ordering, site-scoped history, one execution for a retried key, `409` on payload mismatch, `422` on a malformed key, and a single stored record after the retry.
- Live HTTP (CMS `:4005`, `sentuan`): `POST` with `Idempotency-Key` → `201`; the same request again → `201` + `Idempotent-Replay: true`; only one record exists; `PATCH` with a new key → `revision 2`; `GET /:id/history` returned `[update (draft→draft, locales, tags, requestId probe-2), create (—→draft, type, locales, requestId probe-1)]` with the session user as actor; the CMS modal rendered both entries. Probe record, its audit entries and its keys were removed afterwards.
- Remaining gaps (why this is Partial): content, category, media, settings and menu writes are audited, but import/export and background jobs are not; the operator reason is now collected by every CMS write screen and verified end to end (2026-11-16); no idempotency for Web/public writes; and no test with two concurrent clients on the same key (the duplicate-insert path is covered by unit logic, not by a race test). Retention is settled (Q16) and documented in `08`.

### SYS-02 — P2.d site settings record (2026-11-08)

- Target files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/settings-admin.ts` (`AdminSiteSettingsSchema`, `AdminSiteSettingsPatchSchema`, `SETTINGS_LOCALES`, `SITE_MODULES`, `defaultSiteSettings`), `tm-cms/server/models/settings.ts`, migration `settings-indexes` v6, `tm-cms/server/utils/modelRegistry.ts` (`Settings`), `tm-cms/server/services/settingsService.ts` (`getSiteSettings`, `updateSiteSettings`, `SettingsServiceError`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/settings/{index.get,index.patch}.ts`, `cms-base/app/composables/useSettingsAdmin.ts`, `cms-base/app/pages/configs.vue`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json`, `scripts/build/test-settings-service.ts`.
- Behaviour: one document per site (unique `siteId`), sections `company` (display/legal name plus per-locale `names`, tax id, email, phone, address, logo id), `seo` (title template, default description, default image id, and their per-locale maps), `locales` (default + enabled, canonical order, the default must stay enabled) and `modules` (content/catalog/orders/inventory). `revision` is the optimistic lock and `updatedBy` the session actor; the audit resource type `settings` was added so the Audit Log page filters it like the other domains. `GET` returns defaults at `revision: 0` for an unsaved site, so the first save is an upsert; the unique index decides a concurrent first save and the loser gets `409`.
- Capabilities: `configs.read` to read, `configs.write` to save. **Hub roles need `configs.write` granted** before a real (non-mock) admin can save; the mock session's `*` hides that in local checks.
- CMS UI: `/configs` keeps the Hub config list but gains a *Site settings* card (company/SEO fields, default locale select, enabled-locale checkboxes that cannot disable the default, module switches, Save with inline result). A Hub-side failure is now shown inside the Hub card instead of blanking the page, so the settings card still works when Hub is unreachable.
- Tests: `pnpm test:settings` — 8 live checks in a disposable database: defaults at revision 0, first save + one audit entry with actor/changed sections, partial merge that keeps untouched sections, stale revision, empty diff, invalid locale combinations, payload validation (email, unknown locale) and site isolation.
- Live HTTP (CMS `:4005`, mock auth, `sentuan`): `GET` → defaults `revision 0`; `PATCH` `revision 0` → `200` `revision 1` with the session actor and `locales.enabled ['vi','en']`; the same request with the same `Idempotency-Key` → `200` + `Idempotent-Replay: true` and still `revision 1`; stale revision → `409`; unchanged → `422`; bad email, unknown locale → `422`; the audit endpoint showed `settings/site-settings` revisions 1 and 2 with the changed sections.
- Browser (`:4005/configs`): the card rendered the defaults, saving a display name + EN + catalog produced "Site settings saved." and `Settings revision 1`, and the API confirmed the stored document. A second save without edits showed "There is nothing to save."; after another client bumped the revision, saving from the stale form showed the conflict message. Probe document, audit entries and idempotency keys were removed afterwards (`settings 0 / audit 0 / keys 0`).
- Media fields: the logo and default SEO image are picked from the media library in a dialog that lists images (`kind: image`, searchable) and stores only the media id; the stored label falls back to the id when the file is not on the loaded page, and a clear button empties the field. Verified in the browser with a probe upload (`brand/logo-probe.png`): picking it filled the field, saving stored `company.logoMediaId`, and the probe file, settings document, audit entries and keys were removed afterwards.
- Remaining gaps (why SYS-02 stays Partial): the settings' SEO descriptions and title templates are wired into the storefront, while the default image id is projected but not resolved to a media URL (Q13); the logo id has the same blocker, and there is no import of Hub's existing config values (Q5). `default`/`thanhtung`/`hoangvi` still lack a registry database, so only `sentuan` was exercised (Q2).

### P2.d — pagination type reaches the storefront (2026-11-26)

- `settings.site.paginationType` was stored and editable but read by nothing. The public projection now publishes it as `cursor` | `offset` (`site-public.ts`: `PUBLIC_PAGINATION_TYPES`, `normalizePublicPaginationType`, `PublicSite.site.paginationType`), and the CMS normalizes the legacy `skip_limit` value to `offset` on read and on write (`settingsService.ts`), so a migrated document no longer ships a value the editor's select cannot show.
- Consumer: `/bai-viet` reads `usePublicSite().paginationType`. `cursor` keeps the load-more button and the cursor API; `offset` requests `?page=N`, renders `UPagination`, and makes a paged URL (`/bai-viet?page=2`) its own canonical and indexable, while a filtered view stays on the bare canonical. `tm-web/server/api/public/content` accepts `page` (1–500) and returns `meta.page`/`meta.total`; the cursor path is unchanged and pays no extra count query.
- Tests: `pnpm test:web-site` 29 checks (legacy `skip_limit`, `offset`, unknown → `cursor`, contract parse); `pnpm test:web-content` 28 model/helper + 20 live reader checks (paged and cursor meta shapes). `pnpm test:web-site-db` 6 checks. `pnpm typecheck` for `tm-cms` and `tm-web` passes.
- Live `sen-tuan` (Web `:4006`, stored value `cursor`): `GET /api/public/site` → `site.paginationType: "cursor"`; `GET /api/public/content?type=post&limit=1&page=1` → `meta.page 1, meta.total 1`; `?page=2` → empty data with `page 2, total 1`; the cursor request carries no `page`/`total`. `/bai-viet` and `/bai-viet?page=2` render `200`; with `cursor` the page renders no pager, so the `offset` branch was verified at the API level only and was not viewed in a browser.
- Still open: `/tim-kiem` is a bounded two-section preview (content + products) on a page robots already disallow, so both of its sections keep the load-more button by design; the searchable content listing that honours the mode is `/bai-viet?q=`. The legacy importer still leaves `lastImportPrice` at 0 (`08`).

### P2.e — the mode reaches the category and tag landings (2026-11-27)

- Both remaining content landings now read the same setting as `/bai-viet`: `/danh-muc/<slug>` and `/tag/<tag>` request `?page=N` when `paginationType` is `offset`, render `UPagination` against `meta.total`, make `?page=N` their own canonical and keep `page` as the only indexable query key; `cursor` keeps the load-more button and pays no count query.
- Root cause fixed while wiring them: `paginationType` came from `usePublicSite()`, which falls back to `cursor` until its async read resolves, so a server render painted the cursor mode (and the bare canonical) on a `?page=` URL and only switched after hydration — `/bai-viet` had the same latent bug. `usePublicSite.ts` now exports `usePublicSiteData()` (the one shared read) and `usePublicPaginationType()`, which awaits it; the three listing pages use the awaited form, so the SSR HTML already matches the stored mode.
- `usePublicContentCategories()`/`usePublicSite()` are unchanged for every other page, and the public content API needed no change: `page` already combined with `tag`, `category` and `q`.
- Tests: `pnpm test:web-content` 22 live reader checks, two new ones proving a tag facet and a category facet page with skip/limit, a count, no overlap and a page past the end. `pnpm typecheck` passes for `tm-cms` and `tm-web`.
- Live SSR (`sen-tuan`, Web dev on `localhost:4006`): with the stored mode temporarily set to `offset`, `/bai-viet?page=2` and `/tag/khong-co?page=2` returned `200` with canonical `…/bai-viet?page=2` and `…/tag/khong-co?page=2`, while `/bai-viet` stayed on the bare canonical. After restoring `cursor`, both page-2 URLs canonicalised back to the bare path. The setting was restored to `cursor` afterwards and the database value re-read to confirm it. `sen-tuan` holds no published posts and no content categories, so no pager could render and `/danh-muc/<slug>` returned `404`; the category pager branch was not exercised with rows, and no page was viewed in a browser.
- Gotcha for the next check: `/api/public/site` is a cached handler (`maxAge 60`, `staleMaxAge 300`, SWR) and the dev server keeps that cache on disk under `tm-web/.nuxt/cache/nitro`. A settings change is not visible to SSR until the entry expires or that directory is cleared — two probe reads looked wrong until it was.

### CAT-05 — internal import price reaches the catalog (2026-11-27)

- Why: `site.allowManualImportPrice` had no consumer because the v2 catalog carried no cost column, so a store that wanted to type a purchase cost had nowhere to put it and the legacy receipt update had no target.
- Field: catalog items and their variants gained `lastImportPrice` (non-negative, default 0) in `models/catalog.ts` and `flow-contracts/catalog-admin.ts`. It is internal: the public projection in `tm-web` is unchanged, so no storefront response or page can read it.
- Who may write it: `createCatalogItem`/`updateCatalogItem` only accept the submitted number when `CatalogWriteMeta.allowManualImportPrice` is true. Both admin routes read `site.allowManualImportPrice` through `getSiteSettings` and pass it, so an editor with the setting off keeps the stored cost even if a stale form or a hand-written request sends a different value; for variants the stored price is restored by SKU, because the editor replaces the whole variant set.
- Who writes it otherwise: `recordReceivedImportPrice` in `catalogService.ts` sets it from a received purchase-order line (`unitCost`) on the item and, matched by SKU, on its variant. `receivePurchaseOrder` calls it after the order is committed and only when `meta.catalogItemModel` is passed (the receive route passes `CatalogItem`); it is best-effort, like the payable auto-post, because stock and the order are already written and an unlinked legacy item must not fail the receipt. This path ignores the setting — the price actually paid is never a manual entry.
- CMS UI: `/catalog` shows a *Giá nhập nội bộ* field next to the sale price and one column per variant, disabled with a hint unless the site allows manual entry; the page reads the same policy object that already gates SKU and stock edits.
- Tests: `pnpm test:catalog` 53 checks (locked writes ignored, allowed writes accepted, variant prices, receipt writes item + variant, an omitted cost keeps the stored one, locked editor preserves the receipt price, unlinked ids ignored) and `pnpm test:procurement` 27 checks (a receipt sets the catalog cost and still moves stock). `pnpm typecheck` passes for `tm-cms` and `tm-web`. Not viewed in a browser: the disabled field and hint were not opened.
- Import note: the legacy catalog importer still leaves `lastImportPrice` at 0; carrying the legacy value is a separate decision (`08`).

### CNT-01 / P2.d — category and media history endpoints (2026-11-08)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/services/contentAudit.ts` (`listRecordAudit`, with `listContentAudit` now a thin wrapper), `tm-cms/server/services/mediaService.ts` (`getMedia`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/categories/[id]/history.get.ts`, `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/media/[id]/history.get.ts`, `cms-base/app/components/RecordHistory.vue` (used by the two edit dialogs), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.history.*`), `scripts/build/test-audit-service.ts`.
- Behaviour: one read-only trail per record, newest first, capped at 50. The resource type is part of the filter — an id alone is ambiguous across collections — and the record is resolved first, so another site's or a malformed id fails the way that domain already does (`CATEGORY_NOT_FOUND` / `MEDIA_NOT_FOUND` / `*_ID_INVALID`) instead of returning an empty list. Capabilities are the read pair the domain already uses (`content.read`, `media.read`).
- Tests: `pnpm test:audit` grew to 9 live checks: the media trail returns only the media row for that id, the content trail excludes the create row of the same key, a matching id under another type returns nothing, the same id on another site is scoped out, the cap survives an over-large `limit`, and an unknown id returns an empty list.
- Live HTTP (CMS `:4005`, mock auth, `sen-tuan`): a probe category created directly and then `PATCH`ed to `revision 2` returned one `update` row with the mock admin actor and `changedFields: ["parent","order","locales"]`; a probe media record `PATCH`ed to `revision 2` returned one `update` row with `changedFields: ["title"]`; an unknown media id returned `404 MEDIA_NOT_FOUND`, a malformed one `422`, and an anonymous request `401`.
- Browser (`:4005`): the category edit dialog rendered an *Activity* block with `Edited / Revision 2 / Quản trị viên / parent, order, locales` and a timestamp, and the media edit dialog rendered the same block with `title`. A failed read shows the failure text instead of an empty list, which is why the component does not fall back silently. Probe category, media record and their audit rows were removed afterwards (`categories 0 / media 0 / audit 0`).
- Remaining gaps: there is still no revision snapshot for either domain, the trail is capped at the newest 50 rows and is not paginated, and the audit page remains the only way to see activity across records rather than per record.

### P2.d — one title template for every page (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `flow-utils/src/seo.ts` (`applySeoTitleTemplate`), `web-base/app/composables/useSiteSeoTitle.ts`, `tm-web/app/app.vue`, `scripts/build/test-web-site.ts`.
- Shape: `%s` is the page's own title, so `%s | Sen Tuấn` reproduces the legacy `Page | Company` pattern. An empty template or the `%s` default means "no suffix", so a site that has not saved settings keeps bare titles; a template without a placeholder is treated as the suffix.
- Applied once in `app.vue` (`useHead(() => ({ titleTemplate }))`) rather than in every page: pages keep ownership of their title, and the root head entry covers every layout and route including the catch-all content pages.
- Two details the editor would otherwise hit: an empty page title does not leave a dangling `| Sen Tuấn` behind (separators are trimmed), and a title that already names the company is left alone — the home page is only the brand, so a suffix would have read `Sen Tuấn · Sen Tuấn`, and a content page titled `Giới thiệu Sen Tuấn` keeps its wording.
- `og:title` deliberately stays the page's own title: a share card should not repeat the company suffix that the document title needs for disambiguation.
- Tests: `pnpm test:web-site` 11 checks (placeholder, bare `%s`, empty template, whitespace, template without placeholder, empty page title, brand already present, prefix template).
- Live: a seeded settings document with `seo.titleTemplate = '%s · Sen Tuấn'` turned `Products` into `Products · Sen Tuấn` and `Danh mục` into `Danh mục · Sen Tuấn`, while `/` stayed `Sen Tuấn` and `/gioi-thieu` stayed `Giới thiệu Sen Tuấn`; deleting the document reverted every title to the bare form, so nothing is hardcoded. The probe document was removed afterwards (`settings 0 / menus 0 / audit 0`).
- The settings field previews the result while it is typed (`Preview: Trang mẫu | Sen Tuấn`), using the same `applySeoTitleTemplate` as the storefront, so an editor does not have to publish to see what a page title will look like.
- A template can now be set per locale: `seo.titleTemplates` overrides the shared `titleTemplate` for a locale, an empty entry falls back to it, and the storefront picks the template for its current locale. See the per-locale slice below.
- A save now clears the storefront cache itself: the CMS calls the storefront's `POST /api/_internal/purge` after a settings or menu write, so the new template, brand or menu is visible on the next request instead of after the cache window. See the purge slice below.

### P2.d — a title template per locale (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/settings-admin.ts` (`seo.titleTemplates`), `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/site-public.ts` (`readTitleTemplates`, `PublicSite.seo.titleTemplates`), `tm-cms/server/models/settings.ts`, `tm-cms/server/services/settingsService.ts` (`normalizeTitleTemplates`, `mergeSeoSettings`), `cms-base/app/pages/configs.vue`, `web-base/app/composables/useSiteSeoTitle.ts`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json`, `scripts/build/test-{settings-service,web-site}.ts`.
- Shape: `seo.titleTemplate` stays the shared default and `seo.titleTemplates` holds optional per-locale overrides for the enabled locales. A locale with no entry, or with an empty string, keeps using the shared template — so a single-language site never fills in the extra field, and a bilingual site can end an English title with `| Sen Tuan` instead of `· Sen Tuấn`.
- Merge rule: the patch merges `titleTemplates` key by key (`mergeSeoSettings`), because a shallow `seo` merge would drop the Vietnamese override when only the English one is saved; an explicit empty string removes that locale's entry, and a locale that is not in `SETTINGS_LOCALES` is dropped before it reaches the document.
- Storefront: `useSiteSeoTitle` reads the template for `useI18n().locale`, falling back to `titleTemplate`; `buildPublicSite` publishes only enabled locales with a non-empty, trimmed string, so the payload cannot grow keys the site does not serve.
- Editor: the settings page keeps the shared field and adds one field per locale (`SEO title (VI)`, `SEO title (EN)`) whose placeholder is the shared template and whose preview line renders a sample page through that locale's effective template. The English sample title was also corrected from `Trang mẫu` to `Sample page`.
- Tests: `pnpm test:settings` 9 checks (both locales saved, `titleTemplate` untouched, an empty string clears one locale only, an unknown locale cannot change the document); `pnpm test:web-site` 12 checks (published map keeps the enabled non-empty entry, trims it, drops the empty and the not-enabled locale, empty map by default).
- Live (`:4005` → `:4006`, probe settings removed afterwards): with `titleTemplate: '%s · Sen Tuấn'` and `titleTemplates: { en: '%s | Sen Tuan' }`, `GET /products` rendered `<title>Sản phẩm · Sen Tuấn</title>` while the same URL with the `i18n_redirected=en` cookie rendered `<title>Products | Sen Tuan</title>`; the browser settings page showed `Preview: Sample page · Sen Tuấn` for VI and `Preview: Sample page | Sen Tuan` for EN. Deleting the document reverted both to bare titles (`Sản phẩm`, `Products`).
- Still open: the template is still one string per locale (no per-page-type template), and no locale-specific default description or OG image.

### P2.d — a save clears the storefront cache (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/utils/storefrontRevalidate.ts` (`parseRevalidateTarget`, `storefrontPurgeUrl`, `revalidateStorefront`, `revalidateStorefrontForEvent`, `setRevalidateHeader`), `tm-cms/nuxt.config.ts` (`webRevalidateUrl`, `revalidateSecret`), the four admin routes that change what the storefront reads (`settings/index.patch.ts`, `menus/index.post.ts`, `menus/[id].patch.ts`, `menus/[id].delete.ts`), `scripts/build/test-storefront-revalidate.ts`.
- Why this exists: `GET /api/public/site` is a cached Nitro handler (60s / 300s stale) and there is no event bus, so an editor who fixed a menu had to wait a minute. The storefront already shipped a secret-gated `POST /api/_internal/purge`; nothing in the CMS had ever called it.
- Pairing: the CMS needs the storefront base URL (`TM_WEB_REVALIDATE_URL`) **and** the shared secret (`TM_REVALIDATE_SECRET`, the same value as the storefront's `REVALIDATE_SECRET`). With either missing, the call is skipped — never sent with an empty secret — and the response carries `x-storefront-revalidate: not-configured`.
- Best-effort by design: the database write already committed, so a storefront that is down, slow or holding another secret must not fail the save. The result is a value (`revalidated` / `http-error` / `request-failed` / `not-configured`), reported back in `x-storefront-revalidate` and logged as a server warning, and the admin response stays `200`. The request has a 5s timeout.
- Idempotent replays do not purge again: a retried save returns the stored response and reuses the revision, so there is nothing new to invalidate.
- Tests: `pnpm test:revalidate` 8 checks (an unconfigured pair never fetches, a non-`http(s)` URL is refused, normalization/trimming, a path prefix is preserved, a `2xx` posts the secret header and the reason with an abort signal, a `401` and a refused connection are reported instead of thrown, an explicit timeout is accepted).
- Live (`:4005` → `:4006`, `sen-tuan`): with the cache warm at company name `''`, `PATCH /api/v1/admin/settings` returned `200` with `x-storefront-revalidate: revalidated` (the storefront reported `removedCount: 33`) and `GET /api/public/site` answered `Probe Purge Co` on the very next request. `POST /api/v1/admin/menus` likewise purged and the new menu appeared in the site projection immediately; deleting it purged again and the menu list was empty at once. Killing the storefront and repeating the save returned `200` with `x-storefront-revalidate: request-failed` and a `[storefront-revalidate] settings.update: request-failed (fetch failed)` warning, with the new value still saved. A direct purge with no or a wrong secret answers `401`. Probe settings, menu, audit and idempotency rows were removed afterwards (`settings 0 / site_menus 0 / audit 0 / idempotency_keys 0`, legacy `content` 2 untouched).
- The editor is told when only the refresh failed: `useSettingsAdmin.saveSettings` reads `x-storefront-revalidate` from the response and the settings page swaps `admin.configs.site.saved` for `admin.configs.site.savedNotRevalidated` ("… Chưa xoá được cache trang web …"), so a saved-but-stale site never looks like a plain success. A missing header (older server, stripping proxy) counts as success, since the save itself did happen.
- Browser (`:4005/configs`, English UI): with a wrong `TM_REVALIDATE_SECRET` the save showed `Site settings saved. The website cache could not be cleared, so the change will appear in about a minute.` while `revision 2` and the stored name updated; with the matching secret the same save showed `Site settings saved.` Probe settings/audit/idempotency rows were removed afterwards (`settings 0 / audit 0 / idempotency_keys 0`).
- Still open at that point: the purge deleted every handler entry in the cache and was not signed or replay-protected. Both are now addressed for the cache half — see the 2026-11-13 record below. Content PATCH, publish/status events, bulk and revision restore now purge as well (2026-11-17), so a new or removed automatic slug redirect is visible at once; content create stays draft-only until a public transition, and a CDN in front of the storefront would still need its own purge call (Q12). Live against `:4005`/`:4006` with matching secrets: a published page warmed at `/probe-purge-cu` was renamed (`revalidated`) and the next request was `301 /probe-purge-moi`; after warming that path to `301`, bulk-publishing a page that reused the old slug (`revalidated`) made `/probe-purge-cu` answer `200` immediately, without the 30s window. Probes were removed afterwards.

### P2.d — product URLs in the sitemap (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `tm-web/server/routes/sitemap.xml.ts`, `tm-web/server/utils/catalogService.ts` (`listCatalogSitemapEntries`), `tm-web/server/utils/catalogMapper.ts` (`toCatalogSitemapEntry`), `tm-web/server/models/catalog.ts` (`CATALOG_ITEM_SITEMAP_PROJECTION`).
- The sitemap covered published content, tags and content categories, so every product detail page was invisible to crawlers even though `/products/<slug>` is the site's main commercial URL. Product rows are now read straight from the customer `items` collection with the same published filter the public API uses (`isDeleted`, `isActive`, `status: 'published'`), projecting only `slug fullPath updatedAt`.
- The canonical slug comes from the same mapper the product pages use (default-locale slug, else the last segment of `fullPath`), so a sitemap URL always resolves to a `200`. Slugs are de-duplicated and a record without a slug is skipped, and the query is capped at 5000 rows (the sitemap protocol limit) with the rest reachable through listing pages.
- A site without a catalog binding, or an unreachable database, still answers the sitemap: the product query is caught and contributes no rows instead of failing the whole document.
- Catalog category landing pages (`/categories/<slug>`, from `catalogCategoryPath`) were missing for the same reason, so `listCatalogCategorySitemapEntries` lists every live category with the API's own filter and path, skipping a category without a slug. These rows carry no `lastmod`: legacy `item_categories.updatedAt` values are BSON timestamps rather than dates, and inventing a date from them would be wrong.
- Verified over HTTP on `:4006` (customer `sentuan`, real legacy data): `/sitemap.xml` `200` with **298** `<url>` entries — 228 `/products/…` (+ `<lastmod>`), 66 `/categories/…` and the rest content/tag/category-of-content URLs. The collection holds 229 published items, so the product difference is one slug (`man-khung-ngang`) published twice, collapsed by the de-duplication; 66 of 67 category rows are listed and the 67th is the `isDeleted` one (`chan-ga-goi-dem-ga-tran`, absent from the sitemap and `404` from the API). Three non-published products (`dem-hanvico-nano-ueros*`) appear zero times, `/products/man-khung-ngang` and a listed `/categories/…` page both answer `200`, and `robots.txt` still advertises the same sitemap.

- Before this, a nested row could only move up by being recreated as a top-level item and having the old one deleted, which lost its place in the header. The child row now has a *Move to top level* button (outdent icon, next to edit) that lifts it out of `children` and inserts it directly under its former parent; nothing else changes order.
- The total number of rows does not change, so `MENU_MAX_CHILDREN` and `MENU_MAX_ITEMS` are unaffected and the save still goes through the revision guard.

### P2.d — demote a menu row (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `cms-base/app/pages/menus.vue` (`demoteItem`), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.menus.demoteItem`).
- The target of a demotion is unambiguous — the row above — so it needs no dialog: `demoteItem` appends the top-level row as the last child of its previous sibling and removes it from the root list. Label and target are carried over, and the appended child is a leaf, so a demoted row cannot smuggle a grandchild in.
- Ordering: because it only appends, the row keeps its reading position instead of jumping to the front of the sibling list. The total number of rows is unchanged, so only the per-parent cap matters: the button is disabled at `index 0` and when the parent already holds `MENU_MAX_CHILDREN` children, and `demoteItem` repeats that guard with the localized message in case it is called another way.
- Live (`:4005/menus?menu=…`, probe menu of two rows): row 2's indent button was enabled and row 1's disabled, one click rendered `1.1 Probe two` under `1 Probe one` with `Unsaved` shown, and saving reached `Revision 2`. `GET /api/v1/admin/menus/<id>` read back `[(Probe one, [Probe two])]`, and `GET /api/public/site?locale=vi` on `:4006` returned the nested child with its `href`. Probe menu, audit rows and idempotency keys removed; typecheck clean.
- Still open: no touch drag, and the dropdown does not repeat the parent link inside itself.

### P2.d — two-level menus with drag-and-drop (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `flow-contracts/src/menu-admin.ts` (`AdminMenuChildItemSchema`, `AdminMenuItemSchema.children`, `countMenuItems`), `tm-cms/server/models/menu.ts` (recursive normalize), `tm-cms/server/services/menuService.ts` (`flattenItems`, total `itemCount`), `tm-cms/server/services/menuReferences.ts` (`menuLinksTo`, JS filter), `flow-contracts/src/site-public.ts` (`PublicMenuLeafSchema`), `tm-web/server/utils/publicSite.ts`, `web-base/app/composables/usePublicSite.ts`, `web-base/app/layouts/default.vue`, `cms-base/app/pages/menus.vue`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json`.
- Shape: a top-level item may own up to 10 children, one level only — the dropdown a header can actually show. Children are leaves: the model rejects a grandchild with `MENU_DEPTH_LIMIT` instead of silently dropping it, and the 30-item cap counts children so `itemCount` in the list view is the real size of the menu.
- Editor: rows render nested (child position shown as `2.1`), an *Add a child item* action sits on each row, and rows are draggable. Drag and the arrow buttons share one `reorder(list, from, to)` routine, so both ways of moving a row cannot drift apart; dragging is state-based (not `dataTransfer`), which also makes it testable.
- Delete guard: `findMenuReferences` now walks children too, so a page only a child links to still refuses to be deleted. Menus per site are few, so the filter runs in JS (`menuLinksTo`) rather than a nested `$elemMatch` that Mongo applies per array.
- Storefront: `usePublicSite` returns `children` per link, the footer flattens them (it is a single column), and the header renders a dropdown on hover **and** on keyboard focus (`group-focus-within`). A child's category key is resolved to its locale slug in `tm-web/server/utils/publicSite.ts`, so a dropdown link is not the only unresolved menu URL.
- Tests: `pnpm test:menus` 16 checks (children stored/counted/returned, depth 422, children 422, total 422, child-link validation naming `1.1`, child links found by the reverse lookup, `details.missing[].path` on the flat case); `pnpm test:web-site` 10 and `pnpm test:web-site-db` 6 still green; `pnpm test:content` 27, `pnpm test:categories` 12.
- Live HTTP (`:4005`): a menu with `Trang sức → [Nhẫn, Dây chuyền]` plus an `About` page link created `201` with `itemCount 4`; a grandchild PATCH returned `422 MENU_DEPTH_LIMIT` *Item 1.1: menus support one level of children*; the list endpoint reported `main · 4`.
- Browser (`:4005/menus`): rows rendered `1 / 1.1 / 1.2 / 2`; dragging `1.2` above `1.1` reordered the children, dragging the last top-level row to the top reordered the roots, **Save** moved the menu to revision 2 and the API read back `Giới thiệu, Trang sức → [Dây chuyền, Nhẫn]`. *Add a child item* on the first row showed `Inside: About`, and saving appended it (revision 3, 5 items).
- Storefront (`:4006`): `/api/public/site` returned the nested items with resolved `href`s after the 60s cache expired, and the header rendered `About → [Bảo hành]` and `Jewellery → [Necklaces, Rings]`; focusing the trigger made the dropdown visible (192×82) with both links, and the page console stayed clean.
- Probes were removed afterwards (`site_menus 0 / audit 0`, real content still the 2 pages).

### P2.d — the editor warns before the delete (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/services/{content,category}Service.ts` (`listContentMenuReferences`, `listCategoryMenuReferences`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/content/[id]/menu-references.get.ts`, `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/categories/[id]/menu-references.get.ts`, `flow-contracts/src/menu-admin.ts` (`MenuReferenceSchema`, `MenuReferencesSchema`), `cms-base/app/composables/{useContentAdmin,useCategoryAdmin}.ts`, `cms-base/app/pages/{content,categories}.vue`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json`.
- Rule: the same reverse lookup the delete guard uses is exposed as a read. Both routes resolve the record first, so another site's id is a `404` instead of an empty list, and both are gated by `content.read`. The content detail dialog shows a *Menus linking to this page* block and the category edit dialog a *Menus linking to this category* block, each listing `name` plus the `key` in mono and a one-line hint that the record cannot be deleted until the menu is updated. Each name links to `/menus?menu=<id>`, and the menu editor opens that menu instead of the first one, so fixing the reference is one click from the warning. A record with no references renders nothing at all, so the warning never becomes noise.
- Failure behaviour: the block is a warning, not a gate, so a failed read is swallowed and the dialog renders without it — the delete path still refuses and explains.
- Tests: `pnpm test:content` (check extended, still 27) asserts the reference list matches the delete refusal, that `details.menus` and the list agree, that another site's id is `404`, and that the list is empty once the menu is removed; `pnpm test:categories` (12) does the same for a category.

- Browser (`:4005`): the page detail dialog rendered `Menus linking to this page / Probe ref menu probe-ref-menu / Update the menu before deleting …`, and the category edit dialog the same block for the category. With two menus in the site (`probe-aaa-first`, `probe-zzz-linked`) the category warning linked to `/menus?menu=<zzz id>` and that page opened `ZZZ linked menu` rather than the first entry. Probes were removed afterwards (`site_menus 0 / categories 0 / audit 0 / content_revisions 0`, real content still 2).

### P2.d — a menu reference blocks the delete (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/services/menuReferences.ts` (`findMenuReferences`), `tm-cms/server/services/contentService.ts` (`ContentWriteMeta.menus`, guard in `runContentEvent`), `tm-cms/server/services/categoryService.ts` (guard in `deleteCategory`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/categories/[id].delete.ts`, `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/content/[id]/events.post.ts`, `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/content/bulk.post.ts`, `cms-base/app/pages/{content,categories}.vue`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json`.
- Rule: one reverse lookup, `items: { $elemMatch: { 'link.type': type, 'link.target': target } }`, answers which menus of the site point at a record. Deleting a category a menu links to returns `409 CATEGORY_IN_MENU`, and deleting a page a menu links to returns `409 CONTENT_IN_MENU`; both carry `details.menus` (`{ id, key, name }`) and neither writes anything. The content guard only applies to `delete` on a record that is not already deleted, so restore or re-delete is unaffected, and a bulk delete reports the refusal per row instead of failing the batch.
- Why refuse instead of auto-editing menus: silently dropping the item would change navigation the editor never opened; naming the menu instead keeps the fix one click away.
- Tests: `pnpm test:content` is 28 checks (a menu-linked page cannot be deleted, `details.menus` names it, the revision does not move, then the delete succeeds once the menu is gone) and `pnpm test:categories` is 12 live checks (the same shape for `deleteCategory`). `pnpm test:menus` 14 and `pnpm test:bulk` 9 still pass.
- Live HTTP (CMS `:4005`, probe category + page + a menu linking both): `DELETE /api/v1/admin/categories/:id` returned `409 CATEGORY_IN_MENU` with `details.menus = [{ key: "probe-guard-menu", name: "Probe guard menu" }]`, and `POST /content/:id/events` with `{ event: "delete" }` returned `409 CONTENT_IN_MENU` with the same menu; both records kept their revision (page still `draft rev 1`, category still listed).
- Browser (`:4005`): the category delete dialog rendered `The menu Probe guard menu links to this category — update it before deleting.`, and the bulk delete of the page row reported `Processed 0/1 records` with `A menu links to this page — update it before deleting.`; the two pages that predate the probes kept their status and revision. Probes were removed afterwards (`site_menus 0 / categories 0 / audit 0 / content_revisions 0`, real content still 2).
- i18n note: the message uses `{menus}`, not `{{menus}}` — intlify rejects the double-brace form as a nested placeholder, and that failure blanks the whole locale bundle, so every label in that locale falls back to its key.

### P2.d — menu link reference validation (2026-11-09)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/services/menuService.ts` (`MenuWriteMeta.refs`, `assertLinksExist`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/menus/index.post.ts`, `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/menus/[id].patch.ts`, `cms-base/app/pages/menus.vue` (`MENU_TARGET_NOT_FOUND` message), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.menus.errors.targetNotFound`), `scripts/build/test-menu-service.ts`.
- Rule: a menu item's `content` target must match a page in this site whose `deletedAt` is null, and a `category` target a category in this site; `url` targets remain literal text and only their shape is checked. Both `POST` and `PATCH` resolve the referenced keys in one batched query per collection before touching the document, so a refused save writes nothing and leaves the revision where the editor loaded it. `details.missing` lists every offending item as `{ index, type, target }`, and the editor maps the code to `admin.menus.errors.targetNotFound` inline and as a toast while keeping the draft so the broken item can be fixed.
- Scope: the lookup is opt-in through `MenuWriteMeta.refs`, so a caller that has no content/category collections (the existing service tests, a future importer) keeps the old behaviour; the two admin routes always pass them.
- Tests: `pnpm test:menus` is now 13 live checks — the new one saves a menu whose `content`, `category` and `url` targets all resolve, then refuses an unknown page key (`details.missing` = `[{ index: 3, type: 'content', target: 'khong-ton-tai' }]`), a soft-deleted page and another site's category key, and asserts the revision is still the one before the refused saves.
- Live HTTP (CMS `:4005`, `sen-tuan`, probe category + draft page): `PATCH` with a `category` target `khong-co-that` returned `422 MENU_TARGET_NOT_FOUND`, message `Item 1: no category with the key "khong-co-that" exists in this site`, `details.missing` intact; the same menu saved `200` once the targets were the probe category and page.
- Browser (`:4005/menus`): editing item 1 to `khong-co-that` and saving showed the inline error and toast `Liên kết đang trỏ tới một trang hoặc danh mục không tồn tại trong site này. Chọn lại mục có thật rồi lưu.`, the header still read `Revision 2` and the draft kept the bad target. Probe menu, category, page and their audit rows were removed afterwards (`site_menus 0 / categories 0 / audit 0`; the two pages that predate the probes are untouched).
- Editor picker: `GET /api/v1/admin/menus/link-options` returns the site's live pages and categories as `{ key, label }`, and the item dialog's target field is a select of those choices for `content`/`category` (a `url` item keeps the free-text input). Changing the link type clears the target, because `/` is a URL and `trang-suc` is a key. A saved target that no longer exists stays visible and is marked `(no longer exists)` so it can be replaced instead of reading as an empty field. Tests: the 14th check in `pnpm test:menus` (a soft-deleted page and another site's category are not offered, the `vi` name is the label, a record without a title falls back to its key). Live HTTP on `:4005` returned three pages (`pages/dich-vu`, `pages/gioi-thieu` and the probe page, soft-deleted probe page absent) and the two probe categories; in the browser the category list showed `Probe danh mục · probe-pick-cat` / `Probe đã xoá · probe-pick-gone`, the saved menu went to `Revision 2`, and after deleting the referenced category the reopened item showed `probe-pick-gone (no longer exists)`. Probes were removed afterwards (`site_menus 0 / categories 0 / audit 0`).

### SYS-02 — P2.d public site projection (2026-11-08)

- Target files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/site-public.ts` (`PublicSiteSchema`, `PublicMenuItemSchema`, `buildPublicSite`, `toPublicMenu`, `publicMenuHref`, `publicMenuLabel`, `findPublicMenu`, `PUBLIC_MENU_HEADER`/`PUBLIC_MENU_FOOTER`), subpath export `@tm/flow-contracts/site-public`, `tm-web/server/models/sitePublic.ts` (read-only `settings`/`site_menus` schemas, projections, `buildCategorySlugMap`), `tm-web/server/utils/publicSite.ts` (`getSiteLocale`, `readPublicSite`), `tm-web/server/api/public/site.get.ts`, `web-base/app/layouts/default.vue`, `scripts/build/test-web-site.ts`, `scripts/build/test-web-site-db.ts`.
- Contract: the projection carries company identity, SEO defaults, locales and menus only. Revisions, `updatedBy`, `modules` and the stored link targets never leave the database, and `PublicSiteSchema.parse` at the endpoint is the last gate, so a leaked admin field fails the response instead of reaching the browser.
- Read path: one settings document, all menus sorted by key, then a single category query for the keys the menus reference. The endpoint is cached per locale (`public-site:<locale>`, 60s / 300s stale) and the CMS purges that cache after a settings or menu save — a customer database, never Hub, never a session.
- Link resolution: a `content` key becomes the catch-all path (`pages/dich-vu` → `/dich-vu`), a `category` key becomes `/danh-muc/<slug>` for the requested locale with the storefront default locale and then the key as fallback, and a `url` target is only kept when it is a rooted path or absolute `http(s)` link. An item with no label or an unresolvable target is dropped rather than rendered as a dead link.
- Storefront: `usePublicSite()` owns the read (one `useAsyncData` key, re-fetched on locale change) and exposes brand, contact details, SEO defaults and both link lists with the static navigation and Hub company name as the fallback. The layout's brand, contact block, header and mobile nav and the footer *Explore* list consume it, the home page's title, description and Organization JSON-LD use the settings display name and default description, and the product JSON-LD brand uses it too, so no page renders Hub's company name when the site has saved its own.
- Tests: `pnpm test:web-site` — 10 pure checks (path/category/url resolution, label fallback, dropping unusable items, the allowlist, defaults for an unsaved site, a partly written document, per-menu category overrides). `pnpm test:web-site-db` — 6 live checks in a disposable database: the settings projection drops `modules`/`revision`/`updatedBy`, the menu projection drops `siteId`/`revision` while keeping item order, the header resolves per locale, a category with only a `vi` slug falls back, the document satisfies `PublicSiteSchema` with no actor email or revision string, and an empty database reads defaults.
- Live HTTP (Web `:4006`, probe settings + `main`/`footer` menus in `sen-tuan`): `GET /api/public/site?locale=vi` returned the probe company, SEO, locales and both menus with `/gioi-thieu`, `/danh-muc/trang-suc` and `/lien-he`, plus `cache-control: s-maxage=60, stale-while-revalidate=300`; `?locale=en` served the English labels.
- Browser (`:4006`): `document.title` was `Sen Tuấn`, the meta description and the footer contact block came from the settings document, and the header rendered `Sen Tuấn` (the settings display name, not the Hub name) with `Giới thiệu` / `Danh mục` linking to `/gioi-thieu` and `/danh-muc/trang-suc`, and the footer *Explore* list rendered the `footer` menu item instead of the three static links; after switching to `en` the snapshot showed `About us` / `Category` / `Contact` on the same hrefs. The first snapshot of the tab was stale HTML from the PWA service worker and had to be reloaded before it reflected the new data. Probe settings and menus were removed afterwards (`settings 0 / site_menus 0`, legacy `menus` still 9).
- The title template is now applied globally (see the slice below), so a page keeps its own title and the settings document decides how it reads.
- Remaining gaps: the logo id is projected but not resolved to a URL yet (Q19), and a menu has no per-locale variants (a label carries both languages, the item order is shared). Reordering now works with a finger as well as a mouse, recorded in [`tasks/todo.md`](../../tasks/todo.md). Menu references are checked in both directions now: saving a target that does not resolve is refused, and deleting a record a menu links to is refused until the menu is fixed (see the two slices below).

### SYS-02 — P2.d site menus record (2026-11-08)

- Target files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/menu-admin.ts` (`AdminMenuSchema`, `AdminMenuSummarySchema`, `AdminMenuCreateSchema`, `AdminMenuPatchSchema`, `MENU_LINK_TYPES`, `MENU_MAX_ITEMS`), `tm-cms/server/models/menu.ts`, migration `menu-indexes` v7, `tm-cms/server/utils/modelRegistry.ts` (`Menu`), `tm-cms/server/services/menuService.ts`, `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/menus/{index.get,index.post,[id].get,[id].patch,[id].delete}.ts`, `cms-base/app/composables/useMenuAdmin.ts`, `cms-base/app/pages/menus.vue`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json`, `scripts/build/test-menu-service.ts`.
- Model: a menu is an immutable key (`main`, `footer`) plus an ordered `items` list of at most 30 entries. An item stores a per-locale label and exactly one link — a content key, a category key, or a literal path/URL — so renaming a record never rewrites a menu and switching locale only swaps the label. A missing translation stays empty instead of borrowing the other language; the editor falls back for display only.
- Collection: `site_menus`, deliberately not `menus`. Every migrated customer database already holds the legacy shop menus (9 documents in `sen-tuan`, none carrying a `siteId`), and they all collide on the new unique `(siteId, key)` index — the first attempt failed with `Index build failed ... dup key` until the collection was renamed. Those documents stay untouched until the import slice (Q2/Q5).
- Capabilities: `configs.read` to list and read, `configs.write` to create/save/delete — the same pair as the settings document, because a menu is site configuration. **Hub roles need `configs.write`** before a real admin can edit menus.
- Conflict and validation rules: the key is folded to a slug once and refused on change (`MENU_KEY_IMMUTABLE`); a duplicate key is `409`; a stale `revision` is `409`; an unchanged save is `422`; a bad target, label, link type or an over-long list is `422` with a code the editor shows verbatim. Every accepted mutation writes one `audit_logs` entry (`resourceType: menu`, resource key = the menu key) with the session actor, plus `Idempotency-Key` support on all three writes.
- CMS UI: `/menus` (nav entry gated on `configs.read`) lists menus on the left and edits the selected one on the right: name field, ordered item list with up/down/edit/remove, an item dialog (vi/en labels, link type, target with a per-type hint), unsaved-change marker, Save against the loaded revision, and a delete confirmation. `menu` was added to the audit filter options.
- Tests: `pnpm test:menus` — 12 live checks in a disposable database: empty list, key folding + one audit entry, duplicate key, unusable key/empty name, item order preserved, site-scoped detail read, stale revision, no-op save, label/target/link-type validation, the 30-item cap, immutable key, same key free on another site, and delete with revision + audit.
- Live HTTP (CMS `:4005`, mock auth, `sen-tuan`): create `Menu chinh` -> `201` with `key: menu-chinh`; duplicate -> `409`; `PATCH` with three items -> `200 revision 2` keeping order (`gioi-thieu`, `trang-suc`, `/lien-he`); stale -> `409`; unchanged -> `422`; `target: lien-he` without a leading slash -> `422`; changing the key -> `422`; cross-origin `PATCH` -> `403`; malformed id -> `422`; anonymous -> `401`; a retried create with the same `Idempotency-Key` -> `201` + `Idempotent-Replay: true` with one stored document.
- Browser (`:4005/menus`): the list rendered both menus with key and item count; selecting one loaded its items with type badges; adding `Tin tuc / News -> /tin-tuc` through the item dialog, moving it up and saving produced `Revision 3` then `4` on both the page and `GET`, with the new order stored; creating `phu` through the dialog and deleting it through the confirmation left the list at two menus. Probe menus, audit entries and keys were removed afterwards (`site_menus 0 / audit 0 / keys 0`), and the legacy `menus` collection still holds its 9 documents.

### CNT-01 — P3.e category tree record (2026-11-07)

- Target files: `tm-cms/server/models/category.ts` (`categories`, unique `(siteId, key)` / `(siteId, locale, slug)`, key normalization), `tm-cms/server/services/categoryService.ts` (`listCategories`, `buildCategoryTree`, `createCategory`, `updateCategory`, `deleteCategory`, `getCategory`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/categories/{index.get,index.post,[id].patch,[id].delete}.ts`, migration `category-indexes` v4, `tm-cms/server/utils/modelRegistry.ts` (`Category`), and the audit model's new `category` resource type.
- Contracts: `@tm/flow-contracts/category-admin` — `AdminCategorySchema`, `AdminCategoryNodeSchema`, `AdminCategoryDraftSchema`, `ADMIN_CATEGORY_MAX_DEPTH`.
- Behaviour: the key is the identity and cannot change, `parent` stores a key, `order` is 0–9999, depth is capped at 5 levels, and a category can be deleted only when it has no children and no content references it. `updatedAt`-style optimistic locking uses the monotonic `revision`; every mutation writes one `audit_logs` entry with `resourceType: category`. Categories deliberately have no revision snapshots — the audit trail is the history.
- CMS UI: `/categories` renders the tree with indentation, per-row add-child/edit/delete, a create/edit modal with a vi/en name+slug block, a parent picker that excludes the node and its descendants, and an inline delete guard message. `content.vue` replaced the comma-separated text input with a `USelectMenu` (multiple, searchable) fed by the same tree; legacy keys that are not in the tree stay visible on the record.
- Tests: `pnpm test:categories` — 11 live service checks (normalized key and folded slugs, ordered tree with depth, duplicate key/slug, missing parent, self/descendant cycle, depth cap, optimistic-lock update, delete guarded by children then by content usage, site scoping, one audit entry per mutation).
- Live evidence (CMS `:4005`): created `trang-suc` (vi `Trang sức`, en `Jewelry`) and a child `nhan` under it, which rendered indented at `padding-left: 1.25rem`; editing the child pushed it to `Order: 3 · Revision 2`; deleting the parent returned `409` with `This category still has children` shown inline in the dialog; deleting the child and then the parent returned the empty state. In the content editor the picker listed `Jewelry` and saved `categories: ["trang-suc"]` on a probe record (`revision 2`); probe content, its revisions/audit rows and the test categories were removed afterwards.
