- Direction: a component that both this repo and `tm-hub` need belongs in `D:\Applications\tm-platform\packages` (`@tuanmjnh/*`), not in `tm-shared`. This slice tried the first adoption: `@tuanmjnh/tm-ui`'s `StatusBadge` for the CMS module badges (`ModuleStatusBadges.vue`, one call site, replacing two hand-written `UBadge`s).
- What a `link:` dependency needs on top of what `tm-platform/docs/upgrade.md` documents (dependency, Vite `server.fs.allow`, Tailwind `@source`): the package's `dist/*.js` is precompiled, so its `resolveComponent('UBadge')` / `('UIcon')` calls require those components on the app instance (a client plugin registering them from `#components`), and Vite must not bundle a second Vue.
- Why it is blocked: with `link:` the package resolves `vue` from `tm-platform` (3.5.45) next to this repo's 3.5.44, and Vite loads both. `resolveComponent` then runs with no `currentInstance`, returns the tag name string, and Vue renders `<ubadge label="…" color="success">` as an unknown element — empty pills, no console error, and `pnpm build:cms` still green. Registering the components changed nothing: the registry was correct, the wrong Vue instance was asking.
- The obvious repair does not work here: `vite.resolve.dedupe: ['vue']` only helps when `vue` resolves from the app root, and adding `vue` to `tm-cms/package.json` for that broke `vue-tsc` with 122 errors, including `Cannot find module 'vue'` in `cms-base/app/composables/useMobileBar.ts` — the layer has no `vue` of its own to resolve.
- Consequence (superseded by WEB-15 above, which pins `vue` with a Vite alias and made the link work): no `link:` for Vue-dependent `@tuanmjnh/*` packages in this repo until `@tuanmjnh/tm-ui` is published (its publish is currently blocked on npm OTP). Installed from the registry, `vue` comes from the app as a peer and there is one instance; `tm-platform` should also consider shipping the SFCs instead of a precompiled `dist`, so the app's auto-imports apply. Pure-TypeScript packages (`tm-utils`, `tm-validators`, `tm-types`) have none of these problems and can be adopted first.
- Reverted in full: no dependency, config, CSS or component change landed; typecheck, lint and `build:cms` were green before and after.

### WEB-12.d — colour contrast in both themes (landed 2026-11-13)

- What was wrong (light theme only, `/products`: 39 text nodes under WCAG AA): the palette's 500 step was used for `--ui-primary`, which is ~2:1 as text on white, and Nuxt UI builds `text-muted`/`text-dimmed` as translucent mixes of the body colour, so they fade to 2.5:1 and 1.2:1 instead of holding a documented contrast.
- Fix, all in `tm-shared/packages/design-system/css/tokens.css`: light uses `primary-800` (~6.6:1 on white, 5.6:1 on `bg-primary/10`) with muted/dimmed at `neutral-600` (~7:1); `.dark` re-states the three at its lighter steps, which is load-bearing — Nuxt UI's light values sit on a zero-specificity selector and were leaking into dark mode; the two utilities now resolve to solid palette steps.
- Evidence: axe-core 4.10.2 `color-contrast` on the live storefront, both modes, 0 violations on `/` (95 nodes), `/products` (105), `/bai-viet` (30), the 404 page (5), and no violations on product detail or the cart. Two measurement traps are recorded in `08`: the decorative gradient makes axe skip nodes, and paused CSS transitions in a background tab report the previous theme's surface (it produced 24–56 false failures until transitions were disabled).
- The CMS was measured the same way and needed three more token fixes: dark `text-dimmed` was 3.25:1 (now `neutral-400`), and `--ui-error`/`warning`/`success`/`info` start at 1.9–3.6:1 as text, with the 700 steps still under AA on their own 10% tinted badges (4.06 and 4.38), so light takes 800. `/login`, `/`, `/catalog`, `/content`, `/configs`, `/audit` and `/settings` now report 0 violations in both themes.
- Not covered: only the contrast rule ran; focus rings, screen-reader output and `text-primary` over photos are not measured.

### WEB-12.c — the 410 branch with a real redirect row (landed 2026-11-13)

- Why: the error-state pass left 410 unexercised because the local data had no redirect mapping, and inventing one to satisfy a check is not allowed. The row was therefore written through the CMS, the way an operator would write it, and removed with the same tooling in the same run.
- Flow (CMS `:4005` mock session with `TM_WEB_REVALIDATE_URL`/`TM_REVALIDATE_SECRET` → storefront `:4018`, `sen-tuan`): baseline `/probe-gone` → `404`; `POST /api/v1/admin/redirects` `{from:'/probe-gone',to:'',statusCode:410}` → `201` + `x-storefront-revalidate: revalidated`; purge with `redirect.create` → `removedCount: 0`; `GET /probe-gone` with `Accept: text/html` → **`410`**, the site's own error page (`Trang này đã được gỡ`, `noindex, follow`, `no-store`). A second row `{from:'/probe-move',to:'/',statusCode:301}` → `201`, and `/probe-move` → `301` with `Location: /`, so a gone row and a moving row do not interfere.
- Cleanup: `DELETE /api/v1/admin/redirects/<id>?revision=1` → `200` (`422` without the revision); `content_redirects` 2 → 0, `content_revisions` 0, redirect `audit_logs` 4 → 0. The lookup caches a path for 30 s, so `/probe-gone` kept answering `410` briefly and then `404`; `/probe-move` `404` as well — the rows are really gone.
- Trap worth keeping: a request without an HTML `Accept` (curl's `*/*`) gets Nitro's dev JSON error envelope instead of the page. That is not specific to 410 — `/khong-ton-tai` behaves the same and renders `Không tìm thấy trang` only with `Accept: text/html` — so error-state checks must send a browser-like `Accept`.
- Limits: the back-home action is a client-side `clearError({ redirect: '/' })` button, read from the DOM but not clicked; 410 for a path that still has content behind it is not covered; the pending affordance and colour contrast stay open on WEB-12. Steps and numbers in `08`.

### WEB-09 — scoped storefront cache purge (landed 2026-11-13)

- Target files: `tm-web/server/utils/publicCache.ts` (key format, `scopesForReason`, `purgePublicCache`), `tm-web/server/utils/catalogCache.ts` (`catalogCacheKey`, new `siteCacheKey`), `tm-web/server/api/public/{site,site-config}.get.ts`, `tm-web/server/api/_internal/purge.post.ts`, `scripts/build/test-purge-scope.ts`, `package.json`, `scripts/build/run-gates.ts`.
- Problem: a CMS save sent `POST /api/_internal/purge` with a reason, but the endpoint deleted every `nitro:handlers:` entry — all tenants' catalog and site projections — and cleared the process-wide redirect map. Separately, `/api/public/site`'s cache key was `public-site:<locale>` with no tenant, so two customers sharing a cache could read each other's site projection.
- Key format now: `public__<database>__<scope>__<rest>`, slugged to `[A-Za-z0-9_]` because Nitro stores handler keys with `String(key).replace(/\W/g, "")` (colons, dashes and slashes vanish; underscores survive). Scopes are `site` (`/api/public/site`, `/api/public/site-config`) and `catalog` (products/categories and their detail pages).
- Scope from the reason: `catalog.item.*` → `catalog`; `catalog.category.*` → `site` + `catalog` (menus and URLs carry category names); `menu.*`, `settings.*`, `redirect.*` → `site`; anything unknown → both, so a new caller can under-purge but never under-invalidate silently. `{ scope: 'all' }` stays as the operator escape hatch and still stops at handler entries.
- Redirect map: cleared per database (`clearRedirectCache(database)`) instead of process-wide, and only as part of a `site`/`all` purge.
- Tests: `pnpm test:purge-scope` — 8 checks against a map-backed storage: key format survives Nitro's sanitiser, the reason→scope table, catalog purge leaving the same tenant's site key and another tenant untouched, site-only purge, category rename dropping both scopes of one tenant, unknown reason, `all`, empty cache, and an unknown tenant dropping nothing.
- CMS → storefront (mock-auth CMS `:4005` with `TM_WEB_REVALIDATE_URL`/`TM_REVALIDATE_SECRET` against storefront `:4018`): a real `POST /api/v1/admin/catalog/items` returned `201` with `x-storefront-revalidate: revalidated`, a follow-up purge with the same reason found nothing left (`removedCount: 0`), and `settings.update` then removed exactly the two site keys — so the save cleared its own scope and only its own scope. The probe item and its two audit rows were deleted afterwards (`items 0 / audits 0`). The reverse direction was checked the same way: a real `PATCH /api/v1/admin/settings` (first save, `revision 0 → 1`) returned `revalidated`, its own reason then found `removedCount: 0`, and a `catalog.item.update` purge still found the products key (`removedCount: 1`) — so a settings save leaves the catalog cache alone. The settings document and its audit row were removed afterwards (`settings 0 / audits 0`), restoring `revision: 0` with an empty company name.
- Live (`:4018` sen-tuan, `:4019` bag-post, both stopped afterwards): after warming four reads, `catalog.item.update` removed exactly the 2 sen-tuan catalog keys and reported 2 remaining; `settings.update` then removed exactly the 2 sen-tuan site keys; a repeat returned 0; `catalog.category.update` removed 2 (one per scope) with the bag-post key still present; `scope: 'all'` then removed that bag-post key, proving it had survived both scoped purges in the same store. No secret / wrong secret → `401`, unknown `scope` → `422`, unknown reason → `["site","catalog"]`.

### WEB-09 — catalog edits clear the storefront cache (landed 2026-11-11)

- Why: the storefront serves `/api/public/products`, `/api/public/products/:slug`, `/api/public/categories` and `/api/public/categories/:slug` through `cachedEventHandler` (60s, 300s stale) over the CMS-owned `catalog_items`/`catalog_categories` collections. A publish, price fix or category rename therefore stayed invisible for up to a minute; settings and menus already purged, the catalog never did.
- Writes now purging: `POST /items`, `PATCH /items/:id`, `POST /items/:id/events` (publish/archive/restore/delete), `POST /items/bulk`, `POST /items/bulk-by-filter`, `POST /categories`, `PATCH /categories/:id`, `DELETE /categories/:id`.
- Semantics reused unchanged from the settings slice: best-effort (a down storefront never fails the save, the outcome is reported in `x-storefront-revalidate` and logged), 5s timeout, skipped entirely without `TM_WEB_REVALIDATE_URL` + `TM_REVALIDATE_SECRET`, not re-run on an idempotent replay, and a bulk patch whose rows all failed does not purge. The reason names the write (`catalog.item.publish`, `catalog.item.bulk`, `catalog.category.delete`, …).
- Evidence (tests): `pnpm test:revalidate` → 10 checks, the two new ones reading the eight route sources so a catalog write that stops importing or calling the purge fails the suite, and so does one that purges on a replay.
- Evidence (HTTP, `:4015` CMS → `:4016` Web with the shared secret, `sen-tuan`, disposable probe `probe-e2e-item`): the product URL was warmed to `404` by the handler cache, `POST /api/v1/admin/catalog/items` answered `201` with `x-storefront-revalidate: revalidated`, the draft still read `404`, `POST …/events {event: publish}` answered `200` with `revalidated`, and the next `GET /api/public/products/probe-e2e-item` returned `200` with `Probe E2E item`, `price: 250000` and `cache-control: public, max-age=60, s-maxage=300`. `DELETE` (event) purged again and the same URL answered `404` at once.
- Evidence (HTTP, same CMS against a stub storefront): the eight write paths sent `/api/_internal/purge` with the matching reasons; a repeated `PATCH` with the same `Idempotency-Key` returned `idempotent-replay: true` and sent nothing; with the stub killed the save stayed `200` with `x-storefront-revalidate: request-failed` and the row still saved at revision 6.
- Probe cleanup: the item, the category and their audit rows were deleted afterwards (`catalog_items 0 / catalog_categories 0 / audit 0` for both probe keys); no other collection touched.
- Limits: the purge is still broad (every Nitro handler key, redirect cache included) rather than per-product or tag-scoped, unsigned and not replay-protected, and a CDN in front of the storefront keeps serving its own copy for `s-maxage` (Q12). Content writes still need no purge: those handlers are not cached.

### CNT-02 — attribute filter on the content list (landed 2026-11-11)

- Service: `listContent` gained `attributeKey` / `attributeValue`. A key alone means "has this attribute at all"; with a value it means "equals", compared as text so the numeric `5` and the boolean `true` answer a filter typed as `5` / `true`. Attribute names may contain dots, so the match uses `$getField` on `attributes` rather than a dotted path (which would read `meta.series` as a nested object).
- API/contract: `ContentListQuerySchema` validates the key against the model's own `ATTRIBUTE_KEY_PATTERN` (both now exported, so a filter cannot accept a name the editor could not save), caps the value at the model's 500 characters, and rejects a value without a key as `422 CONTENT_QUERY_INVALID`. `ContentListQuery` in `useContentAdmin` mirrors the pair.
- CMS UI: two text inputs in the content toolbar ("Lọc theo tên thuộc tính", "Giá trị thuộc tính"), debounced 400 ms like the search and keyword boxes. The value input stays disabled until a key is typed, and a value is never sent on its own.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB): `pnpm test:content` → 25 model + 32 live checks; the new one covers has-attribute, exact value, numeric `5`, boolean `true`, a dotted key (`meta.series`), an unknown name, a wrong value, and another site's identical attribute.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4005`, `sen-tuan`, disposable probe `pages/probe-attr-gold` with `{ material: 'gold', weight: 5, featured: true }`): `?attributeKey=material&attributeValue=gold` and `?attributeKey=weight&attributeValue=5` each returned only the probe, `?attributeKey=weight` returned it by name alone, `?attributeKey=material&attributeValue=bronze` returned none, `?attributeValue=gold` was `422` and `?attributeKey=1bad` was `422`.
- Evidence (browser `:4005/content`): typing `material` left only the probe row while the site-wide counters stayed at 4; adding `silver` hid it, replacing it with `gold` brought it back. The probe record and its audit row were deleted afterwards (`content` 3, `audit_logs` 1).
- Limits: only one attribute at a time, exact value matching (no ranges, no "contains", no `has any of`), and `$expr` means the filter cannot use an index — fine at this record count, worth revisiting if content grows past a few thousand rows. Attributes are still not part of the public projection.

### P10.a — legacy content import (landed 2026-11-11)

- Command: `pnpm migrate:content --site <id> [--tenant default] [--apply]` (`scripts/ops/migrate-content.ts`), the content counterpart of `migrate:catalog`. It reads the legacy `contents` / `content_categories` raw, never through a model, plans everything in memory, and only touches the database with `--apply`; a dry run now writes nothing at all (no collections, indexes or `tm_migrations` rows).
- Mapping: legacy `type: page` → `page`, everything else → `post`; the key is `pages/<vi slug>` or `posts/<vi slug>` (the shape the CMS already used for hand-made records), with the legacy id as the fallback when a key collides. Each locale carries its own slug, title, excerpt, body, keywords and SEO title/description/image; the body is HTML, so `format` is `html` and the storefront's sanitizer, not a guess, handles it. Legacy formats (`gallery`, `video`, `audio`, `quote`, `link`) are body templates, not render modes.
- Carried where a column exists: `categories` (legacy ids resolved to the new category keys), `tags` (legacy `{ title, slug }` → display names), `seo.*`, `publishedAt`, `deletedAt`, `createdAt`/`updatedAt`.
- Carried where no column exists: per-record `legacy.format`, `legacy.template`, `legacy.thumbnail`, `legacy.parent`, `legacy.sort`, `legacy.showOnHome`, `legacy.priority`, `legacy.views` attributes. They are filterable through CNT-02 (`?attributeKey=legacy.format&attributeValue=gallery`) instead of being lost, and the `legacy.` prefix keeps them visibly distinct from authored metadata.
- Deliberately not carried, and counted by the dry run: legacy `gallery` image lists (4 records) and `media` embeds with external URLs or raw `<iframe>` markup (6 records) — v2 `media` holds ids of rows in the `media` collection, so writing URLs there would store data the media service cannot resolve. Importing media is its own slice.
- Idempotency: `findOneAndUpdate` on `(siteId, key)` with `$setOnInsert` for `revision`, `schemaVersion` and `createdAt`, so a second `--apply` updates wording without resetting an editor's revision or creation date. A slug that another key already owns is suffixed with the legacy id tail and reported, never merged.
- No audit entries and no revision snapshots are written: the import is not an editorial change, and seeding fake actors would pollute the trail.
- Evidence (dry run, real snapshot): the user's local `nuxt4-cms` database (123 `contents`, 6 `content_categories`, tenant `default`, all published, `vi`+`en`, no `attributes`, 69 `items` handled by `migrate:catalog`) printed 7 pages / 116 posts, 246 locales, formats `standard 101 / quote 6 / link 6 / gallery 4 / audio 3 / video 3`, 116 tagged and 116 categorized, 0 invalid, 0 conflicts, and left the 42 legacy collections unchanged.
- Evidence (apply into a disposable database): `tm_import_probe` (a copy of the two legacy collections) received 6 categories and 123 contents, all published, unique `(siteId, key)`, `pages/gioi-thieu` with `legacy.template`/`legacy.thumbnail` and both locales' SEO, `posts/dot-pha-ai-tao-sinh-1` with category `xu-huong-ai` and tags `ai/future/trend`; `legacy.format` landed on exactly the 22 non-standard records; a second `--apply` kept 123 rows, `revision 1` and the original `createdAt`.
- Evidence (storefront `:4016` on the imported database): `/api/public/content/gioi-thieu` and `/api/public/content/pages/gioi-thieu` → 200 with the legacy body, `?locale=en` returning `About Us`/`about-us`, `/api/public/content?type=post` listing imported posts, `/api/public/content/categories` returning the 6 migrated categories, `/gioi-thieu` rendering 200, and `/sitemap.xml` listing 160 URLs.
- Evidence (CMS `:4015`, site `sentuan-legacy` inside the registry database): `meta.counts` `{ all: 123, published: 123 }`, `?attributeKey=legacy.format&attributeValue=gallery` → exactly the 4 gallery posts, `?q=dot-pha-ai` → 10 matches, 6 categories; browser `/content` showed 123 rows and the detail drawer rendered both locales, the HTML body, tags and category, with an empty history (expected). The legacy copy and the imported site were deleted afterwards, and `sen-tuan` still holds its own 3 content records / 8 legacy contents.
- Both importers take `--tenant` (default `default`) and import one legacy tenant per run, print `legacy tenants {…}` with `(none)` for records that predate tenancy, and warn instead of planning nothing quietly: one line when the database holds none of the legacy collections (the `app_*_cms` mistake), another when the collections exist but nothing matches the tenant. A tenant bucket with more than one key therefore never reaches a site unseen — `tm_import_gate` seeds another tenant's item to prove it is skipped.
- The catalog report also prints `legacy item fields not carried {…}` / `legacy category fields not carried {…}`: the legacy fields that hold a value but have no v2 column, counted (`sen-tuan`, 2026-11-18: `supplierPrices 9`, `minStock/reserved/hasImei/sort/display/shipping/fullPath 241`, `options 223`, `history 85`; category `sort/searchString 67`, `banner/gallery/history 66`, `tags 60`). That set is a schema decision — inventory and supplier cost wait on P5/P6 — but it is now visible instead of implied, and the gate pins the line. The same pass carries `isDeleted`/`deletedAt` for items **and** categories: a soft-deleted legacy category used to be written back as live.
- Both reports now print a `field coverage {…}` line — how many planned records carry each field — because a dropped field is invisible in the totals: every catalog tag was missing for a day and the report still read `categories 67, items 241`. On the local copies (2026-11-18): `sen-tuan` content 8 of 8 with thumbnail/template/keywords/body/excerpt/SEO image, `bag-post` 18 of 18 thumbnails (the 7 `{ url }` ones included), 16 bodies, 9 with keywords; catalog `sen-tuan` tags 230 of 241, thumbnails 224, gallery 224, variants 223, sku 241, sale price 138, categories 241, category thumbnails 66 of 67, and `attributes: 0` because the legacy field is null. The operator-facing runbook is [§ Legacy import runbook in `08`](08-operations-and-verification.md#legacy-import-runbook-p10-2026-11-18).
- Field rules live in one module, `tm-cms/server/utils/legacyImport.ts`, shared by both importers: `legacyTags` (string, `{ title }`, `{ name }`; 50 × 60 chars), `legacyImageUrl` (`{ url | secure_url | src }`), `legacyKeywords`, `legacyLocalized`, `legacySlugMap`, `legacyNumber`, `legacyDate`, `legacyAttributeList`, `legacyContentAttributes`, `legacyContentStatus` and `legacyCatalogStatus`. They were duplicated per script, and a duplicated rule does not fail — it writes less data: `migrate:catalog` had dropped all 230 tagged items that way (fixed 2026-11-18), and `legacy.thumbnail` was kept only when the value was a string, silently losing the 7 `{ url }` thumbnails of `bag-post`'s 18 legacy contents. `pnpm test:legacy-import` (19 checks, no build or database) covers each shape, the caps, both status rules, and fails if either CLI declares its own copy again.
- Evidence (shared rules, 2026-11-18): dry runs after the refactor still plan `sen-tuan` 6 categories / 8 contents / 16 locales and `bag-post` 12 / 18 / 25 (17 `standard` + 1 `gallery`, 3 tagged, 5 gallery lists reported not carried), and `migrate:catalog` still plans `sen-tuan` 67 categories / 241 items — all without writing. A throwaway probe against the same legacy collections counted `sen-tuan` 8 string thumbnails and `bag-post` 11 string + 7 `{ url }`, and the shared rule now yields `https://images.unsplash.com/photo-…` for the documents that previously produced no `legacy.thumbnail` at all.
- The `--apply` half is gated too: `pnpm test:migrate-apply` (25 checks, disposable `tm_import_gate`) spawns both CLIs with the flags an operator uses against a synthetic fixture that holds every legacy shape, a duplicate category and item slug, a record whose slug an existing CMS row already owns, one soft-deleted record, a published-but-inactive catalog item, a variant without a SKU and one unusable record. It asserts a dry run creates no collection, the first apply writes `revision 1` / `schemaVersion 1` with mapped tags, `legacy.*` attributes, resolved category links and the expected statuses, a second apply changes no revision, creation date or count, and the CMS-owned row survives with its own revision.
- Two defects that gate caught, both real data loss on `--apply`: (1) a duplicated category slug — one inside the legacy data, or one an editor already owns — kept both records' slug while only the *key* was suffixed, so the unique `(siteId, locale, slug)` / `(siteId, slug.vi)` index aborted the run halfway with `E11000` after some rows had been written; both importers now suffix the category slug with the legacy id tail and report it (`category slug conflicts resolved`). (2) `migrate:catalog` called `prepareDatabase` before planning, so even a dry run created the target collections, their indexes and `tm_migrations` rows in the customer database — planning now reads the target collections raw, like the content importer, and prepares the models only with `--apply`.
- `pnpm check:migration --site <id>` (2026-11-18) is the read-only parity report for after the fact and for a rehearsal: content, content categories, catalog items and categories, published counts, nested legacy paths, and whether settings carries a company identity. v2 may hold **more** than the source but never fewer; fewer prints `❌` and exits non-zero. Its first run found something real: `sen-tuan` catalog matched (241/67, 229 published) but **content had never been applied** (8 usable records, 2 rows in v2, 0 categories) and `bag-post` had 18 records and 0 rows. The local rehearsal was then completed on 2026-11-18 — content applied to both copies (`sen-tuan` 6 categories / 8 contents, `bag-post` 12 / 18, each self-checked), `migrate:settings` wrote the site identity, and `migrate:redirects` wrote 288 rows (verified 288/288) *after* the content apply, so the live-path guard saw the published content slugs. Both copies now report `✅ parity` on every domain: `sen-tuan` content 8→9, categories 6→6, catalog 241/67 and 229 published, 288 301 rows, settings identity with `['vi','en']`; `bag-post` content 18→18, categories 12→12, identity with `['vi']`.
- Every apply now ends with a self-check: `migrate:content`, `migrate:catalog` and `migrate:redirects` re-read the database for exactly the keys, `legacyId`s and redirect paths the plan expected (`verified N/N`). A write loop can stop half-way and still print a healthy count — the 2026-11-18 `E11000` did — so a mismatch prints a warning and exits non-zero instead of looking like success.
- Site identity comes from `pnpm migrate:settings --site <id> [--apply]` (2026-11-18): an explicit four-key allowlist from legacy `system_configs` → `settings` (`site_name` → `company.name`/`names.vi`, `site_description` → `seo.defaultDescription`, `content_locales` → `locales.enabled`, `enabled_modules` → `modules.content`/`catalog`), written once and never over an edited document. The allowlist is narrow because the same collection holds plaintext credentials — on the local copies 18 keys, including JWT and revalidate secrets, a Google client secret, a Firebase private key, a VAPID private key, a Cloudinary API secret and MongoDB URIs with a password: the command reports those **by name only**, imports none of them, and never prints a value.
- Legacy URLs are preserved by `pnpm migrate:redirects --site <id> [--apply]` (2026-11-18): it plans a 301 from each legacy `fullPath` (`/<category-slug>/<slug>` for products, `/categories/<slug>` for categories) to the row `migrate:catalog --apply` actually wrote. It writes only what the database proves — a published v2 target, at least two path segments, no published page owning the path, no existing row (a human mapping always wins), and no path claimed by two legacy records (that one is reported, never guessed) — and it reports legacy category paths that are a single segment instead of guessing, because `/<slug>` is also the content short URL. On the local `sen-tuan` copy it plans 288 paths: 227 products, 61 categories, 12 skipped for a missing/withdrawn v2 row, 1 ambiguous, 6 single-segment categories reported.
- Evidence (served storefront, 2026-11-19): `pnpm dev:web` on `TM_MONGO_DB=sen-tuan` — the database the applies wrote — renders the migrated identity from `settings` (name plus the legacy description), six products with prices, six catalog categories and the migrated post on the home page; `/products/chan-to-tam` shows `₫1,050,000`, `SKU-725TRX9M` and the category breadcrumb, and its "Out of stock" is the source data (221 of 241 legacy items carry `stock: 0`), not a dropped field; `/gioi-thieu` renders the 981-character migrated body; `/bai-viet` and the short URL `/ffhyguyhguii` open the migrated post; three sampled legacy product URLs answer `301` with `Location: /products/<slug>` from the server, against the 288 rows the redirect plan wrote. Limits of that pass: one click, one viewport, no language switch, no CMS session.
- `pnpm rehearse --site <id> [--tenant] [--apply]` (2026-11-19) runs the five documented steps — content, catalog, redirects, settings, then `check:migration` — as separate processes, stops at the first failure instead of planning dependents against a half-written database, and refuses a `TM_MONGO_DB` named like a v2 app database before running anything. It owns no migration logic: each step's own report is printed under its header. Gated through the real CLIs on the fixture (51 checks); on the local `sen-tuan` copy the apply run printed `verified 6/6 categories, 8/8 contents`, `67/67 categories, 241/241 items`, `0/0 redirects` (all 288 rows kept) and the parity verdict for every domain.
- An empty legacy copy is reported as such (2026-11-19): `bag-post` holds `items`/`item_categories` with no documents, and the report used to tell the operator to change a correct `TM_MONGO_DB`. The collection state is now read explicitly, so *absent* collections keep the wrong-database hint while *present and empty* prints "this copy has nothing to import" — same for `contents`, `system_configs` and the parity report.
- Owner decisions J1/J2 (2026-11-19) settle the two open migration questions without a new renderer or a new redirect rule. `legacy.format` and the original HTML body are kept for lookup; v2 renders none of `gallery`/`video`/`audio`/`quote`/`link` and never turns a gallery/media URL into a media id without a mapping. A writable record with no body, or with a gallery/media list that was not carried, is marked `legacy.incomplete` (`no-body`/`gallery`/`media`, plus `legacy.gallerySkipped`/`legacy.mediaSkipped`) so it cannot read as a finished migration; the dry run prints `not fully migrated N: …` and `check:migration` prints `ℹ️ not fully migrated N content row(s)`. Single-segment legacy category paths `/<slug>` stay unredirected — the root URL can belong to content — and are redirected only by hand, after each URL is compared with the published content slugs. Re-applied to the local `bag-post` copy on 2026-11-19: 7 of its 18 records are marked (`no body 2`, `gallery 5`), the self-check printed `verified 12/12 categories, 18/18 contents`, `check:migration` prints `ℹ️ not fully migrated 7 content row(s)` and parity stays ✅; `sen-tuan` has none (8 bodies, no gallery or media lists).
- Importer ↔ parity consistency (2026-11-19): both importers plan **every** legacy catalog item and category — a row with no slug still gets `item-<legacyId>` (item) or its key as the VI slug (category), so the parity counter may compare against all of them without a false loss. Content is the one place where the importer deliberately skips (no locale with both title and slug), and `check:migration` applies the same filter and prints how many it skipped, so a rule-driven skip never reads as a lost record. Verified on the local copies: `sen-tuan` 241 items / 67 categories, `bag-post` 18 contents / 12 categories all counted on both sides.
- J1/J2 re-check on the migrated local data (2026-11-19): `sen-tuan` 9 content rows carry no `legacy.format` and no `legacy.incomplete`; `bag-post` 18 rows, 1 `gallery` format, 7 marked `legacy.incomplete` (`gallery 5`, `no-body 2`). Neither copy contains a legacy URL that was converted into a media id — the single `res.cloudinary.com` reference is still the original `<img src>` inside the HTML body. Both copies hold **0** single-segment category redirects, so J2's manual-mapping rule has not been bypassed.
- Media cross-host: **BLOCKED, not implemented.** `mediaService` builds `url` as `<request origin>/media/<storage.key>`, and the bytes live on the CMS host's local disk (`TM_MEDIA_DIR` or `.data/media`); `tm-web` has no `/media` route and no proxy. A storefront on another origin therefore cannot serve a CMS-host media URL, and no object-storage/public-origin provider is configured locally, so no adapter was written (plan Batch 2 leaves it as a recorded blocker rather than a guessed provider).
- Limits: no media/gallery migration, no tags/keywords vocabulary migration (v2 tags are strings on the record), no menu import, and legacy `parent`, `display` and `sort` only survive as attributes.

### WEB-02 — a slug change leaves its own 301 (landed 2026-11-11)

- Why: the storefront resolves a record by its immutable key (`/pages/gioi-thieu` keeps working after a rename) or by the locale slug, served at the short path `/<slug>`. So renaming the slug broke exactly one URL — the short one — and nothing repaired it unless an editor remembered to retype the old path in the redirect map.
- Service: `tm-cms/server/services/contentSlugRedirects.ts` — `changedSlugPaths` compares the stored and submitted `locales`, and `syncContentSlugRedirects` writes one row per changed locale with `$setOnInsert` on `(siteId, from)`: `301`, active, `note: 'auto: slug changed'`. An old path that is still a slug of the record after the save (a VI/EN swap) is not redirected, one old path with two different new targets is skipped as ambiguous, and an old path a published or scheduled record still owns is skipped. When a record is published, an automatic row under its live short paths is removed, so reusing an old slug leaves the new page reachable; a manual row is untouched. It is called from `updateContent`, `restoreContentRevision` and a publish transition through the new `ContentWriteMeta.redirects`, wired in `PATCH /api/v1/admin/content/:id`, `POST …/revisions/:revision/restore`, the content event route and the bulk route from the already-prepared `Redirect` model; a bulk publish therefore clears the same automatic rows.
- Guards: a draft writes nothing (nobody could have visited its URL); a locale whose slug did not change writes nothing; an old path that already has a row is kept untouched, because a hand-typed target is more specific than a generated one and `resolveRedirect` already follows chains; an upsert that loses a race is reported as kept instead of failing the save; an old slug that names a real storefront route (`about`, `contact`, `products`, `categories`, `danh-muc`, `bai-viet`, `tag`, `tim-kiem`, `gio-hang`) is skipped, since such a page was never reachable there and a row would hijack the route.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB): `pnpm test:content` → 37 service checks; the redirect check publishes a page, renames it twice, and asserts the generated `/ten-cu` → `/ten-moi` row (301, active, auto note), that a hand-typed `/ten-moi` → `/gioi-thieu` 302 survives the next rename, that a draft rename writes nothing, that two locales produce their own rows while a newly added locale produces none, that another site sees no rows, and that renaming a page slugged `products` writes no `/products` row. Further checks publish a record, swap its VI/EN slugs, and assert neither live path gets a row; a record whose two old locale paths collapse onto one ambiguous old path is also left without a row; publishing a record that reuses an old slug removes the automatic row; and an old path still owned by another published record gets no row. `pnpm test:bulk` adds the bulk-publish path for the same cleanup. A 2026-11-17 HTTP probe on the real routes published a page, renamed it, and observed `/probe-http-move-cu` → `301 /probe-http-move-moi` with the new path `200`; a second page created on `/probe-http-cu` and published through the bulk route removed the automatic row, and `/probe-http-cu` then answered `200`. The three probe records, their 8 revisions, 8 audit rows, the 1 generated redirect and 3 idempotency keys were removed afterwards.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4005` + storefront `:4006`, real `sen-tuan`, disposable probe page): create `201`, publish `200`, then `PATCH` with a new slug `200`; `GET /api/v1/admin/redirects?q=probe-slug` returned exactly one row `/probe-slug-moi → /probe-slug-ba` (`301`, active, `auto: slug changed`); `GET :4006/probe-slug-moi` → `301 Location: /probe-slug-ba`, `GET /probe-slug-ba` → `200`, and `/pages/probe-slug-cu` (the immutable key path) still `200`, which is why the key path is not redirected. Browser `:4005/redirects` showed the row with its `301` badge, `Đang bật` and the auto note. The probe record, its revisions, its audit rows and the generated row were deleted afterwards (`content` 3, `content_redirects` 0, `audit_logs` 1).
- Limits: only the short `/<slug>` form is written (the key path does not change, and the reserved route names are skipped); a row generated for an old slug is not re-pointed when the same record is renamed again — the chain resolves, but the map grows one row per rename; unpublishing, archiving or deleting a record removes the automatic rows that lead to or from its paths (manual rows stay, and a later restore does not recreate them); there is still no automatic row for a legacy import.

### WEB-08.v — the catalog branch of the empty-site test (landed 2026-11-11)

- `pnpm test:empty-listing` now covers the catalog the same way it covers posts: it copies one published item and one live category out of `sen-tuan` (so the record shape is real, not guessed), retags them for the borrowed site, and checks that `/products` turns `index, follow`, `/categories/<slug>` turns `index, follow` with its own canonical, the category index data lists the category, and the sitemap gains `/products`, `/categories`, the category URL and the product URL. Both rows are deleted before the run ends.
- Two things the catalog forced into the open. Slugs are per locale in `catalog_items`/`catalog_categories` (`slug.vi`), so a plain-string slug made the category invisible to the page lookup while still reaching the sitemap — the probe now writes both locales and clears `pid`/`ancestors` so it is a real top-level category. And `/api/public/categories` is cached for five minutes, so the category index page cannot flip inside a run; its data is asserted through the same API under an unused query key, while `/products` (60s cache) is watched flipping on the page itself.
- The run also had to learn two lessons about its own environment: Nitro keeps its handler cache on disk (`.nuxt/cache/nitro`), so a previous run's answer could be served to the next one — the script now clears it before starting; and a killed run can leave probe rows behind, so every probe row is deleted before the emptiness gate, not only before its own insert. The empty-state checks now wait for `noindex, follow` instead of assuming it, which removes the last ordering dependency.
- Evidence: `pnpm test:empty-listing` → 31 checks, exit 0. Afterwards the borrowed database has 0 content, 0 catalog items, 0 catalog categories, 0 redirects and 0 idempotency keys. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the catalog rows are inserted directly, so this proves the storefront's read/index rules, not the CMS catalog write path; and the category index page's own robots flip is inferred from its data because of the five-minute cache.

### WEB-08.u — the populated branch is covered too (landed 2026-11-11)

- Every earlier check in `pnpm test:empty-listing` proved the empty side. The run now writes one published post into the borrowed customer database (`key` and `slug` `probe-listing-record`, tag `probe-listing`) and proves the other direction: `/bai-viet` turns `index, follow` with its own canonical, `/tag/probe-listing` turns `index, follow`, and the sitemap gains both the listing row and the record's URL. It then deletes the record and proves the flip comes back — `/bai-viet` `noindex, follow` again and both sitemap rows gone.
- The record is written before the flip and removed before the run ends, and the removal is idempotent in `finally`, so a failed check cannot leave the borrowed site populated. 25 checks against `bag-post`; afterwards `content`, `content_redirects`, `audit_logs` and `idempotency_keys` are all 0 and no `probe-listing-record` remains.
- The sitemap sweep was also fixed while adding this: the "every sitemap URL is 200, indexable and self-canonical" check no longer returns early from `main`, so the record branch runs on a sitemap that has rows (and still skips cleanly when it has none).
- Evidence: `pnpm test:empty-listing` → 25 checks, including all five populated-side assertions; production `:4006` untouched and answering after the run. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the record is inserted straight into the `content` collection rather than through the CMS write path, so this proves the storefront's read and index rules, not the editor's publish flow — that stays with the service tests.

### WEB-08.t — 410 coverage, and why `/danh-muc` looked empty (landed 2026-11-11)

- `pnpm test:empty-listing` now covers the gone page too: the run writes one `content_redirects` row (`410`, no target) into the borrowed customer database, polls the path until the redirect map's 30s cache settles, asserts `410` + `noindex, follow` + no canonical, asserts the sitemap never lists it, and deletes the row in `finally` even when a check fails. 20 checks against `bag-post`; `bag-post.content_redirects` was back to 0 afterwards.
- The six `content_categories` documents sitting in `sen-tuan` are not a storefront filter at all — they are dead legacy data. The storefront reads the CMS `categories` collection (`key`, `parent`, `locales[]`), and that collection holds **0** documents for this site, which is the whole reason `/danh-muc` renders its empty state. The six rows use the old CMS shape (`tenantId`, `slug.vi`, `title.vi`, `pid`, `fullPath`, `ancestors`, `__v`) from a database that also carries `bag-post`-style content; nothing on either side reads that collection, and cleaning it up belongs to the Hub import slice rather than here.
- With that known, the empty branch of `/danh-muc/<slug>` was driven live instead of inferred: a category created through the admin API (no published post carries it) answered `200` + `noindex, follow` with a canonical pointing at itself, `/danh-muc` switched to `index, follow` while it listed that one category, the sitemap never carried the probe, and after deleting it the landing went `404` and the index back to `noindex`. Cleanup left `categories` 0, `content_categories` 6 (untouched), `audit_logs` 1, `content_redirects` 0 and `idempotency_keys` 0.
- Evidence: `pnpm test:empty-listing` → 20 checks; the probe's own five checks; live `:4006` after cleanup: `/danh-muc` `noindex`, `/danh-muc/probe-danh-muc-rong` `404`, sitemap 303 rows. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the probe writes to the real `sen-tuan` database for the length of the run; a crashed run would leave a category behind (named `probe-danh-muc-rong`), so it is written to be recognisable and deletable by hand.

### WEB-08.s — the last inline rule, and 404 coverage (landed 2026-11-11)

- `/danh-muc/<slug>` was the one page still carrying the rule inline, because that file is under the editor's in-flight work. The robots branch alone was moved to `usePageRobots({ empty: () => !items.value.length })` and committed on its own: the staged change contains that hunk and nothing else, and the editor's other changes to the same file are untouched and still uncommitted.
- `pnpm test:empty-listing` now also asserts that a detail URL for a record the site does not have is a real `404` with `noindex, follow` and no canonical, for `/products/<slug>` and `/categories/<slug>` — the two pages where an empty-but-`200` detail was a real bug earlier in this run. 18 checks against `bag-post`.
- Evidence: `pnpm test:empty-listing` → 18 checks. Live `:4006`: `/danh-muc` `200` + `noindex` (unchanged), `/danh-muc/khong-ton-tai`, `/products/khong-ton-tai` and `/categories/khong-ton-tai` all `404` + `noindex` with no canonical, `/products` and `/` still `index, follow`, sitemap still 303 rows. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the migrated empty rule on `/danh-muc/<slug>` cannot be driven live on `sen-tuan` because none of its six content categories is served by the site, so only the 404 path of that page is exercised; the composable branch itself is identical to the tag and listing pages that are covered.

### WEB-08.r — two URLs that changed answer, on purpose (landed 2026-11-11)

- `usePageRobots` made two URLs `noindex, follow` that used to be `index, follow`, and both are intended:
  - `/products?sort=newest` is the bare catalog: `newest` is the default order, so the page renders exactly what `/products` renders. The old filter check compared the sort value against the default and called this "unfiltered"; the shared rule only asks whether the URL carries a parameter the page needs to name itself, and `sort` is not one. A duplicate of the canonical page should not compete with it.
  - `/bai-viet?page=2` (and `?page=1`) never had a page two to serve: the post listing pages by cursor, so `page` is ignored and the response is the same as `/bai-viet`. It is a query view like `/tag/about?page=2`, and now answers the same way.
- Both keep their existing canonicals (`/products` and `/bai-viet`), neither is in the sitemap, and the canonical URLs are unchanged and still indexable, so nothing that was crawlable before has become unreachable.
- `/bai-viet` gained numbered pagination when `settings.site.paginationType` is `offset` (2026-11-26): that mode passes `keepQuery: ['page']` and the paged-canonical rule, exactly as predicted here; with the default `cursor` this answer is unchanged.
- Evidence, live `:4006`: `/products?sort=newest` and `?sort=price-asc` `noindex, follow` with canonical `/products`; `?page=2` `index, follow` with canonical `?page=2`; `?page=2&sort=newest` `noindex, follow` with canonical `?page=2`; `/bai-viet` `index, follow`, `?page=1` and `?page=2` `noindex, follow` with canonical `/bai-viet`.
- Whole build suite re-run after the refactor: all 24 `scripts/build/test-*.ts` pass, including the two files the editor has in flight and the database-backed storefront checks.

### WEB-08.q — one robots rule, in one place (landed 2026-11-11)

- The rule the last ten slices kept re-deriving now lives in `usePageRobots` (`tm-shared/nuxt-layers/web-base/app/composables/usePageRobots.ts`): a site with `indexable: false` answers `noindex, nofollow`; a query view (any query key the page does not use to name a page), an alias of another URL, or an empty page answers `noindex, follow`; everything else is `index, follow`. Pages pass what they know — `keepQuery: ['page']` for the paged catalog views, `empty` for a listing that has nothing yet, `alias` for `/pages/<key>`, `never` for the cart and search.
- Eleven pages dropped their copy of the rule, including the two that had grown subtle branches (`/products` and `/categories/<slug>` decide between a paged view and a filter). Two behaviours moved with it and are worth naming: `/tag/<tag>` no longer treats `?page=` as anything but a view (it pages by cursor), and the cart and search now honour the site-wide switch by answering `noindex, nofollow` where they used to say `noindex, follow`.
- `pnpm test:empty-listing` now covers the tag landing and its query view on an empty database (16 checks), so the newest page in the family is covered without a hand-written probe.
- Evidence: `pnpm test:empty-listing` → 16 checks against `bag-post`. Re-check on real data `:4006` after the refactor: 22 URLs covering home, search, cart, both listings, both indexes, tag plain/query/unknown, content detail and alias, unknown content, product plain/query, category plain/paged/filtered all answer exactly as before; sitemap still 303 rows. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: `danh-muc/[slug].vue` still carries the inline four-line rule because that file is under the editor's in-flight work and was not opened for this slice; it can join the composable when that work lands.

### WEB-08.p — tag pages and their query views (landed 2026-11-11)

- `/tag/<tag>` pages through a cursor, so it has no `?page=` of its own: `/tag/about?page=2` is served identically to page one, and it was still `index, follow` while its canonical pointed at `/tag/about`. Every query view of a tag is now `noindex, follow` with the canonical unchanged. Because there are no page URLs, there is no page-two canonical to fix — the same reason `/bai-viet` needed none.
- An unknown tag stays `200` + `noindex, follow`: the tag may exist on drafts only, and the earlier decision was that the empty state renders rather than a 404. Nothing to change, and it is out of the sitemap.
- Evidence, live `:4006` (6 URLs): `/tag/about` `index` with self canonical and self `og:url`; `?page=2`, `?page=1` and `?utm_source=x` `noindex` with canonical `/tag/about`; `/tag/huong-dan` (draft-only tag) and `/tag/khong-ton-tai` `200` + `noindex` with self canonical. Sitemap stayed at 303 rows, includes `/tag/about`, and carries no `?page=` or unknown-tag rows. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: cursor paging means the "page two" case is answered by ignoring the parameter, not by serving a second page, so a future numbered tag pagination would need the paged-canonical rule the catalog uses.

### WEB-08.o — catalog detail pages follow the same rules (landed 2026-11-11)

- `/products/<slug>` was correct for the plain URL — `200`, `index, follow`, canonical and `og:url` on the product's own path, a sitemap row with `lastmod` — but a query view of it (`?utm_source=news`) also asked to be indexed, so it now answers `noindex, follow` with the canonical unchanged. An unknown product was already a real `404`, not an empty page at `200`, and the error page supplies its `noindex`.
- `/categories/<slug>` carried the bug the `/products` listing had before: `?page=2` canonicalised to page one, so a crawler following the canonical would never reach the rest of the category. A plain paged list is now its own canonical (`?page=N`) and stays indexable, page one keeps the bare URL, and any other parameter (`?sort=`, `?page=2&sort=`) makes the view `noindex, follow` with the canonical on the bare category URL. A category with nothing on page one is `noindex, follow` as well, matching the rest of the listings.
- Evidence, live `:4006` (28 checks): product `index` + self canonical + self `og:url`; product `?utm_source` `noindex` + self canonical; unknown product `404` + `noindex` with no canonical; category `index` + self canonical; `?page=2` `index` + canonical `?page=2`; `?page=1` canonical back to the bare URL; `?sort=price-asc` and `?page=2&sort=price-asc` `noindex` + bare canonical; unknown category `404` + `noindex` with no canonical. Sitemap stayed at 303 rows with the product and category rows present, and carries no `?page=` or unknown-slug rows. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the empty-category branch is not driven live because all 67 catalog categories carry products; the paged canonical has only been exercised on page two of one category.

### WEB-08.n — one record, one URL; error pages out of the index (landed 2026-11-11)

- `/<slug>` itself was already correct: `200` with `index, follow`, canonical and `og:url` on the short URL, a matching sitemap row with `lastmod`, and no `/pages/` rows in the sitemap.
- Two ways to reach the same record still asked to be indexed: the `/pages/<key>` alias (which correctly canonicals to `/<slug>`) and any query view (`?utm_source=`, `?x=`). Both are `noindex, follow` now, with the canonical untouched; the rule is "index only when the URL is the canonical one", so a future alias inherits it without a new branch. The site-wide `indexable: false` switch still wins.
- The error page carried no head at all: no `robots`, and correctly no canonical. It now answers `noindex, follow` for every status it renders — 404, 410 and 5xx — because an error body is never a destination. It still has no canonical, and it keeps system-level copy rather than inventing page metadata.
- Evidence, live `:4006` against real data (26 checks): `/gioi-thieu` `index` + canonical `/<slug>`; `?utm_source=news` and `/pages/gioi-thieu` `noindex` + canonical `/<slug>`, the alias still `200`; `/khong-ton-tai-abc`, `/pages/khong-ton-tai` and the draft `/dich-vu` all `404` + `noindex` with no canonical; a probe `410` row (`/probe-410-audit`, created through the admin API, removed afterwards) answered `410` + `noindex` with no canonical and never appeared in the sitemap; sitemap stayed at 303 rows. Cleanup left `content` 2, `content_redirects` 0, `audit_logs` 1 and `idempotency_keys` 0, exactly the pre-probe state. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the 5xx branch shares the same `useHead` but was not driven live; an error page's title still comes from the site defaults rather than the error copy.

### WEB-08.m — home and search under the same criteria (landed 2026-11-11)

- `/tim-kiem` came through the audit unchanged and correct: `noindex, follow`, canonical and `og:url` pointing at `/tim-kiem` with the query stripped, absent from the sitemap, and no `SearchAction` in the site JSON-LD. A query view of it (`?q=about`, `?q=khong-co-gi`) inherits the same answer.
- The home page had the one gap: `/` was `index, follow`, but so was every query variant of it — `?utm_source=news` and even `?page=2`, a parameter the page ignores. Each answered as a second home page while canonically claiming to be the first. Only the bare `/` is that page now; a query string makes it `noindex, follow` with the canonical still on `/`. The site-wide `indexable: false` switch still wins with `noindex, nofollow`.
- The home page is the one listing-shaped page that stays indexable with nothing to list: it carries the brand, the description and the site JSON-LD. `pnpm test:empty-listing` now proves that too, alongside `/tim-kiem` (indexable home, noindex search, and the search page absent from the empty sitemap), so the new rule is covered on a site with no content rather than only on this one.
- Evidence: live `:4006` — `/` `index, follow` + canonical `/`; `/?utm_source=news` and `/?page=2` `noindex, follow` + canonical `/`; `/tim-kiem`, `?q=about`, `?q=khong-co-gi` `noindex, follow` + canonical `/tim-kiem`; sitemap 303 URLs, home present, `/tim-kiem` absent, all rows `200` + indexable. `pnpm test:empty-listing` → 14 checks against `bag-post`. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the home query rule covers unknown parameters by design, so a future feature that puts real content behind a home query would need its own indexable URL instead of reusing `/`.

### WEB-08.l — the empty-listing branch is a script, not an afternoon (landed 2026-11-11)

- The empty branch was proved by hand once, by pointing the web app at another customer's empty database. A proof that costs a manual restart, an ad-hoc script and a careful restore is one nobody repeats, so it is now `pnpm test:empty-listing` (`scripts/build/test-empty-listing.ts`).
- The script resolves its customer from the registry, so the site and its database always agree: `--site=bagpost` (the default) reads `bag-post`, and the switch happens only in the temporary server's environment. It refuses to run if `content`, `catalog_items` or `catalog_categories` hold rows, because a populated customer cannot show the empty state and the checks would be measuring the wrong thing. `content_categories` is reported but not gated: its rows can belong to another site or locale and still be served nowhere, and if the site does serve one the `/danh-muc` check fails with the honest reason.
- It starts Nuxt on a free port with that customer, waits for a real `200` on `/`, then asserts: `/bai-viet`, `/products`, `/categories` and `/danh-muc` answer `200` + `noindex, follow` with a canonical pointing at themselves; `/gio-hang` is `noindex, follow` with no canonical; the sitemap still lists `/` and lists none of the noindex listings; every sitemap row is `200`, indexable and self-canonical.
- The run stops the server it started, leaves the database untouched (it only counts and reads) and leaves nothing to restore, because it never touched the site you had running. Nuxt owns `tm-web/.nuxt`, so the port must be free: the script says so instead of starting a second dev server over the same build directory.
- Evidence: `pnpm test:empty-listing` → 12 checks pass against `bag-post` (content 0, catalog 0), port released afterwards, `.devlogs/web-empty-bagpost.log`. Guards exercised: `--site=sentuan` refuses with the row counts, `--site=default` refuses for having no database binding, and neither starts a server.
- Limits: it proves the robots/canonical/sitemap policy, not the empty-state copy; a customer without an empty database still has to be cleared or added to the registry before this test can be pointed at it.

### WEB-08.k — listing views answer search engines honestly (landed 2026-11-11)

- `/bai-viet`, `/products` and `/categories` were audited with the criteria the tag and category landings were fixed with. `/gio-hang` needed nothing: it was already `noindex, follow` with no canonical, which is right for a per-visitor cart.
- Two real problems. First, a filtered or searched view (`?tag=`, `?q=`, `?sort=`, price bounds) answered `index, follow` while its canonical pointed at the plain listing: the page asked to be indexed as a page it simultaneously said it was not. Those views are `noindex, follow` now; the facets that deserve an index entry already have their own URLs.
- Second, `/products?page=2` canonicalised to `/products`, so a crawler following the canonical would never see the rest of the catalog. A paged view with no filters is now its own canonical (`/products?page=N`) and stays indexable; combined with a filter it falls back to the filtered rule (canonical `/products`, `noindex`).
- An empty listing (no published posts, no products, no categories) is `noindex, follow` as well, matching the tag and content-category policy; it becomes indexable with its first record. The site-wide `indexable: false` switch still wins with `noindex, nofollow`.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data, 11 checks, all passing): `/bai-viet` `index, follow` + canonical `/bai-viet`; `/bai-viet?tag=about`, `?tag=huong-dan`, `?q=khong-co-gi` `noindex, follow`; `/products` `index, follow` + canonical `/products`; `/products?page=2` `index, follow` + canonical `?page=2`; `/products?sort=price-asc`, `?page=2&sort=price-asc`, `?q=khong-co-gi` `noindex, follow` + canonical `/products`; `/categories` `index, follow`; `/gio-hang` `noindex, follow` with no canonical.
- The sitemap was re-read after these changes: 303 URLs, every one 200 with a self-referencing canonical and no `noindex`, so nothing had to be removed — the sitemap never carried a filtered, paged or empty view in the first place. Paged catalogs stay out of it because it lists `/products` once, as the collection, not page by page.
- The empty branch was then driven live rather than left to inspection, using the registry's other customer `bagpost` and its own `bag-post` database (content 0, catalog 0), which is separate data, not a fabricated record: `/bai-viet`, `/products` and `/categories` all answered `200` + `noindex, follow` with their empty-state copy, `/gio-hang` stayed `noindex`, and the sitemap collapsed to the single `/` row. Nothing was written to either database. Restoring `sentuan` reproduced the indexable state and the 303-URL sitemap.
- That run found one gap in the same family: `/danh-muc`, the content-category index, was still `index, follow` with nothing to list. It now returns `noindex, follow` until its first category (site-wide `indexable: false` still wins), which also matches its absence from the sitemap.
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: `/bai-viet` paginates by cursor, so it has no page URLs to self-canonicalise; the empty-state wording was matched in vi only.

### WEB-08.j — an empty content category landing is noindex (landed 2026-11-11)

- `/danh-muc/<slug>` was audited against the tag problems. Two of the three were already right: an unknown slug answers `404` (the category read throws), and canonical plus `og:url` are the absolute short URL for both locales. The third was not: a category that exists but lists nothing was `index, follow`, so every empty landing was indexable thin content.
- It now answers `noindex, follow` while it lists no published post and `index, follow` once one carries it, matching the rule the tag landings follow; the site-wide `indexable: false` switch still wins with `noindex, nofollow`. The page stays `200` with its own empty copy ("Danh mục “…” chưa có nội dung nào được đăng"), because the category is a real, published-by-an-editor page rather than an invented URL.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` database with a temporary probe category, removed afterwards): `/danh-muc/probe-danh-muc` → `200`, `noindex, follow` in vi and en, canonical and `og:url` both `http://localhost:4006/danh-muc/probe-danh-muc`, empty copy rendered; `/danh-muc/khong-ton-tai` → `404`; `/danh-muc` → `200` `index, follow`. Probe category and both audit rows deleted (`categories` 0, `audit_logs` 1).
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the "has posts" branch was not exercised live (that needs a published post assigned to the probe category); it is the same expression the tag page already proved. This page also carries an in-progress editor field in the working tree, so only the robots change is in this commit — the page's breadcrumb, `ItemList` and absolute share-image additions from WEB-08.b/.f are still uncommitted with that work.

### WEB-08.i — an empty tag page is noindex (landed 2026-11-11)

- A tag landing is `/tag/<anything>`, and it answered `index, follow` with an empty list for a tag that no published record carries (`/tag/khong-ton-tai`), so the site offered crawlers an unlimited supply of indexable empty pages built from a URL anyone can type.
- The page also listed a tag that only a draft carries (`/tag/huong-dan`), which is right for a visitor and wrong for an index. It now answers `noindex, follow` while it has nothing published, and `index, follow` as soon as one post carries the tag. The site-wide `indexable: false` switch still wins with `noindex, nofollow`.
- A tag page still returns `200` with its empty state rather than a 404: the tag may exist on drafts only, and pointing a visitor at the posts is better than a dead end. No sitemap row changes: the tag entries come from published content only.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `/tag/about` → `200` `index, follow`; `/tag/huong-dan` (draft-only tag) → `200` `noindex, follow`; `/tag/khong-ton-tai` (invented tag) → `200` `noindex, follow` and still renders "Chưa có bài viết".
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: a tag that exists only on drafts is still a 200 page (deliberate), and an empty tag has no `lastmod` in the sitemap because it is not listed there at all.

### WEB-03/CAT-01 — a variable product offers its size and colour options (landed 2026-11-23)

- The detail payload exposed variants as a display name plus a price, so the page could only list one button per SKU: a 25-variant mattress read as 25 anonymous buttons (`SKU-…-120X190-5F`), and a visitor could not say whether they were picking a size or a thickness.
- Variants now carry `attributes: [{ name, value }]` in `PublicCatalogVariantSchema`, and `products/[slug]` builds one chip group per option name (`Kích thước`, `Độ dầy`) in first-appearance order, with the active value pressed and a value disabled when no variant sells that combination with the other current choices. The chip helper (`buildVariantOptionGroups`, `selectVariantWithOption`, `isVariantOptionAvailable`) is a pure module in the web layer, so the mapping is checked without a browser. A product whose variants carry no options keeps the single variant list.
- The group labels come from the item's own legacy `options` list: variants store the option **code** (`kich-thuoc`), so `migrate:catalog` now resolves each attribute name/value through the item's options and reports `optionLabels` in its coverage line (678 labels on `sen-tuan`, 0 before the change). The first attempt at this feature shipped labels like `kich-thuoc` on screen because only the variant attributes had been migrated.
- Detail projection already selected `variants`, and it continues to: the missing piece was the DTO's variant shape, not the Mongo projection.
- Evidence (`HTTP :4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `GET /api/public/products/dem-kc-titalium` → 25 variants, each with `attributes` (`Kích thước = 120x190`, `Độ dầy = 5F`); the server-rendered `/products/dem-kc-titalium` contains the `Kích thước` and `Độ dầy` groups, exactly two `aria-pressed="true"` chips (the default variant) and disabled chips for combinations the catalog does not hold. `pnpm test:web-variant-options` → 8 checks; `pnpm test:migrate-apply` covers the code→title mapping (53 checks); `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- The CMS editor now edits those pairs too: each variant row carries its own "Variant options" list (name/value inputs with add and remove), blank rows are dropped instead of saved, and the item's own attributes list is unchanged. Without this, an editor could only inherit options from the migration and a newly created variable product had no way to say what its variants differ in. `pnpm test:catalog` (47 checks) creates a variant with an option, reads it back and replaces the set on update, including a variant that ends with none.
- Limits: chip clicks were not exercised in a browser (only the server-rendered pressed/disabled state and the pure helpers), and the CMS option editor was checked by typecheck plus `test:catalog`, not by saving a form in a browser.

### WEB-03 — an unknown product or category answers 404, not an empty 200 (landed 2026-11-11)

- An unknown product slug, and an unknown catalog category slug, both answered `200` with the header, the footer and nothing between them: a soft 404 that a crawler indexes and a visitor reads as a broken page. The API behind them already answered `404`, so the page was losing that answer in three places at once.
- `catalogFailure()` read only `error.statusCode`, but `$fetch` also reports the status as `status` or on `response.status`; a real 404 was classified as "unavailable", and the page rendered its disconnected/empty state with 200. All three places are read now.
- The page's guard ran during `setup`, but the catalog read settles after setup (`pending` is still true then), so the guard could never fire. `useCatalogItem`/`useCatalogCategory` now expose a `ready` signal, and the server awaits it before deciding the status; on the client a watcher on the same status calls `showError`, so navigation to a missing slug also lands on the error page.
- The error page prefers the translation key a page passes as its status message, so a missing product says so instead of the generic 404 copy: `error.contentNotFound`, `error.productNotFound`, `error.categoryNotFound` and `error.contentUnavailable` are translated in both locales.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `/products/khong-ton-tai` and `/products/abc-xyz` → `404` with "Sản phẩm này không tồn tại…" (English with `i18n_redirected=en`), `/categories/khong-ton-tai` → `404` with "Danh mục này không tồn tại…", `/khong-ton-tai` → `404` with the content copy. No regression: `/products/bo-hn-bo`, the real category landing, `/products`, `/categories`, `/gioi-thieu`, `/bai-viet`, `/tag/about`, `/gio-hang`, `/tim-kiem` and `/` all answer `200`.
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the client-side path (clicking a stale link to a deleted product) was not exercised in a browser; only the server-rendered response was.

### WEB-08.h — every sitemap row carries its own freshness (landed 2026-11-11)

- After WEB-08.g the sitemap dated its records and listing pages, but three rows still had no `lastmod`: the home page (seeded by the builder with no timestamp at all), and every tag landing.
- `buildSitemapEntries` now takes an optional `homeLastmod`, so the home row is dated by the caller that knows what the page renders instead of being guessed inside the builder.
- The storefront dates the home page with the newest of its content and catalog feeds, and dates each tag page from one aggregation over published content (`$unwind` tags, `$max` of `updatedAt`), rather than one query per tag. A tag with no published content still gets a row, just undated.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): 303 `<loc>` and 303 `<lastmod>` (was 300 of each, three rows undated); `/` → `2026-11-10T05:07:17.392Z` (newest catalog item, the freshest thing the home page shows), `/tag/about` and `/tag/silver` → `2026-11-07T04:33:14.378Z` (the one published post carrying those tags). A draft post's tags never reach the sitemap.
- `pnpm test:web-site` → 24 checks pass, three of them new (the home row is undated when freshness is unknown, a known home freshness is published, a page listed twice is published once). `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: a `/danh-muc` index row (when content categories exist) still has no `lastmod`, because a content category carries no timestamp; tag freshness is the newest post on the tag, not a per-locale view.

### WEB-02 — a removed path can answer 410 instead of pretending to be new (landed 2026-11-11)

- The redirect map could only move a path (301/302), so a record deleted for good looked identical to a URL that never existed: an editor had no way to say "this is gone", and the storefront always answered 404. Resolving that gap is what WEB-02's "true 404/410" acceptance line was waiting for.
- A map row may now answer `410 Gone` and then carries no target: the contract, the CMS model and the service all accept `410`, and a gone row with a target is refused (`422 REDIRECT_TARGET_NOT_ALLOWED`) instead of silently dropping it. A gone path is never an edge in the loop graph, so it cannot create a chain and a chain that reaches it stops there.
- The storefront middleware flags such a request instead of emitting a Nitro-level error, so the site's own error page renders the status: a global app middleware turns the flag into a real `410` before any page runs, and `error.vue` shows a gone message (`Trang này đã được gỡ` / `This page has been removed`) instead of the 404 or 5xx copy.
- The CMS redirect editor offers `410` and hides the target field for it; the list shows no target rather than an empty arrow.
- Evidence (`:4005`/`:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): creating `{from:'/probe-410-gone', to:'', statusCode:410}` → `201`; `GET /probe-410-gone` → `410` rendering the site error page with the Vietnamese gone copy, and with `i18n_redirected=en` the English one; the same create with a target → `422`; a 301 row still answers `301 Location: /gioi-thieu`; an unknown path still `404`; after deleting the rows and the 30s lookup cache expired, both probe paths answered `404` again. Probe rows and their audit entries were deleted (`content_redirects` 0, `audit_logs` 1).
- `npx tsx scripts/build/test-redirect-service.ts` → 15 checks pass, three of them new (gone row stores no target and resolves as 410; a gone row cannot carry a target; a redirect into a gone path stops at it). `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: a gone row only answers the exact stored path (no wildcard/prefix matching), the `410` label in the CMS editor stays an English literal because the CMS locale files were not part of this change, and the editor was not opened in a browser.

### WEB-08.g — the sitemap lists the listing pages too (landed 2026-11-11)

- The sitemap carried every record URL but not its own index pages: `/bai-viet`, `/products` and `/categories` answered 200 with `index, follow` and were never submitted. They are crawlable URLs in their own right, so a crawler had to find them from navigation alone.
- Each listing row is conditional (an empty listing is not worth a crawl entry, the same rule `/danh-muc` already followed) and carries a `lastmod` taken from the newest record it shows, not from the moment the sitemap was built.
- Catalog category rows previously carried no `lastmod` at all, although the projection already selected `updatedAt`; they now use the record's own timestamp through `toCatalogCategorySitemapEntry`, the same way product rows already did.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): 303 `<loc>` (was 300), 300 `<lastmod>`; `/bai-viet` → `2026-11-07T04:33:14.378Z` (newest published content), `/products` → `2026-11-10T05:07:17.392Z` (newest catalog item, matching the item's own `updatedAt`), `/categories` → `2026-11-10T03:39:47.313Z` (newest catalog category). `/gio-hang` and `/tim-kiem` stay out (noindex / no public content) and no `/pages/` URL appears.
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: home, `/danh-muc` and the tag pages still have no `lastmod` because nothing in their payload carries a timestamp; a tag's freshness would need an aggregate over its content.

### WEB-08.f — share cards are absolute and per record (landed 2026-11-11)

- The storefront published `og:image = /branding/og-cover.png` from every customer head config: a relative path a social crawler cannot fetch, and the same card for every page on the site.
- Product detail now resolves its share image (SEO image, else the first gallery image, else the site card) to an absolute URL, publishes `og:image:alt` / `twitter:image` / `twitter:image:alt` with the product name, and the `Product` JSON-LD `image` list is absolute too. A catalog category landing resolves `category.image` the same way; a content page keeps its absolute SEO image with the page title as alt text.
- The relative default is gone from the three customer head configs and from `scripts/generate-customer.ts`, replaced by one absolute default in `tm-web/app/app.vue` resolved against the site's own origin. Every page inherits it and a record page overrides it, so no page can publish a relative share image again.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `/products`, `/bai-viet`, `/gio-hang`, `/` → `og:image http://localhost:4006/branding/og-cover.png` with the brand as alt; `/products/bo-hn-bo` → its Unsplash image with alt `Bộ HN-BO`; `/categories/chan-ga-goi-dem-bo-chan-ga-bo-thuong` → its Cloudinary image with alt `Bộ thường`; `/gioi-thieu` → the site card with alt `Giới thiệu Sen Tuấn`. Every one of them also carries `twitter:image`.
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: no `og:image:width`/`height` (the stored image's real dimensions are unknown), no generated per-record cards or resizing (that needs a media-rendering service), and a content category landing has no image of its own, so it shares the site card.

### WEB-08.e — the site describes itself (landed 2026-11-11)

- Helper `buildWebSiteJsonLd(baseUrl, { name, language })` in `tm-shared/packages/flow-utils/src/seo.ts` publishes a `WebSite` block with the site name, its home URL and its content language. A site with no name publishes nothing.
- The block carries no `SearchAction`, and that is the decision rather than an omission: `/tim-kiem` is deliberately `noindex, follow` (a search result page is not a page worth indexing), so advertising its URL in structured data would tell a crawler to use a page the same site tells it to ignore. Google also retired the sitelinks search box that consumed this action, so the markup would earn nothing for the contradiction. The search page's own canonical and `noindex` policy are unchanged.
- Wired into the home page next to the `Organization` block it already had, as a separate script with `key: 'ld-website'`, so both blocks survive. The language follows the active UI locale (`vi-VN` / `en-US`).
- Evidence (unit): `pnpm test:web-site` → 21 checks, 3 of them new — the `WebSite` payload with a trimmed name, the home URL and the language; no block without a name; and no `potentialAction`, which is what keeps the markup honest about the `noindex` search page.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `/` → `Organization + WebSite`, name `Sen Tuấn Jewelry & Gemstones`, url `http://localhost:4006/`, `inLanguage: vi-VN` and no `potentialAction`; with the `i18n_redirected=en` cookie the same page reports `inLanguage: en-US`. `/tim-kiem` still answers `noindex, follow`.
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the `WebSite` block is one per language rather than one per site, because the same URL serves both languages (there is no locale prefix to hang `inLanguage` on); there is no `sameAs`/social profile list and no `ContactPoint`.


### WEB-08.d — one public URL per record (landed 2026-11-11)

- Before this slice a record answered on both `/<slug>` and `/pages/<key>`, and each of those URLs declared itself canonical, so the same page was published under two addresses: the listing cards linked the key path, the `ItemList` row copied it, the sitemap listed it, and the detail page's canonical was whichever URL the visitor happened to open.
- The short `/<slug>` form is now the only published address, which matches what a slug rename already does (the old short slug 301s to the new one, while the key path stays reachable as the record's immutable handle):
  - `tm-web/server/models/contentPublic.ts` builds a listing row's `path`, and a new `toPublicContentSitemapEntry`, from the localized slug (`publicContentSlugPath`) instead of the key path.
  - `tm-web/server/utils/publicContent.ts` adds `listContentSitemapEntries`, so `sitemap.xml` publishes slug URLs; the sitemap stays locale-free, preferring the default locale's slug and falling back to any locale that has one.
  - `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/web-base/app/pages/[...slug].vue` canonicalises on the resolved record's slug, so `/pages/<key>` still renders `200` but points its canonical, OG URL, JSON-LD `url`, breadcrumb and `ItemList` at `/<slug>`.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `/gioi-thieu` → `200`, canonical `http://localhost:4006/gioi-thieu`; the same record on `/pages/gioi-thieu` → `200` with the same canonical; `/bai-viet` renders its card as `href="/gioi-thieu"` and its `ItemList` row as `http://localhost:4006/gioi-thieu`; `sitemap.xml` lists `/gioi-thieu` and no `/pages/` URL.
- Suites: `pnpm test:web-site` 18 checks, `pnpm test:web-content` 20 checks, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: the key path is still served (it is the stable handle a redirect row and an external link may point at) but nothing advertises it; a record whose requested locale has no slug is listed in the sitemap under its default-locale slug, so a locale-specific sitemap is still not published.

### WEB-08.b — breadcrumbs as structured data (landed 2026-11-11)

- Helper `buildBreadcrumbJsonLd(baseUrl, entries)` in `tm-shared/packages/flow-utils/src/seo.ts` turns the trail a page already draws into `BreadcrumbList`: an unlabelled step is dropped, positions are assigned after filtering (1..n), fewer than two usable steps returns `null` (a one-step trail describes nothing), and a step that is already an absolute URL is kept as-is instead of being prefixed with the base URL again.
- Wired into the six pages that render a breadcrumb nav: product detail (home → products → primary category when the record has one → product), the catalog category landing, a content page, the tag landing, the content category landing and the content category index. Each block is a separate script with `key: 'ld-breadcrumb'`, so it sits next to the page's own `Product` / `Article` / `WebPage` / `CollectionPage` block instead of replacing it. Nothing is emitted before the record loads, so a 404 or a loading shell carries no trail, and the JSON is `<`-escaped like every other block here.
- Evidence (unit): `pnpm test:web-site` → 15 checks, 3 of them new — absolute `item` URLs with ordered positions, an unlabelled step and a repeated URL dropped with the two-step minimum returning `null`, and an already-absolute step not prefixed twice.
