- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `/products/bo-hn-bo` → `Product + BreadcrumbList` (`Trang chủ` → `Sản phẩm` → `Chăn ga gối đệm` → `Bộ HN-BO`), `/categories/chan-ga-goi-dem-bo-chan-ga-bo-thuong` → `CollectionPage + BreadcrumbList` (3 steps), `/gioi-thieu` → `Article + BreadcrumbList` (home → the page), `/tag/huong-dan` and `/danh-muc` → 3 steps each. The content category landing needs a category this store does not have, so it was checked with one disposable category: `/danh-muc/probe-seo-danh-muc` returned home → `Probe SEO danh mục`, and with the `i18n_redirected=en` cookie `/danh-muc/probe-seo-category` returned `Home` → `Probe SEO category`; the category and its two audit rows were deleted afterwards (`content` 2, `categories` 0, `audit_logs` 1).
- Limits: a page with no visible breadcrumb (`/products`, `/bai-viet`, `/tim-kiem`, `/gio-hang`) emits no trail, and a step is text + URL only (no per-step image, no `datePublished`).

### WEB-08.c — listings as structured data (landed 2026-11-11)

- Helper `buildItemListJsonLd(baseUrl, entries, { name })` in `tm-shared/packages/flow-utils/src/seo.ts` publishes the rows a listing already renders as an `ItemList`: a row without a label or without a link is dropped, a repeated URL is kept once, positions are 1..n, `numberOfItems` matches what was published, and the list stops at 50 rows so a "load more" listing cannot grow the payload without bound. An empty list returns `null`, so nothing publishes an `ItemList` with no rows.
- Wired into seven pages next to the block they already carried: the catalog listing, a catalog category landing, the category index, the post listing, a tag landing, the content category index and a content category landing. Each block is a separate script with `key: 'ld-items'`, so it sits next to `BreadcrumbList`, `CollectionPage`, `Product` and `Article` instead of replacing them.
- Only the view its canonical URL describes is published: the catalog emits nothing for a filtered or paged view (`/products?q=`, `?page=2`), the post listing emits nothing for a search/tag/category view, and a category landing emits nothing past page one, so a crawler never caches a filtered list as the catalog.
- Evidence (unit): `pnpm test:web-site` → 18 checks, 3 of them new — ordered rows with `name`/`numberOfItems`/absolute URLs, unusable rows and a repeated URL dropped with an empty list returning `null`, and the 50-row cap.
- Evidence (HTTP `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): `/products` → `ItemList` 12 rows named `Sản phẩm` with absolute product URLs; `/categories` → 50 rows named `Danh mục` (the 67-category tree is capped); `/bai-viet` → 1 row (`Giới thiệu Sen Tuấn`); `/tag/about` → `BreadcrumbList + ItemList` (1 row); `/categories/chan-ga-goi-dem-bo-chan-ga-bo-thuong` → `CollectionPage + BreadcrumbList + ItemList` (8 rows). `/products?page=2`, `/products?q=bo` and `/tag/huong-dan` (no posts) published no `ItemList`; `/danh-muc` kept only its breadcrumb because this store has no content categories.
- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass for both slices.
- Limits: a row carries a name and a URL only (no price, image or `datePublished`), a category index over 50 categories is truncated rather than paginated, and a content row links the record's stored `/pages/<slug>` path exactly as the card does while the record's own canonical is still the short `/<slug>` form.

### WEB-01 — latest content on the home page (landed 2026-11-11)

- Page: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/web-base/app/pages/index.vue` grew a "Bài viết mới" section between the newest products and the category strip. It renders through the shared `WebContentList` card grid, so the home page shows exactly what `/bai-viet` shows: title, teaser, tags, date and the same link.
- Reader: one `useAsyncData` call to `GET /api/public/content?type=post&locale=<ui locale>&limit=3`, keyed by locale and watched on locale change. No new route, contract or server code — the public reader already enforces published-only, started-window, site scoping and the field allowlist.
- States: the section is rendered only when the reader returned posts, so a store with no published content shows no empty section rather than a placeholder, and a reader failure hides the section instead of printing an error block on a marketing page. Nothing is invented: no counters, no fake "trending"/"sale" labels.
- i18n: `home.posts.{title,subtitle,viewAll}` in both locales, `{name}`-style placeholders only.
- Evidence (`:4006`, `sen-tuan`): with the store's real content the section rendered `Giới thiệu Sen Tuấn` with its `#about`/`#silver` tags and 7/11/2026 date; after publishing four disposable posts the API returned `probe-home-4, probe-home-3, probe-home-2` with `hasMore true` and the home page rendered exactly those three cards (newest first, the fourth absent), and a fifth post left unpublished appeared in neither the API nor the HTML. Probe records, their revisions and audit rows were deleted afterwards (`content` 3, `audit_logs` 1).
- Limits: fixed order (newest published first) and fixed count (3) — there is no per-site configuration for which sections or how many items they show, no featured/trending/sale flag on a record, and the section is not part of the sitemap or the JSON-LD payload. The home page still cannot show catalog items by an editorial flag, only by recency.

### WEB-02 — content category landing 301s (landed 2026-11-17)

- Why: `/danh-muc/<slug>` is a public, indexable landing, but a renamed category slug left the old landing as a 404 while the catalog category and content writers already repaired their own URLs. Reusing a freed slug also kept an older automatic row in place, shadowing the new landing.
- Service: `tm-cms/server/services/contentCategorySlugRedirects.ts` writes one active `301` per changed locale from `/danh-muc/<old>` to `/danh-muc/<new>` with `note: 'auto: content category slug changed'`, through the shared `writeSlugRedirects` upsert. It removes an automatic row under any path that is live after the save, so a new category on a reused slug is reachable. It skips a VI/EN swap (both paths stay live), one old path with two targets, a path another category still owns, a manual row, and a target whose existing chain already leads back to the old path.
- Wiring: `createCategory` (reuse cleanup) and `updateCategory` (rename) take the prepared `Redirect` model from `ContentWriteMeta.redirects`; `POST` and `PATCH /api/v1/admin/categories` pass it and request the storefront purge, so the change applies on the next request instead of after the 30-second lookup cache.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB): `pnpm test:categories` → 15 checks. The two new checks rename a category in both locales and assert the two rows, their `301`, target and auto note, then swap the slugs and assert neither live path has a row; the second creates a category on a reused slug and asserts the old automatic row is gone. `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm verify:all` (37/37) pass.
- Evidence (live `:4005` + `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): create `201` with `x-storefront-revalidate: revalidated`, `/danh-muc/probe-danh-muc-cu` `200`; PATCH to `-moi` `200` + `revalidated`, then `/danh-muc/probe-danh-muc-cu` and `…-cu-en` both `301` to their locale's new path and `/danh-muc/probe-danh-muc-moi` `200`; creating a second category on the freed VI slug returned `revalidated`, `/danh-muc/probe-danh-muc-cu` became `200`, and the only remaining auto row was the EN one. Both categories were deleted through the API, that row and the probe audit rows were removed, and both servers stopped; `categories`, `content_redirects`, `audit_logs` and `idempotency_keys` carried no probe document afterwards.
- Delete: removing a content category now drops every automatic row that leads to or from its landing, so an older URL answers `404` directly instead of `301` into a missing page; a manual row is left alone. The delete route passes the prepared `Redirect` model and requests the storefront purge, which closes the 30-second window where the deleted landing or its 301 was still served. The catalog category soft delete uses the same rule in `syncCatalogCategorySlugRedirects`, and its route already purged.
- Evidence (delete): `pnpm test:categories` → 16 checks and `pnpm test:catalog` → 45 checks, each with a new case that renames a category, adds a manual row into it, deletes the category, and asserts the automatic row is gone while the manual row stays. Live `:4005`/`:4006`: a probe category was renamed (old path `301`), then deleted (`x-storefront-revalidate: revalidated`); the old path answered `404` immediately and `content_redirects` held no probe row; the category, audit rows and idempotency keys were removed and both servers stopped.
- Limits: there is no automatic redirect for the category index or for a subtree move, and no 410 on delete (a removed landing is a plain 404); a manual row that points into a deleted landing still does, because it is an explicit instruction.

### WEB-02 — product landing 301s (landed 2026-11-17)

- Why: `/products/<slug>` is the storefront's main commercial URL, and a renamed product slug left every shared link on a 404. Category and content landings already repaired themselves; the item route did not.
- Service: `tm-cms/server/services/catalogItemSlugRedirects.ts` writes one active `301` per changed locale from `/products/<old>` to `/products/<new>` with `note: 'auto: catalog item slug changed'`, through the shared `writeSlugRedirects` upsert. Only a publicly visible item (`status: published`, active, not deleted — the storefront's own filter) owns a path: a draft writes nothing, and archiving, deleting or deactivating removes the automatic rows that lead to or from its paths, so an older URL does not answer `301` into a hidden page. A manual row stays.
- Guards: a VI/EN swap keeps both paths live; one old path with two targets is skipped; an old path another live item owns is skipped; a previously generated row under a live path is removed so a reused slug is reachable; a target whose manual chain already returns to the old path is not closed into a loop.
- Wiring: `updateCatalogItem` reconciles a rename, `runCatalogItemEvent` handles publish (reuse cleanup) and archive/delete (removal), and the item PATCH and event routes pass the prepared `Redirect` model. Both routes already request the storefront purge, so the map change applies on the next request.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB): `pnpm test:catalog` → 47 checks. Three new checks cover a draft rename with no row, a published two-locale rename with both `301` rows and their auto note followed by an archive that removes them, and a second item published on the freed slug clearing the old automatic row. `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm verify:all` (37/37) pass.
- Evidence (live `:4005` + `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): create `201`, publish `200`, `/products/probe-item-live-cu` `200`; PATCH to `-moi` `200` + `x-storefront-revalidate: revalidated`, old path `301 Location: /products/probe-item-live-moi`, new path `200`, exactly one auto row; a second item on the freed slug published with `revalidated`, the old path `200` and the row gone. The two probe items, their 5 audit rows, and the redirect/idempotency rows were removed; both servers stopped.
- Limits: a product is created as a draft, so no row exists until it is published; archive/delete removes rows and a later restore does not recreate them; bulk price/stock updates never change a slug, and a legacy `fullPath` import still gets no automatic row.

### WEB-02 — hidden records drop their redirects, drafts do not steal them (landed 2026-11-17)

- Why: unpublishing, archiving or deleting a record left its automatic `301` row behind, so a shared URL answered `301` into a page that now 404s. Separately, editing a draft that merely reused an old slug could delete the automatic row an older, still-published record owned, breaking that redirect before the draft ever went live.
- Rule: a write cleans the automatic rows that lead to or from a record's paths only when the record **was public before the write and is hidden after it**. A draft that was never public is a no-op, and a manual row is never touched. This is implemented with `wasPublished` in `syncContentSlugRedirects` and `wasLive` in `syncCatalogItemSlugRedirects`; the content event route now calls the sync for every status change (publish, unpublish, archive, delete, restore), and the catalog item update/event routes pass whether the item was public before the write.
- Evidence (service): `pnpm test:content` → 39 checks (new: editing a draft that reuses an old slug keeps the live row, then publishing it clears the row; unpublishing and deleting each remove the automatic rows into and out of the page while a manual row survives). `pnpm test:catalog` → 47 checks (the reuse case now edits the draft before publishing and asserts the old row is still there). `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm verify:all` (37/37) pass.
- Evidence (live `:4005` + `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): a page was published (`/probe-hide-cu` `200`), renamed (`200` + `revalidated`, old path `301 /probe-hide-moi`), then unpublished (`200` + `revalidated`): both paths answered `404` immediately and `content_redirects` held no probe row. The probe content, its 4 audit rows and its idempotency row were removed; both servers stopped.
- Limits: the cleanup is by path, so it also removes an automatic row whose target merely shares that path; a manual row into a hidden record remains as typed; a later restore or republish does not recreate the removed rows (publishing does clear a reused slug, but it does not restore history).

### WEB-02 — the storefront follows a redirect chain (landed 2026-11-17)

- Why: `findRedirect` said in its own comment that it followed at most five hops, but it answered the first row it found. A mapped path whose target had since received its own `301` therefore cost the browser a second round trip, and a pair of rows pointing at each other (`/a → /b`, `/b → /a`) answered `301` forever — `ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS` instead of a page. The CMS guards a typed map against a cycle, but a row written by an import or by another writer does not go through that guard, so the read side cannot assume the map is acyclic.
- Change: `tm-web/server/utils/publicRedirects.ts` now walks the map, one indexed equality read per hop (`resolveChain`), capped at `MAX_CHAIN = 5`, and keeps the first row's status code (`301`/`302`). A missing or inactive row ends the walk, a `410` still answers gone, and a chain that comes back to the requested path answers `null`, so the page that owns that path renders instead of looping. The 30-second per-database cache stores the resolved chain rather than the first hop, and the CMS purge still clears it.
- Evidence (live `:4005` + `:4006`, real `sen-tuan` data): two manual rows `/probe-chain-x → /probe-chain-y` and `/probe-chain-y → /gioi-thieu` made `GET /probe-chain-x` answer a single `301 Location: /gioi-thieu`, where it used to stop at `/probe-chain-y`; `/probe-chain-y` answered the same target. Two rows pointing at each other, `/probe-loop-a → /probe-loop-b` and `/probe-loop-b → /probe-loop-a`, inserted straight into `content_redirects` because the CMS refuses to create such a pair, made both paths answer `404` from the site's own error page instead of a `301` each. `/`, `/gioi-thieu`, `/products`, `/bai-viet`, `/pages/gioi-thieu`, `/api/public/site` and `/sitemap.xml` all stayed `200`. The probe page was published, renamed and deleted through the API; its document, 4 revisions, 7 audit rows, 5 redirect rows and 5 idempotency keys were removed, leaving `content` 2, `content_revisions` 8, `content_redirects` 0, `audit_logs` 1 and `idempotency_keys` 0 — the pre-probe state — and both servers stopped.
- Limits: a manual row at a live record's own path still wins and can hide that page (the CMS `resolveRedirect` guard keeps the automatic writer from closing such a pair into a loop, so the page is hidden, never looped); a chain longer than five rows ends at an intermediate path, which then answers its own `301`; the 30-second cache still means an unconfigured purge leaves a stale chain briefly.

### WEB-02 — a closed publish window is not a live URL (landed 2026-11-17)

- Why: the automatic writer asked `status === 'published'`, but the storefront hides a published record the moment its `unpublishAt` passes (`isPubliclyVisible`). Renaming a record whose window had already closed therefore wrote a `301` to a page nobody could see, and closing the window of a live page left its old URL pointing `301` into a 404 — the defect the hidden-record slice fixed for an explicit transition, reached by the clock instead.
- Change: `updateContent`, `runContentEvent` and `restoreContentRevision` now pass `isPubliclyVisible(record)` and `isPubliclyVisible(updated)` as `wasPublished`/`published`, so the writer uses the storefront's own definition of a live page instead of the status word. The routes, payloads and the writer itself are unchanged: a record only owns its short paths while someone can open them, which is also the rule the catalog item writer already applies to `status`/`isActive`/`isDeleted`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB): `pnpm test:content` → 41 checks. Two new checks publish a page whose window is already in the past and assert a rename writes no row while the record still reads `published`, then rename a live page (`301` written) and close its window through a normal update, asserting the row is gone.
- Limits: nothing runs when only the clock closes a window, so rows written while the page was live stay until the record is written again or hidden explicitly; a rename after the window closed is a no-op rather than a cleanup, which matches the hidden-record rule that a write cleans only the transition in front of it.

### CRM-01 — customer profiles and segments (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: the CRM row was evidence only. Orders, quotations and the portal all need a customer record that outlives a Hub session, and the legacy schema (`docs/legacy-analysis/crm.md`) had the field list but nobody could create, edit or hide a profile in the new system.
- Model: `tm-cms/server/models/customer.ts` (`crm_customers`) and `customerSegment.ts` (`crm_customer_segments`). New collection names on purpose, like `site_menus`: `customers`/`customer_segments` still hold unmigrated legacy documents that the import slice owns. A customer carries `subjectId` (the Hub user), a unique `code` (`CUS-<8 hex>` when nobody types one), contact fields, a default-first address list, `customerSegmentId`, `loyaltyPoints`, an internal note, `isActive`, `isDeleted`/`deletedAt`, `revision` and the legacy `metrics` counters. Indexes: site+code unique, site+subject unique, site+segment, site+created and a text-ish keyword set for the list.
- Commands: `customerService.ts` and `customerSegmentService.ts` handle create (generated/normalized code, address normalization with exactly one default, segment existence and deletion checks, `CUSTOMER_SUBJECT_EXISTS` per site), update (a changed field merges over the stored one; `subjectId` is immutable, `CUSTOMER_SUBJECT_IMMUTABLE`), soft delete (the profile is hidden, never removed), restore, and a cursor list filtered by keyword, segment and `includeDeleted` with `all/active/inactive/deleted` counts. Every write is audited with actor, request id and the optional operator note; concurrent writers get `CUSTOMER_REVISION_CONFLICT`.
- Segments: code, title, description, default discount, free shipping, thumbnail/gallery, `autoJoinCriteria` (stored only — nothing auto-enrolls), sort and active state. A segment with customers cannot be hidden (`CUSTOMER_SEGMENT_IN_USE`), and a hidden segment cannot receive a customer.
- API + contracts: twelve routes under `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/crm/**` behind `content.read`/`content.write` like the other catalog-domain routes, DTOs and limits in `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/customer-admin.ts`, and `customer`/`customer_segment` added to `AUDIT_RESOURCE_TYPES` so the Audit Log filter and its vi/en labels cover the new writes.
- CMS: `pages/customers.vue` with search, segment filter, "show hidden", cursor "load more", counts, a create/edit dialog (address list with one default, segment picker, loyalty points, note, active switch, audit note), hide/restore with confirmation, and a segment manager for CRUD and hide/restore. `composables/useCrmAdmin.ts` is the client; the nav entry sits next to the other content-domain pages.
- Bug found while testing: `customerSegmentModel` passed `'customer_segments'` as the collection override, which would have written the legacy collection despite the schema declaring `crm_customer_segments`; the override now matches the schema. The service test had been green against the wrong collection.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_crm_service`): `pnpm test:crm` → 11 checks — generated code plus single default address and revision 1, per-site subject uniqueness, normalized explicit code and reuse refusal, missing subject refused, foreign/hidden segment refused, keyword+segment filter with cursor paging and counts, per-site isolation, merged update under optimistic lock without relinking the subject, in-use segment refused then allowed after the move, hide/restore with an audit row, and one audit entry per CRM mutation.
- Evidence (static): `nuxt typecheck` (CMS + layer app) clean, `eslint` clean after removing an unused pattern, `pnpm verify:scaffold` 207/207 (the new `test:crm` gate is declared in `run-gates.ts`), `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43 (the new page is in `WRITE_PAGES`), `verify:modules` 34/34 and `verify:audit-meta` 4/4.
- Limits: `metrics.totalOrders/totalSpent` and `lastOrderAt` exist but no commerce command computes them yet; no import of the 3 legacy customers, no deduplication by email/phone, no customer portal, and the page was typechecked and gated but not clicked through in a browser. Segment discount and free shipping are stored attributes — orders do not read them.
### COM-02 — a quotation can be listed, re-statused and printed (landed 2026-11-23)

- The quotation service and its admin routes shipped on 2026-11-19, but the CMS had no quotation screen at all: the three quotations on `sen-tuan` existed only in the database and the API, so nobody could see what had been offered, mark it accepted or hand a customer a printed sheet.
- `/quotations` lists them with a search box, a status filter and the active/all counts, and each row links to `/quotations/[id]` — a `layout: false` A4 sheet with the customer and address snapshot, the line table (option attributes under the item name), subtotal/shipping/discount/total, notes and the two signature lines. `window.print()` prints the sheet alone; the toolbar and the navigation are `print:hidden`.
- The sheet's toolbar also changes the status through the existing `PATCH /api/v1/admin/commerce/quotations/:id` with the record's `revision`, so a stale tab cannot overwrite someone else's edit. A navigation entry appears next to Orders only when the orders module is enabled and the user may read content.
- Evidence (`HTTP :4005`, mock auth, real `sen-tuan` data): the list answers `success: true` with 3 rows and `counts {all: 3, active: 3}`; the first quotation (`QUO-2611-0013`, `sent`, 375000 VND, 3 lines) PATCHed to `draft` (revision 2) and back to `sent`; `/quotations` and `/quotations/<id>` answer `200`. `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
- Limits: no quotation create/edit screen yet (the API supports it; the UI does not), the printed page was not opened in a browser or sent to a printer, and the status round-trip bumped that quotation's revision from 1 to 3 (the status is back to `sent`).

### COM-01 / COM-02 — orders and quotations (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: the CRM slice gave orders a customer to point at, but no order or quotation could be created, so the metrics writer and the whole admin order surface were unreachable.
- Model: `tm-cms/server/models/order.ts` (`crm_orders`) and `quotation.ts` (`crm_quotations`) — new collection names again, because `orders`/`quotations` still hold unmigrated legacy documents. An order keeps `code` (`ORD-YYMMDD-xxxxxx` when nobody types one), customer reference, channel (`admin|portal|pos`), status (`pending|processing|shipped|completed|cancelled`), `paymentStatus`/`paymentMethod`/`currency`, line snapshots (name, sku, attributes, price, quantity, thumbnail, `returnedQuantity`), `itemsPrice`/`shippingFee`/`discountAmount`/`totalAmount`/`paidAmount`, an address snapshot, `warehouseId`, a status `history` array, soft delete and `revision`. Unique site+code, plus site+customer, site+status and site+deleted indexes.
- Commands: `orderService.ts` derives `itemsPrice` and `totalAmount` server-side from the lines (a submitted total is ignored), derives `paymentStatus` from `paidAmount` against that computed total, appends a history entry on every status change, refuses item edits once the order is `shipped`/`completed`/`cancelled` (`ORDER_LOCKED`), rejects a duplicate code (`ORDER_CODE_EXISTS`), and bumps the customer's `metrics.totalOrders/totalSpent/lastOrderAt` best-effort after a create. `quotationService.ts` mirrors the shape with `QUO-` codes, an expiry field, a status set that includes `accepted`/`expired`, and `convertToOrder`, which creates exactly one order per quotation — the second call returns the existing order instead of a duplicate, and conversion is blocked unless the quotation is `accepted`.
- API + contracts: fifteen routes under `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/commerce/**` (`orders` and `quotations` list/get/create/patch/soft-delete/restore, per-record history, plus `POST /quotations/:id/convert`) behind `content.read`/`content.write`, every mutation idempotent through `executeIdempotent` and audited. Query/payload schemas live beside each route (like the inventory and procurement slices) rather than in a new shared contract package; `order` and `quotation` were added to `AUDIT_RESOURCE_TYPES` so the Audit Log filter covers them.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_commerce_service`): `pnpm test:commerce` → 18 checks (totals recomputed from lines, derived payment status, duplicate code conflict, empty-lines refusal, site isolation, merged update under the optimistic lock, item lock after shipping, history growth, exactly-once conversion, quotation expiry refusal, soft delete/restore, one audit row per mutation).
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 208/208, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:error-answers` 85/85.
- Limits: no POS screen, no stock reservation on create or release on cancel (the ledger exists for that; the order path does not call it yet), no shipments/returns, no payment records or allocations, and the routes are gated by tests but were not exercised in a browser.

### INV-01 — warehouses, balances and the movement ledger (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: purchasing and orders both need somewhere to put stock. Legacy's transfer path wrote random reference ids into a collection with no model (`docs/legacy-analysis/inventory.md` §4), so the ledger was rebuilt rather than ported.
- Model: `warehouse.ts` (`crm_warehouses`), `inventory.ts` (`crm_inventories`), `inventoryTransaction.ts` (`crm_inventory_transactions`). A balance is one document per `(itemId, variantId, warehouseId)` under a unique index, with `quantityOnHand`, `quantityReserved`, `binLocation`, `lowStockThreshold`, soft delete and `revision`. A ledger row carries `code` (`TX-YYMMDD-xxxxxx`), the balance reference, item/variant/warehouse, `type` (`import|export|return_in|return_out|transfer_in|transfer_out|adjustment_inc|adjustment_dec`), `quantityChange`, `previousQuantity`, `newQuantity`, `unitCost`, `totalValue`, `imeiCodes`, `referenceId`/`referenceCode`/`referenceModel` (`PurchaseOrder|Order|InventoryTransfer|InventoryAdjustment|Other|None`), `reason` and `performerId` — the legacy field list, with the reference now constrained to a real enum instead of a free string.
- Commands: `warehouseService.ts` (CRUD, soft delete/restore, site-scoped unique code), `inventoryService.ts` (balance CRUD, `adjustInventoryStock`, `reserveInventoryStock`, `releaseInventoryStock`) and `inventoryTransactionService.ts` (`recordInventoryTransaction` writes the movement **and** moves `quantityOnHand` under the balance's revision, refusing a move that would go negative).
- Why conditional updates: reservation and adjustment conditions are evaluated by the database in one operation (`$expr` on `quantityOnHand - quantityReserved` for a reservation, `quantityOnHand >= -delta` for a decrement), so two concurrent writers cannot both pass the check — that is what makes "concurrent reservations cannot oversell" measurable without a transaction.
- Evidence (service, disposable DBs `tm_test_inventory_service` and `tm_test_inventory_transaction_service`): `pnpm test:inventory` → 17 checks and `pnpm test:inventory-transactions` → 8 checks (a ledger write moves the balance, a zero-quantity movement is refused, an over-large export is refused, an item/warehouse pair resolves the balance without an id, type and reference filters with cursor paging, site isolation, audit rows).
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 211/211, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:error-answers` 110/110.
- Limits: no transfers, no stocktake/stock-count model, no serial/IMEI writer (the field exists on the ledger row; nothing allocates serials), an order create/cancel does not move stock yet, and there is no low-stock report or alert pipeline.

### PUR-01 — suppliers, purchase orders and partial receipt (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: the purchase side had no owner in the new system, and the acceptance criterion that matters — "receipt cannot exceed order policy; stock and payables reconcile" — needs both a receipt command and a stock writer.
- Model: `supplier.ts` (`crm_suppliers`) and `purchaseOrder.ts` (`crm_purchase_orders`). A supplier keeps code/title/contact/status/rating/`itemsSupplied`/avatar/note, soft delete and `revision`. A purchase order keeps `code` (`PO-YYMMDD-xxxxxx` when generated), supplier and warehouse references, `status` (`draft|ordered|partial|received|cancelled`), `paymentStatus` derived from `paidAmount`, lines with `receivedQuantity`/`rejectedQuantity`/`note`, `totalAmount`/`orderedTotalAmount`, `expectedDeliveryDate` and `reconcileNote`.
- Receipt: `receivePurchaseOrder` takes **cumulative** totals per line, not deltas, so replaying the same receipt moves no stock instead of booking it twice. It refuses a line the order does not contain (`PURCHASE_ORDER_RECEIPT_ITEM_UNKNOWN`), `received + rejected > ordered` (`PURCHASE_ORDER_RECEIPT_EXCEEDS_ORDER`) and a total that goes backwards (`PURCHASE_ORDER_RECEIPT_REGRESSION`). Each line's delta becomes one `import` ledger row against the order's warehouse with `referenceModel: 'PurchaseOrder'` and the order id/code, so a movement is traceable back to the order. Status derives to `partial` while anything is outstanding and `received` once every line is received or rejected, after which the order refuses further receipts (`PURCHASE_ORDER_LOCKED`).
- Ordering: stock moves **before** the order document is updated, so a failed stock write leaves the order untouched rather than claiming goods that never arrived; an order with no warehouse, or a received line with no item id, is refused explicitly (`PURCHASE_ORDER_RECEIPT_TARGET_MISSING`) instead of silently skipping the movement.
- API + contracts: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/procurement/**` — five supplier routes and seven purchase-order routes (list/get/create/patch/soft-delete/restore plus `POST /purchase-orders/:id/receive`), all behind `content.read`/`content.write`, idempotent, audited with actor/request id/reason. `supplier`, `purchase_order` and `inventory_transaction` added to `AUDIT_RESOURCE_TYPES` and the admin audit contract.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_procurement_service`): `pnpm test:procurement` → 24 checks — supplier CRUD/isolation/merge/soft delete; purchase-order totals, duplicate code, unknown supplier, status and payment derivation, item lock, merged update; and the receipt block: a partial receipt moves stock and marks the order `partial`, an over-receipt is refused, a replayed receipt moves no stock, a lowered total is refused, completing the receipt books the remainder and closes the order, and a closed order refuses another receipt. One audit row per mutation.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 211/211, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:error-answers` 110/110.
- Limits: a rejection creates no payable or credit note and nothing posts to finance, supplier price history is now read from the stock ledger (per receipt, dated, weighted average, variant-aware), and there is no supplier portal. A receipt is not wrapped in a database transaction, so a failed order update after a successful stock move would leave stock ahead of the order — that order was chosen deliberately (over-claiming stock is worse than under-recording an order), and closing the window needs a replica-set session test.

### PUR-01 — purchase-order supplier backfill, by reviewed map (2026-11-26)

- Why: the CRM import wrote 6 purchase orders with `supplierId: null` because the legacy ids they name (`69c4b2b8…` for 0001–0004, `6a1b8d43…` for 0005, and `6a1c072c…` for 0006, found in that order's payment) exist in no collection of any local database. Refusing to invent one was correct, but nothing could close the gap afterwards.
- Command: `pnpm backfill:po-suppliers --site <id> --map <file> [--apply] [--overwrite]` (`scripts/ops/backfill-po-suppliers.ts`, planner/applier in `tm-cms/server/services/purchaseOrderSupplierBackfill.ts`). The map is `{ "<PO code>": "<supplier code or ObjectId>" }`; format and draft live in `scripts/ops/po-supplier-map.README.md` and `po-supplier-map.sen-tuan.draft.json`. Codes are case-insensitive, a blank value means unmapped, and the command is a dry run unless `--apply`.
- Safety: an order that already names a supplier is kept unless `--overwrite`; an unknown purchase order, an unknown supplier or a soft-deleted supplier invalidates the whole plan and nothing is written; the write sets `supplierId` and bumps `revision`, guarded on the previous supplier so a concurrent CMS edit is skipped and reported; a rerun plans every written order as `already-assigned` and writes nothing.
- Tests: `pnpm test:po-supplier-backfill` → 10 checks on the disposable `tm_test_po_supplier_backfill` database (map normalization, code/id resolution, keep/overwrite, unmapped, unknown and deleted supplier refused, apply bumps the revision, rerun no-op, concurrent change skipped). The suite is wired into `verify:all`.
- Dry run on `sen-tuan` (2026-11-26): 6 orders listed, 0 filled entries, each row naming the dangling legacy supplier id; `planned writes 0; nothing was written`. The backfill is **blocked on the owner's map**: the three migrated suppliers are still placeholders (`SUP-SUPPLIER1..3`, "Supplier 01..03"), so which one bought a given order is a business decision, not something the data states.

### FIN-01 — posted payments against orders and purchase orders (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: orders and purchase orders both carried `paidAmount` and a derived `paymentStatus`, but the only writer was an operator editing the field by hand, so nothing tied money to a document. The acceptance line for `PUR-01` — "stock and payables reconcile" — could not be met without a payment record.
- Model: `tm-cms/server/models/payment.ts` (`crm_payments`). A row keeps `code` (`PAY-YYMMDD-xxxxxx` when nobody types one), `direction` (`receivable` for an order, `payable` for a purchase order), `referenceModel`/`referenceId`/`referenceCode`, `partyId` (the customer or supplier, copied from the document at posting time), `amount`, `currency`, `method` (`cash|banking|cod|card|other`), `state` (`posted|voided`), `paidAt`, `performerId`, `voidedAt`/`voidedBy`/`voidReason` and `revision`. Indexes: site+code unique, site+reference, site+direction+state+paidAt, site+party.
- One writer for the balance: `recordPayment` updates the document with an aggregation pipeline (`{ $set: { paidAmount: { $add: ['$paidAmount', amount] } } }`, then `paymentStatus` recomputed from the new `paidAmount` in the same stage) under a `$expr` guard that the outstanding balance still covers the amount. Read-then-write would leave a window; here the server decides and the database enforces in one operation.
- Refusals: an amount at or below zero (`PAYMENT_AMOUNT_INVALID`), a direction that does not match the document kind (`PAYMENT_DIRECTION_MISMATCH`), an unknown or deleted document (`PAYMENT_REFERENCE_NOT_FOUND`/`PAYMENT_REFERENCE_DELETED`), a cancelled or refunded document (`PAYMENT_REFERENCE_CLOSED`), an amount above the outstanding balance (`PAYMENT_EXCEEDS_OUTSTANDING`, with the balance in `details`), and a document that moved underneath the write (`PAYMENT_REFERENCE_CONFLICT`).
- Rollback: if the document balance moved but the payment row could not be stored, the balance is reversed with a compensating pipeline under the revision the first write returned. A failed reversal raises `PAYMENT_ROLLBACK_FAILED` with the reference and amount instead of pretending the payment never happened — the one case where this slice would need an operator.
- Reversal: `voidPayment` keeps the row (`state: 'voided'`, reason, who and when) and takes the amount back off the document under a `$paidAmount >= amount` guard, recomputing `paymentStatus` the same way, so the payment list and the document tell the same story. Voiding twice is refused (`PAYMENT_ALREADY_VOIDED`), as is voiding after the document no longer holds the amount (`PAYMENT_VOID_UNBALANCED`). Nothing is deleted.
- API: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/finance/payments` — list (filters for direction, reference, party and state, cursor paging, counts including the posted total), get, `POST` to post a payment and `POST /:id/void` to reverse it. All behind `content.read`/`content.write`, mutations same-origin, idempotent and audited with actor/request id/reason. `payment` was added to `AUDIT_RESOURCE_TYPES` and the admin audit contract, and the audit log now labels every resource type the CRM, commerce, inventory, purchasing and finance slices introduced (`order`, `quotation`, `warehouse`, `inventory`, `inventory_transaction`, `supplier`, `purchase_order`, `payment`) in both locales — those labels were missing.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_finance_service`): `pnpm test:finance` → 12 checks — a partial payment sets `partial` and moves the balance, an overpayment is refused and moves nothing, a mismatched direction is refused, the remaining balance settles the document, a zero amount is refused, an unknown reference is refused, a void restores the previous balance and status, a second void is refused, rows are site-scoped, the list filters/pages/counts posted money, a payable payment settles a purchase order against the supplier, and every mutation leaves an audit row.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 212/212 (`test:finance` declared in `run-gates.ts`), `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:error-answers` 114/114, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43.
- Limits: no card/bank gateway (a payment is a manual record of money that already moved), no allocation of one payment across several documents and no partial refunds — the `refunded` status stays a manual field, no receivable/payable ageing report, no currency conversion (a document and its payments are assumed to share the `currency` on the payment row), and no CMS screen or portal surface: the routes exist and are gated but were not exercised in a browser.
### INV-02 — warehouse transfers through the movement ledger (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: balances and a ledger existed, but stock could only appear or disappear in one warehouse. Every real warehouse needs to move it between two, and the acceptance row asks for "atomic transfer or tested compensation; audit trail" — legacy wrote a transfer row with a dangling random reference id and no model, so there was nothing to port.
- Model: `tm-cms/server/models/inventoryTransfer.ts` (`crm_inventory_transfers`). A transfer keeps `code` (`TRF-YYMMDD-xxxxxx`), `fromWarehouseId`/`toWarehouseId`, `status` (`draft|in_transit|received|cancelled`), lines (`itemId`, `variantId`, `sku`, `itemName`, `quantity`, cumulative `receivedQuantity`), `note`, `shippedAt`/`receivedAt`/`cancelledAt`/`cancelReason`, soft delete and `revision`. Unique site+code, site+status and site+deleted indexes.
- States are where stock is: `draft` moves nothing, `ship` debits the source with one `transfer_out` ledger row per line and turns the document `in_transit`, `receive` credits the destination with `transfer_in` rows for the delta of each cumulative total, and the document becomes `received` only when every line is fully received. A partial receipt keeps it `in_transit`, so the outstanding quantity is always visible as `quantity - receivedQuantity`.
- Compensation: a shipment that fails on any line puts back the lines that already left (as `return_in` at the source) before refusing, so a refused shipment cannot strand stock in transit. A cancellation of an in-transit transfer returns exactly what has not arrived — the part already received legitimately stays at the destination — and a received transfer refuses cancellation instead of silently reversing a delivery.
- Destination balance: receiving into a warehouse that has never held the item creates the zero balance first (`ensureBalance`), including the read-after-insert race when two receipts run at once. Without that, the first ever transfer into a new warehouse would fail on a missing row.
- Guards: source and destination must differ (`INVENTORY_TRANSFER_SAME_WAREHOUSE`), both must exist, be active and not deleted, one item cannot appear twice on one transfer, an over-receipt is refused (`INVENTORY_TRANSFER_RECEIPT_EXCEEDS_TRANSFER`), a receipt total cannot be lowered, an in-transit transfer cannot be hidden (`INVENTORY_TRANSFER_IN_FLIGHT`), and every state change runs under the document revision, so two operators cannot ship or cancel the same transfer.
- API: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/inventory/transfers` — list (status, warehouse and keyword filters, cursor paging, per-state counts), get, create, soft delete, restore, and `POST /:id/ship`, `/:id/receive`, `/:id/cancel`. All behind `content.read`/`content.write`, same-origin mutations, idempotent and audited. `inventory_transfer` was added to `AUDIT_RESOURCE_TYPES`, the admin audit contract and both locale label sets.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_inventory_transfer_service`): `pnpm test:inventory-transfers` → 13 checks — a draft holds no stock, same-warehouse and duplicate-line and unknown-warehouse refusals, shipping debits only the source, a too-large shipment is refused and leaves the draft and the source untouched, receiving credits the destination and creates its balance with a ledger that nets to zero, a received transfer refuses a second receipt, a partial receipt stays in transit, cancelling returns the undelivered remainder while the received part stays, an in-transit transfer cannot be hidden while a cancelled one can be hidden and restored, site scoping plus filters/paging/counts, and audit rows for create/ship/receive/cancel/delete/restore.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 213/213 (`test:inventory-transfers` declared in `run-gates.ts`), `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:error-answers` 122/122, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43.
- Limits: no approval workflow, no reservation of the shipped quantity while in transit (the source is debited immediately), no damaged/lost bucket on receipt (a partial receipt is the only outcome), no stocktake/stock-count model, no serial/IMEI allocation, and no CMS screen — the routes are gated by tests but were not exercised in a browser. A transfer spanning more lines than a single request is not chunked, and cancelling an in-transit transfer whose source balance has since been deleted fails loudly rather than recreating it.
### MSG-01 — a recipient-owned inbox with idempotent delivery (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: order/payment/stock events had nowhere to land. Legacy had `Notification { recipientId, taskId, isRead }` in the CMS database with no endpoint that named the recipient, no unsubscribe and no duplicate protection, and the parity row asks for exactly those four things: recipient ownership, unsubscribe, idempotent send, and permission tests.
- Model: `tm-cms/server/models/notification.ts` (`crm_notifications`) — `recipientId` is a **Hub subject id**, plus `type` (`order.paid`, `stock.low`, …), `title`, plain-text `body`, site-relative `link`, `severity`, small `data`, `status` (`unread|read|archived`), `readAt`/`archivedAt` and `dedupeKey`. A partial unique index on `(siteId, recipientId, dedupeKey)` makes a retried dispatch a no-op; the inbox index is `(siteId, recipientId, status, createdAt desc)`.
- Ownership: every inbox function takes the recipient as an argument that the route fills from the verified session (`user.id`), and every query filters on it. A notification belonging to someone else answers `404 NOTIFICATION_NOT_FOUND` — a `403` would confirm the id exists in another person's inbox. The one function that accepts a caller-supplied recipient is `dispatchNotification`, and its route requires `content.write`.
- Idempotency: `dedupeKey` is optional (most notifications have no natural key). A retry re-inserts, the unique index refuses, and the service returns the original row with `created: false`; there is no read-then-write window to race.
- Read state: mark-read only matches an `unread` row, so marking twice keeps the first `readAt` instead of moving it. Mark-all-read is scoped to the caller and can be narrowed to one type. Archiving hides a row from the default list; `status=archived` is an explicit view, never "everything including archived".
- Unsubscribe: `crm_notification_subscriptions` stores **mutes only** — a missing row means subscribed, so a new notification type cannot silently arrive disabled for everyone and "who opted out" is the whole collection. Re-enabling deletes the row. Dispatch checks the mute before inserting and answers `created: false, skipped: 'muted'` without storing anything; the operator still gets an audit row saying the delivery was suppressed, which is the difference between "we did not send it" and "it vanished".
- Content safety: `body` is stored as plain text with control characters stripped (newlines kept), and `link` must be a site-relative path — an absolute or protocol-relative URL is refused (`NOTIFICATION_LINK_INVALID`), so a notification cannot become an open redirect. Legacy interpolated customer input into mail HTML; nothing here is rendered as markup.
- Audit: dispatch (including a suppressed one) and preference changes are audited as `notification` / `notification_subscription`. Mark-read and archive are **not** audited: they are per-recipient UI state with no business consequence, and auditing every open would bury the trail; the document already records `readAt`/`archivedAt`. The gate asserts both halves of that decision, including that a read/archive adds no audit row.
- API: `GET /api/v1/me/notifications` (status/type filters, cursor paging, per-status counts), `GET /:id`, `POST /:id/read`, `POST /read-all`, `POST /:id/archive`, `GET /api/v1/me/notification-subscriptions`, `PUT /api/v1/me/notification-subscriptions/:type`, and `POST /api/v1/admin/notifications` for an operator dispatch (idempotent by `Idempotency-Key`, `content.write`).
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_notification_service`): `pnpm test:notifications` → 15 checks — dispatch lands unread with data, a repeated dedupe key returns the first row and stores nothing new, a second recipient gets their own copy of the same event, absolute/protocol-relative/`javascript:` links are refused, empty titles and oversized data are refused, one recipient cannot read/mark/archive another's row (and the owner's row stays unread), mark-read is idempotent and keeps the first timestamp, mark-all-read scopes to the caller and honours a type filter, archived rows leave the inbox and the archived view is explicit, a muted type is skipped without storing anything while other types still arrive, re-enabling deletes the mute and delivery resumes, invalid types/states are refused, cursor paging does not overlap and is newest-first, notifications are site-scoped, and the audit assertions above.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 214/214 (`test:notifications` declared in `run-gates.ts`), `verify:error-answers` 130/130, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43.
- Limits: no UI (no bell, badge or inbox page) — the routes are gated by the service test only; no push/webpush subscription records and no task/assignment records (the MSG-01 wording still lists both); no retention/auto-archive job (Hub's 14-day notification retention was not ported); no digest/batching; a muted type is silent with no "there were 3 suppressed" counter; and dispatch is per-recipient, so a broadcast to every customer is a caller loop, not one idempotent operation.
### MSG-02 — mail templates and a queue that does not lie about sending (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: the CMS could not send a single business email from its own database. Legacy kept templates in Hub settings and interpolated request input into HTML (the contact-form XSS in the legacy analysis), claimed a send with nothing stored, and had no retry, no delivery state and no way to see a failure. The parity row asks for local template/source ownership, a provider adapter, consent/anti-abuse and replay-safe delivery.
- Templates: `tm-cms/server/models/mailTemplate.ts` (`crm_mail_templates`) keyed by `(siteId, code, locale)`, with `subject`/`bodyText`/`bodyHtml`, a declared `variables` list, `purpose` (`transactional|marketing`), `status` (`draft|active|archived`), soft delete and `revision`. Writing a template refuses an empty body (`MAIL_TEMPLATE_BODY_REQUIRED`) and a placeholder it never declared (`MAIL_TEMPLATE_VARIABLE_UNDECLARED`) — the second defect is what made legacy mail render `{{name}}` or fail at send time.
- Rendering: one substitution pass, `{{name}}` only, no loops or expressions. `bodyText` gets the raw value; `bodyHtml` gets the value HTML-escaped, so a customer-supplied name can never become markup. A template has both representations because a text client and an HTML client need different bodies from the same data.
- Queue: `crm_mail_deliveries` snapshots the rendered subject and both bodies at queue time. A later template edit cannot rewrite a queued message, and the delivery stays readable after the template is archived. `status` is `queued → sending → sent`, or back to `queued` with `attempts`/`lastError`/`nextAttemptAt` until `failed`, plus `cancelled`.
- Replay safety: `sendDelivery` **claims** the row with a conditional update before handing it to a provider, so two workers cannot send the same message (`MAIL_DELIVERY_IN_FLIGHT`) and a crash mid-send consumes an attempt rather than retrying forever. Queueing is deduped per `(siteId, to, dedupeKey)` — the recipient is part of the key because one event addressed to two people is two emails, not one.
- Provider boundary: `mailTransport.ts` defines the interface and `resolveMailTransport()` reads `TM_MAIL_PROVIDER`. Only `console` exists (logs the fact of a send, masked address, never the body). With no provider configured, a send answers `503 MAIL_PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED` and the message stays queued with `attempts: 0` — the honest alternative to legacy's silent success. Credentials for a real provider belong in the deployment's secret store, never in Mongo (the `Connect.credentials` defect).
- Retry: provider failures back off (1 min doubling, capped at 1 h) until `maxAttempts`, then `failed`; a non-retryable `MailTransportError` fails at once; a manual retry resets the allowance and requeues. `POST /deliveries/drain` sends at most 25 due messages and reports `{sent, failed, requeued, processed, provider}` — there is no scheduler in the CMS, so an operator or an external cron drains the queue.
- Consent/anti-abuse: a `marketing` template requires `consent: true` from the caller (`MAIL_CONSENT_REQUIRED`), which is an assertion, not a consent registry — enough to make an accidental promotional blast impossible, and the honest limit of this slice. Transactional mail about a person's own order does not need it.
- Audit: template create/update/delete/restore and every delivery state change (queue, sent, failed/requeued, cancelled, retry) are audited as `mail_template` / `mail_delivery`.
- API: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/mail` — templates list/create/get/patch/soft-delete/restore, deliveries list/get, `POST /deliveries` (queue, idempotent by `Idempotency-Key`), `POST /deliveries/:id/send`, `/:id/retry`, `/:id/cancel`, `POST /deliveries/drain`. All behind `content.read`/`content.write`, same-origin mutations.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_mail_service`): `pnpm test:mail` → 17 checks — template creation with declared variables and counts, empty body/undeclared variable/duplicate code refused, revision bump with stale-revision conflict, queue renders and snapshots (text raw, HTML escaped, address lowercased), same recipient+key queues once while two recipients get two emails, draft template / missing variable / bad address refused, marketing needs consent, no provider leaves the queue untouched without burning an attempt, provider failure counts an attempt with error and backoff and refuses an early resend, non-retryable error fails at once, exhausted allowance ends `failed` and a manual retry sends, cancel only from `queued` and retry requeues, drain sends due mail and reports outcomes while refusing without a provider, site scoping, soft delete/restore leaving queued mail alone, paging and per-state counts, and audit rows for both resources.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 215/215 (`test:mail` declared in `run-gates.ts`), `verify:error-answers` 143/143, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43.
- Limits: no real provider adapter (only `console`), no CMS screen for templates or deliveries, no scheduler (drain is manual/cron), no attachments, no delivery/open analytics, no contact form and no newsletter subscription/consent registry, no per-site send rate limit, and `console` is the only built-in transport even in production — a deployment that sets any other value gets queued mail and a 503, which is deliberate.
### HR-01 — a payroll that can explain every number (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: legacy had staff lists and salary read-only views, no shift model, no attendance or payroll write path, no salary→payroll calculation, and `markPayrollAsPaid` with no endpoint calling it. A payroll figure existed with nothing behind it, so nobody could answer "why is this month's pay this number?" The parity row asks for period/state/approval logic, a repeatable calculation, compensation privacy and adjustment audit.
- Staff: `crm_staff_profiles` keyed by `(siteId, subjectId)` (Hub owns identity) with a unique `employeeCode` per site, position, work status (`probation|official|resigned`), join/leave dates and soft delete. An update refuses a payload that tries to move `subjectId` to another Hub account.
- Compensation privacy: salary is **not** a staff field. It lives in `crm_salary_configs` (effective-dated `baseSalary`, itemised allowances, `standardWorkDays`), and salary/payroll routes require `content.write` rather than `content.read` — a read-only contributor can run the directory and attendance without seeing anyone's pay. A staff list shape has no amount fields at all. When Hub's capability vocabulary grows `hr.compensation.read`, it replaces that gate.
- Attendance: `crm_attendances` with a unique `(siteId, staffId, date)`; posting the same day again is a correction (200, same row), because two rows for one day would be paid twice. Refused: an impossible calendar date (`2026-02-31`), check-out before check-in, and check-in times on a leave/absent day. A wrong day is **hard** deleted with an audit row — payroll counts rows, so a hidden row would still be paid.
- Calculation: `dailyRate = baseSalary / standardWorkDays` (two decimals), `basePay = dailyRate × paid days` rounded to đồng, plus monthly allowances and itemised adjustments (bonus/fine, signed). `present` and `late` are paid; `leave` and `absent` are counted but not paid. A result below zero is refused (`PAYROLL_NET_NEGATIVE`) instead of paying a negative.
- The rate is taken from the config in force on the **last day of the period**; a raise effective mid-month therefore applies to the whole month. That is a stated choice, not an accident, and the row stores the config id and `baseSalary` it used, so a later change cannot rewrite history.
- Repeatable: the row stores its inputs and outputs (config, `standardWorkDays`, `baseSalary`, `dailyRate`, paid/leave/absent days, `basePay`, allowances, adjustments, `netPay`). Recalculating identical inputs returns `unchanged` and writes nothing; changed inputs update a `draft` and bump `revision`; an approved, paid or cancelled row is reported as `locked` and never touched. The batch call calculates a whole period and **skips** staff with no salary config (`reason: SALARY_CONFIG_MISSING`) instead of failing the period.
- States: `draft → approved → paid`, plus `draft|approved → cancelled` with a required reason. Paying a draft is refused (`PAYROLL_NOT_APPROVED`), re-approving is refused (`PAYROLL_NOT_DRAFT`), a paid row is immutable (`PAYROLL_LOCKED`), and every transition needs the current `revision`.
- Audit: staff create/update/delete/restore, attendance record/correct/delete, salary config writes and payroll create/update/approve/pay/cancel. `approve`, `pay` and `cancel` were added to the shared audit-action vocabulary (audit model, admin and content contracts, vi/en labels) so a money trail reads in its own terms instead of three generic `update` rows.
- API: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/hr` — staff list/create/get/patch/soft-delete/restore, attendance list/upsert/delete, `salary-configs` list by staff + write, payroll list/calculate/get/approve/pay/cancel. Same-origin mutations, idempotent writes, `content.read`/`content.write` as noted.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_hr_service`): `pnpm test:hr` → 20 checks — profile without compensation and counts, duplicate code/subject refused, revision-guarded update that cannot move the Hub subject, soft delete/restore, one-row-per-day correction, impossible date/time/leave refusals, hard delete of a wrong day, cross-site refusal, effective-dated salary with same-date correction, the full 21-paid-day calculation (1,000,000/day → 21,000,000 + 1,000,000 allowance + 500,000 adjustment = 22,500,000), unchanged recalculation, changed-day recalculation bumping the revision, negative-net refusal, approval freezing numbers while a recalculation reports `locked`, pay/approve state guards and revision conflicts, a later salary change not rewriting a paid month, cancel with a reason and locked cancelled rows, batch skip without a config, totals/site scoping, and audit rows including `approve`/`pay`/`cancel`.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 216/216 (`test:hr` declared in `run-gates.ts`), `verify:error-answers` 160/160, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43.
- Limits: no shift scheduling, no attendance self-service or device import, no overtime/holiday rates, no insurance/tax withholding (adjustments stand in for them), no payslip document, no bulk approve/pay, no CMS screen for any of it, and no dedicated `hr.*` capability yet (compensation rides on `content.write`).
### FIN-02 — finance reports that cannot disagree with the ledger (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: payments, orders and purchase orders existed with no way to answer "how much came in this month", "who still owes us", "how old is that debt". Legacy had report screens fed by whatever query each page happened to run. The parity row asks for fixture totals that reconcile to the ledger, correct date/timezone boundaries, and export access and redaction.
- Nothing is stored: every report is a read of `crm_payments`, orders and purchase orders, so a report cannot drift from the documents it summarizes. `financeReportService.ts` exports `financeSummary`, `financeAging`, `financeLedger` and `exportLedgerCsv`.
- Money in/out: posted payments in the range split by direction and method, `net = received - paidOut`. A **voided** payment stays counted in a separate `voided` block and never enters received or paidOut — the reversal is visible, but it is not money moved.
- Outstanding: `totalAmount - paidAmount` for orders that are not deleted, not cancelled, and still owe; purchase orders the same but also excluding `draft` (an unplaced order is not a payable). Cancelled and soft-deleted documents never appear. Outstanding is "as of now" and the field names say so: a report of last month still has to say who owes money today. `overdueCount` uses 30 days from the document date.
- Time zone: calendar boundaries are the customer's days, `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh` at a fixed +07:00 (Vietnam has had no DST since 1975, so this is exact rather than an approximation). A payment at 23:30 local on 31 October belongs to October even though it is 16:30Z; 00:30 local on 1 November is the next month. Ranges are inclusive of both days, capped at 366 days, refused when reversed or starting in the future, and validated as real calendar days (`2026-02-31` refused).
- Aging: unpaid balances bucketed `current / 1-30 / 31-60 / 61-90 / 90+` by local days since the document date. Age is measured on local calendar days, so a document created at 23:00 is not a day older two hours later. Bucket sums always equal the total.
- Ledger: newest payment date first, cursor-paged. The cursor carries both the payment instant and the row id, because ordering by id alone would skip rows when two payments share a timestamp. Voided rows are listed with `state: "voided"`, and the totals block separates them.
- Export: a CSV of at most 5,000 rows with an explicit column allowlist (day, code, direction, method, amount, state, reference code) — no internal ids, no actor ids, no contact data. Every cell is quoted/escaped and a value starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR is prefixed with an apostrophe, so an operator-entered payment code like `=HYPERLINK(...)` cannot execute in a spreadsheet. Export needs `content.read` **and** `content.write`: leaving the system with a period of money movements is operator-level.
- API: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/reports/finance` — `summary`, `aging`, `ledger`, `ledger.csv` (text/csv attachment, `x-export-rows`, `x-export-truncated`), all `no-store`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_finance_reports`): `pnpm test:finance-reports` → 10 checks — local-day conversion, range refusals (impossible day, reversed, over-long, future), October totals with the 23:30/00:30 boundary proven both ways, outstanding that excludes cancelled/deleted/draft/paid, aging buckets (40 days → 31-60, 100 days → 90+, bucket sums equal totals), ledger paging with no duplicate row, voided rows kept and totalled apart, state/cursor validation, CSV formula-injection escaping, export contents with no internal ids, and site isolation.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 217/217 (`test:finance-reports` declared in `run-gates.ts`), `verify:error-answers` 164/164, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43.
- Limits: no report UI, no scheduled delivery of reports, no import, no reconciliation workflow (matching bank lines to payments), no per-customer statement or VAT/period closing, aging measures from the document date because there is no due-date field yet, and exports are bounded to 5,000 rows rather than paging to a complete file.
### HR-02 — a roster that cannot double-book a person (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: HR-01 could pay a month from attendance, but there was no way to say who was supposed to work it — legacy had no shift model at all. A roster is also the only place "two people scheduled for one slot" and "one person scheduled twice" can be caught before they become payroll disputes.
- Two collections, no new capability: `crm_shift_templates` (`code`, `name`, `startTime`, `endTime`, `breakMinutes`, `capacity`, `isActive`) and `crm_shift_assignments` (staff, date, template, and a snapshot of the shift's code and times). Both gate on `content.read` / `content.write` like the rest of HR until Hub grows `hr.*`.
- Overnight shifts are normal: an end time that is not after the start time means the shift crosses midnight (`22:00–06:00` is 480 minutes), and a full 24-hour span is refused as a typo. Paid minutes = span − break, with the break required to be shorter than the span.
- One person, one day, one shift: unique `(siteId, staffId, date)`. That matches attendance and payroll, which count one row per person per day, so the roster cannot promise two places at once. A duplicate is a `409 SHIFT_ALREADY_ASSIGNED` naming the existing shift, and the unique index also catches the concurrent case.
- The roster is a promise, not a draft: assignment copies `shiftCode`, `startTime`, `endTime` and `breakMinutes`. Editing a template later changes future scheduling only — a published roster does not move under the people on it. A test asserts exactly that.
- Guards: templates validate their payload (code, `HH:mm`, break < span), code is unique per site and reviving an archived code reuses the row instead of duplicating it, and updates honour `revision` (`409 SHIFT_REVISION_CONFLICT`). Competency is checked against the shift's `capacity`. Resigned staff, inactive and archived templates, unknown ids, dates before today and dates more than 180 days ahead are all refused.
- Cancelling is a delete, not a soft delete: a leftover "cancelled" roster row would still show a person on a shift they are not working. A shift dated before today cannot be cancelled at all — that day belongs to attendance and payroll — and a template still holding assignments from today on cannot be archived (`409 SHIFT_TEMPLATE_IN_USE`). Every create/update/archive/assign/cancel writes an audit row into the new `shift_template` and `shift_assignment` resource types.
- Timezone: "today" comes from the shared `customerTime` helper (fixed UTC+7), the same one finance reports and payroll now use, so a roster planned at 00:30 local is not dated yesterday.
- API: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/hr` — templates `GET/POST`, `PATCH/DELETE /:id`; assignments `GET` (range, default today, ≤62 days, filters by template/staff), `POST`, `DELETE /:id`. Reads are `no-store`; mutations are same-origin and idempotent.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_hr_shifts`): `pnpm test:hr-shifts` → 15 checks — overnight duration and paid minutes, code normalisation, break/span/payload refusals, per-site code uniqueness including revival, revision conflict, snapshot immunity to template edits, double-booking refusal, resigned/inactive/unknown staff, backdating and 180-day horizon, capacity, roster summary (people/days/minutes) with range limits, cancel rules for past and future, archive blocked while in use and the shift disappearing from the picker afterwards, audit trail for define/archive/assign/cancel, and tenant isolation.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 218/218 (`test:hr-shifts` declared), `verify:error-answers` 171/171, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43, and the earlier `test:hr` still 20/20.
- Limits: no recurring weekly pattern or bulk roster, no swap/cover requests, no planned-vs-actual reconciliation against attendance (late/absent is still recorded by hand), no per-shift wage premium (overtime, night differential) and no CMS roster screen, so the API is the only surface.
### INV-02 (stocktake) — counting a warehouse and booking the difference (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: transfers moved stock between warehouses, but nothing checked whether the ledger still matched the shelves. A stocktake is the only operation that can say "the system says 10, the shelf says 7" and leave a trace of why 3 units disappeared.
- One collection: `crm_stocktakes` with `code`, `warehouseId`, `status` (`draft`/`completed`/`cancelled`), `lines` (item, variant, sku, name, expected, counted, difference, note), `note`, `completedAt`, `revision`. Lines are stored with `_id: false` so they travel as one document.
- Completing is the moment of truth: for every line the service reads the current balance, computes `difference = counted − expected`, and books it as `adjustment_inc` or `adjustment_dec` through the same transaction writer that every other movement uses, so the balance moves under its revision and the ledger stays reconcilable. A balance that does not exist is created at zero first, so stock that was never recorded is still captured rather than silently skipped.
- A completed stocktake is immutable: its lines, expected quantities and adjustments cannot be rewritten, and it cannot be cancelled. A draft can be edited, cancelled, or revived under its code.
- The count can be overridden at completion time (per line), so the draft can be prepared early and the actual count entered when the shelves are counted, without a second document.
- Code is unique per site while live; a cancelled stocktake's code can be reused.
- Guards: non-negative integer counts, revision conflict on complete, status lock, and site isolation.
- API: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/inventory/stocktakes` — `GET/POST`, `GET /:id`, `POST /:id/complete`, `POST /:id/cancel`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_stocktake`): `pnpm test:stocktake` → 10 checks — draft counts, completing books -3 as `adjustment_dec` and leaves the balance at 7, completed immutability for both complete and cancel, a missing balance created from zero and adjusted up, overriding the count at completion, cancel plus live-code reuse, non-negative/non-integer refusal, stale revision refusal, tenant isolation, and the audit trail.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 219/219 (`test:stocktake` declared), `verify:error-answers` 176/176, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 43/43.
- Limits: no scheduled/assigned counting tasks, no per-line approver, no reason codes beyond a free-text note, no barcode-driven count input, and no CMS screen yet.
### MSG-02 (analytics) — what the mail queue actually did (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: the queue could send, retry and fail, but nobody could answer "how many mails went out this month, per template, and why did last week's fail?" Legacy reported nothing at all. The parity row asks for delivery analytics alongside the queue.
- Read-only: `mailAnalyticsService.ts` counts rows of `crm_mail_deliveries` in the selected period, so the report cannot disagree with the queue it summarises. A delivery is counted once, in the local day it was created, by `templateCode` and by status.
- Shape: `totals` (all/queued/sending/sent/failed/cancelled), `byTemplate` with the same breakdown, a `byDay` series (queued/sent/failed) for charting, `queueHealth` and the top ten `failureReasons` grouped verbatim from `lastError`.
- Queue health is deliberately separate from the period: `dueNow` counts queued rows whose `nextAttemptAt` has passed, `sending` counts claimed rows (a stuck claim is visible even if it was created long ago), and `oldestQueuedAt` + `oldestQueuedAgeMinutes` answer "what is the oldest thing still not sent?". A period of one quiet day would otherwise hide a queue that has been stuck for a month.
- Boundaries are the customer's days (`Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh`, fixed UTC+7 via the shared helper); the default window is the last 30 days, both ends inclusive, capped at 366 days and refused when reversed or not a real calendar day.
- No PII in the response: counts by template and status only — no recipient address, no subject, no body.
- API: `GET /api/v1/admin/mail/analytics?from&to` behind `content.read`, `no-store`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_mail_analytics`): `pnpm test:mail-analytics` → 6 checks — totals and per-template breakdown from a mixed queue, due-now/sending/oldest-age health, grouped failure reasons, tenant isolation, date validation (impossible day, reversed range, over-long range), and the 30-day default window.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 220/220, `verify:error-answers` 177/177, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34.
- Limits: no open/bounce tracking (needs provider webhooks), no per-recipient history (use the delivery list), no scheduled report or export, no retention window on the read, and no CMS screen.
### PUR-01 (supplier prices) — what was actually paid, per receipt (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: the purchase order carried `unitCost`, but nothing recorded the price actually paid over time, so a buyer could not tell whether a supplier had crept up. The order's own `unitCost` is a quote until stock is received; the ledger row is the price paid.
- Source is the stock ledger, not the order: every received line books an `import` transaction with its `unitCost`, dated. Reading the order instead would collapse several partial receipts into one price on the day the order last changed. Only receipts count — an ordered-but-never-received line is a quote.
- Grouping: `supplierId × itemId × variantId`. Each group reports `lastUnitCost` (newest receipt), `previousUnitCost` (the last receipt at a *different* cost), `changePercent` (one decimal, null when the price never changed), `minUnitCost`, `maxUnitCost`, a quantity-weighted `averageUnitCost`, `totalQuantity`, `totalValue`, `observations`, and up to 50 `history` rows (date, PO code, quantity, unit cost, value).
- A separate `checkSupplierPrice` gives a yes/no verdict on a proposed unit cost against the last price paid: `hasHistory`, `changePercent`, `needsReview` once the absolute change reaches the caller's threshold (0–100%, default 10), and a reason string. No history means no verdict — a first purchase cannot be 20% too expensive.
- Bounds: date window defaults to 365 days, cap 1095; invalid or reversed calendar days refused, over-long windows refused; groups capped at 200.
- API: `GET /admin/procurement/supplier-prices?supplierId&itemId&variantId&from&to&limit` and `GET /admin/procurement/supplier-prices/check?supplierId&itemId&variantId&unitCost&quantity&thresholdPercent`, both behind `content.read`, `no-store`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_supplier_prices`): `pnpm test:supplier-prices` → 12 checks — grouping with newest price first, weighted average (2 × 100k + 6 × 120k = 115k, not 110k), min/max and the 20% jump from 100k to 120k, variant separation, supplier filter, ordered-but-unreceived lines excluded, tenant isolation, date-window exclusion, price check above/below threshold, first purchase with no verdict, and bad input refusal.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 221/221, `verify:error-answers` 177/177, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34.
- Limits: no payable posting (finance still open), no supplier portal, no import of the 6 legacy purchase orders, and a receipt is not wrapped in a database transaction. Serial registration from a receipt now lands on `crm_serial_numbers` (count must match the received delta; duplicate serials are rejected); serial writer for order-driven stock movements is still open.
### MSG-02 (newsletter) — consent before contact (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: legacy's newsletter subscribed whoever posted an email, which is the kind of thing that gets a domain blacklisted. Marketing mail already needed an explicit consent assertion at queue time; this adds the consent lifecycle that produces it.
- One collection: `crm_newsletter_subscriptions` with `email` (normalised), `status` (`pending`/`confirmed`/`unsubscribed`), `consent`, `source`, `ipHash`, a hashed confirmation token, `subscribedAt`, `confirmedAt`, `unsubscribedAt`, soft delete and revision.
- Double opt-in: `subscribe` with `consent: true` creates a `pending` row and returns a plaintext token (only at that moment; only its SHA-256 is stored). `confirm` promotes it to `confirmed` and clears the token. A confirmed address re-subscribing stays confirmed and gets no new token; a pending address re-subscribing gets a fresh token and the old one stops working.
- Unsubscribe needs an email or a token; a previously unsubscribed address coming back must consent again and goes through confirmation once more.
- No PII in logs: only counts and status.
- API: `GET/POST /admin/newsletter/subscriptions` (subscribe), `POST .../confirm`, `POST .../unsubscribe`, `GET .../:id`, `DELETE .../:id?revision=`, `POST .../:id/restore`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_newsletter`): `pnpm test:newsletter` → 12 checks — consent required, pending + token, confirmation promotes, confirmed re-subscribe needs no token, pending refresh invalidates the old token, unsubscribe by email, re-subscribe after unsubscribe re-confirms, invalid token rejected, missing target rejected, soft delete/restore, tenant isolation, separate counts.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 222/222, `verify:error-answers` 177/177, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34.
- Limits: no public subscribe/confirm/unsubscribe endpoints yet (those belong to the storefront, tm-web), no email sending wired (the caller queues the confirmation mail through the existing queue with its consent assertion), no rate limiting or anti-abuse beyond the consent flag, no expiry on confirmation tokens, and no preference centre beyond a single list.
### MSG-02 (contact) — capture, triage, close (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: a contact form that only stores rows is a graveyard; someone has to pick them up, reply and close them, and the handover has to survive a second operator. Legacy had none of that.
- One collection: `crm_contact_messages` with `name`, `email`, `phone`, `subject`, `message`, `source`, `ipHash`, `userAgent`, `status` (`new`/`read`/`replied`/`closed`), `adminNote`, `readAt`, `repliedAt`, `closedAt`, soft delete and revision.
- Submissions arrive as `new`. `update` moves the status (and stamps the matching timestamp on first transition), edits the admin note, and bumps the revision. Replaying the same status is a no-op so a double click does not create a second audit line. A stale revision is refused.
- List supports status filter, free-text search across name/email/subject/message, cursor pagination and include-deleted, with counts per status.
- API: `GET/POST /admin/contact-messages`, `GET /:id`, `POST /:id/update`, `DELETE /:id?revision=`, `POST /:id/restore`.
