- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_contact_messages`): `pnpm test:contact-messages` → 10 checks — submission starts new with normalised email, empty message refused, read sets readAt and bumps revision, replay is a no-op, replied/closed stamp their timestamps and note, stale revision refused, soft delete/restore, tenant isolation, per-status counts, search.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 223/223, `verify:error-answers` 177/177, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34.
- Limits: no public submit endpoint yet (belongs to the storefront, tm-web), no email reply wired through the mail queue, no assignment to an operator, no SLA/ageing, and no anti-abuse beyond what the caller enforces.
### COM-03 (shipments) — partial fulfillment against the order (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: an order said "5 shirts, 2 trousers" and the warehouse shipped them in three boxes over two days; nothing recorded which box carried what, so a short delivery or a duplicate dispatch could not be detected. Legacy had no shipment record at all.
- One collection: `crm_shipments` with `code`, `orderId`, `status` (`draft`/`shipped`/`in_transit`/`delivered`/`returned`/`cancelled`), `carrier`, `trackingNumber`, `items` (item, variant, sku, name, quantity), `shippingAddress`, `note`, `shippedAt`, `deliveredAt`, `cancelledAt`, soft delete and revision.
- Fulfillment is checked against the order: every line must match an order line, and its quantity cannot exceed `ordered − returned − already shipped` across the non-cancelled shipments for that order. A cancelled shipment frees its quantity again, so a re-dispatch after a failed delivery does not need the order edited.
- Status transitions stamp `shippedAt`/`deliveredAt`/`cancelledAt` on first entry; carrier and tracking number are editable. Replaying the same status is a no-op.
- List supports status/order filter, free-text search across code/carrier/tracking, cursor pagination and include-deleted, with counts per status.
- API: `GET/POST /admin/shipments`, `GET /:id`, `POST /:id/update`, `DELETE /:id?revision=`, `POST /:id/restore`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_shipments`): `pnpm test:shipments` → 9 checks — draft against an order, over-shipment refused, foreign line refused, missing order refused, partial shipment leaves room for the next, delivered stamps deliveredAt, cancelled frees quantity, soft delete/restore, tenant isolation.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 224/224, `verify:error-answers` 177/177, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34.
- Limits: no returns/refunds record (order.returnedQuantity is still edited by hand), no carrier adapter or label printing, stock movement now lands on the ledger when a shipment ships (export) and reverses on cancel/delete/return, no automatic order-status sync, and no tracking webhook.
### COM-03 (returns) — refunds that move the order's returned quantity (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: a customer sent 2 of 5 shirts back and the order still said 0 returned, so stock and the invoice disagreed and nobody could tell whether a refund was owed. Returns were edited by hand on the order row.
- One collection: `crm_order_returns` with `code`, `orderId`, `status` (`draft`/`approved`/`refunded`/`cancelled`), `items` (item, variant, sku, name, quantity, unit price, reason), `refundAmount`, `refundMethod`, `note`, `approvedAt`, `refundedAt`, `cancelledAt`, soft delete and revision.
- Approving or refunding a return writes the returned quantity back to the matching order lines; cancelling, deleting or restoring reverses that write, so the order's `returnedQuantity` tracks the live returns rather than a stale edit.
- Quantity is checked against `ordered − already returned` across the non-cancelled returns for that order, and a line must match an order line.
- API: `GET/POST /admin/order-returns`, `GET /:id`, `POST /:id/update`, `DELETE /:id?revision=`, `POST /:id/restore`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_order_returns`): `pnpm test:order-returns` → 9 checks — approved return stamps approvedAt and moves the order quantity, a second return cannot exceed what remains, foreign line rejected, missing order rejected, approving a draft moves the quantity, cancelling restores it, tenant isolation, soft delete/restore.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 225/225, `verify:error-answers` 177/177, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34.
- Limits: no payment/refund posting to finance, no carrier adapter, no restock movement, no automatic order status change, and no partial refund allocation across payment rows.
### CAT-02 (serial/IMEI) — register and track serial numbers (landed 2026-11-19)

- Why: a sold phone or laptop came back under warranty and nobody could tell whether the returned unit was the one we shipped, because the IMEI lived in a free-text note on the product. There was no serial record at all.
- One collection: `crm_serial_numbers` with `serial`, `itemId`, `variantId`, `warehouseId`, `status` (`in_stock`/`sold`/`returned`/`scrapped`), `purchaseOrderId`, `orderId`, `receivedAt`, `soldAt`, `returnedAt`, `scrappedAt`, note, soft delete and revision. Serial is unique per site.
- Register many at once; a duplicate serial is refused before any row is written. Status moves forward only: in stock → sold (stamps order and soldAt) → returned (stamps returnedAt), or any state → scrapped (stamps scrappedAt). A serial that is not in stock cannot be sold and one that is not sold cannot be returned.
- List supports status/item/warehouse filter, free-text search on the serial, cursor pagination and include-deleted, with counts per status.
- API: `GET/POST /admin/serial-numbers`, `GET /:id`, `POST /:id/mark-sold`, `POST /:id/mark-returned`, `POST /:id/scrap`, `DELETE /:id?revision=`, `POST /:id/restore`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_serial_numbers`): `pnpm test:serial-numbers` → 10 checks — register as in stock, duplicate rejected, invalid payload rejected, mark sold stamps order and soldAt, non-stock cannot be sold, sold can be returned, returned cannot be returned again, scrap stamps scrappedAt, soft delete/restore, tenant isolation.
- Evidence (static): `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean, `eslint` clean, `verify:scaffold` 226/226, `verify:error-answers` 177/177, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:modules` 34/34.
- Limits: no automatic registration from purchase-order receipt, no stock-balance deduction per serial, no link to the order's line quantity, no warranty/repair history, and no barcode scan integration.

### WEB-07 (newsletter opt-out) — unsubscribe from the mail (landed 2026-11-21)

- Why: the signup mail offered a way in and none out, and confirmation consumed the only token the row had, so a confirmed subscriber could not leave the list — the consent box promised "unsubscribe at any time".
- One extra hash: signup mints a second `randomUUID()` token and stores only its SHA-256 in `unsubscribeTokenHash`; the token exists solely inside the link in the queued mail. The confirmation mail's variables now also carry `unsubscribeUrl` (`/newsletter/huy-dang-ky?token=`), which a template that does not use it simply ignores.
- `POST /api/public/newsletter/unsubscribe` (body `{ token }`, 422 for a malformed token) moves a `pending` or `confirmed` row to `unsubscribed`: `unsubscribedAt` stamped, `consent` cleared, `confirmTokenHash` nulled so the old confirmation link cannot opt them back in, `revision` bumped. The hash is kept afterwards so a repeat click (or a client prefetching the link) answers `unsubscribed` rather than 404; an unknown token answers 404 `NEWSLETTER_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND`.
- Signing up again later is still possible: the pending upsert rotates both hashes and restarts double opt-in, and the old unsubscribe link dies with them.
- Files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/public-newsletter.ts` (`NewsletterUnsubscribeResponseSchema`), `tm-web/server/utils/newsletterService.ts` (`unsubscribePublicNewsletter`, signup tokens), `tm-web/server/api/public/newsletter/unsubscribe.post.ts`, `tm-web/server/models/newsletterPublic.ts` (`unsubscribeTokenHash`), `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/web-base/app/pages/newsletter/huy-dang-ky.vue`, `tm-web/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`newsletter.unsubscribe*`).
- Evidence (static): `pnpm test:newsletter` → 12/12 contract and rendering checks (unsubscribe response shape, token reuse, `unsubscribeUrl` rendering); `tm-web nuxt typecheck` clean.
- Evidence (browser, disposable DB `tmweb_test_unsubui`, dev server :4006): a real link from the queued mail opened `/newsletter/huy-dang-ky` → `Đang huỷ…` (server-rendered) then `Bạn đã huỷ đăng ký nhận tin`; the row moved to `unsubscribed` with `consent:false` and a stamped `unsubscribedAt`; the same link again answered `unsubscribed` (no second revision); an unknown token showed `Liên kết huỷ đăng ký không hợp lệ`; the English copy and the 375 px layout were checked too. The token is dropped from the address after the response.
- Not run: no template in the CMS yet that shows the opt-out link (the queued variable is only rendered when the operator's `newsletter-confirm` template references `unsubscribeUrl`).

### WEB-07 (newsletter resend) — "the mail never arrived" (landed 2026-11-21)

- Why: a pending signup whose confirmation mail was lost, filtered or deleted had no way back — the visitor could only type the address again and hope, and a repeat signup is indistinguishable from a typo. The form said "check your inbox" with no next step.
- `POST /api/public/newsletter/resend` (body `{ email }`, normalised and validated like a signup) looks up the caller's own `pending` row and queues the mail again. Only `email` is accepted: the client cannot choose a token or a recipient for someone else's row.
- Anti-enumeration: the route answers `{ state: 'accepted' }` for every address — pending, confirmed, unsubscribed or unknown. Whether a mail actually went out is decided server-side, so the button cannot be used to probe the list.
- Anti-abuse: `NEWSLETTER_RESEND_COOLDOWN_MS` (5 minutes) per row, read from `confirmSentAt`, which both signup and resend write only after the mail is queued. Two taps that pass the cooldown together still queue one mail, because the token rotation is a conditional `findOneAndUpdate`.
- A resend rotates both hashes and mints new tokens: the resent mail carries fresh confirm and opt-out links, and the links from the lost mail stop working. The `.name` already on the row is reused for the greeting.
- Files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/public-newsletter.ts` (`NewsletterResendRequestSchema`, `NewsletterResendResponseSchema`), `tm-web/server/utils/newsletterService.ts` (`queueNewsletterConfirmation`, `resendNewsletterConfirmation`, `NEWSLETTER_RESEND_COOLDOWN_MS`), `tm-web/server/api/public/newsletter/resend.post.ts`, `tm-web/server/models/newsletterPublic.ts` (`confirmSentAt`), `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/web-base/app/pages/contact.vue` (resend button on the pending/not-queued notice), `tm-web/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`newsletter.resend*`).
- Also fixed while reviewing the opt-out slice: a truncated or malformed token (`422 NEWSLETTER_TOKEN_INVALID`) used to be shown as a retryable failure ("try again later") on both landing pages; it now reads as an unusable link, like a dead token.
- Evidence (static): `pnpm test:newsletter` → 14/14 (resend request normalisation, neutral single-state response, plus the earlier checks); `tm-web nuxt typecheck` clean.
- Evidence (runtime, `tmweb_test_unsubui`): subscribe → 1 delivery, `confirmSentAt` set; resend immediately → `accepted`, still 1 delivery (cooldown); after ageing `confirmSentAt` 10 minutes, resend → 2nd delivery with a different token, the old confirm token answered 404 and the new one confirmed; resend for a confirmed address and for an unknown address → `accepted` with no new row or delivery; an invalid address → 422. In the browser at 375 px (vi): pending notice showed "Gửi lại thư xác nhận", clicking it showed the neutral "Nếu email này đang chờ xác nhận…" copy and queued nothing extra under the cooldown.
- Limits: the cooldown is per row and in-process-free but not distributed beyond the database; there is no captcha, no per-IP limit and no unsubscribe-from-all link in the resend mail beyond the per-subscription token; the neutral answer means the UI cannot say whether a mail was sent.

### WEB-06 (order confirmation mail) — the order says so in writing (landed 2026-11-21)

- Why: a guest finished checkout with a success screen and a code, and that was the end of it — nothing reached their inbox, so the only record of the order lived in a browser tab they were about to close. The confirmation is also the first place a buyer can read back what they ordered and how to look it up later.
- The mail is queued by the storefront, from the order it just wrote: `POST /api/public/checkout` calls `queueOrderConfirmation` after the `crm_orders` insert and the audit row, using the CMS's own `crm_mail_deliveries` queue and the operator's `order-confirmation` template (locale `vi`). The queue row snapshots the rendered subject/body, so a later template edit cannot rewrite a mail already sent.
- Variables: `orderCode`, `name`, `email`, `phone`, `itemCount`, `items` (one `2 × Áo thử — 120.000 ₫` line per line, prices from the server quote), `total`, `totalAmount`, `currency` and `orderLookupUrl` (`/tra-cuu-don`). The item lines are rendered before substitution because a template only replaces `{{ token }}`.
- Anti-duplicate: the dedupe key is `order-confirmation:<order code>`, so a retried submit — the same `Idempotency-Key` resolving to the order that already exists — cannot mail twice. That retry reads the queue back (`orderConfirmationQueued`) instead of guessing, and answers the same `mailQueued` as the first attempt.
- A mail problem never fails an order: no email on the order, a missing/archived template or a queue error leaves the order written and answers `mailQueued: false`. The success page prints "Thư xác nhận đơn hàng đang được gửi tới email bạn nhập" only for `mailQueued: true`, so it never promises a mail that was not queued.
- Files: `tm-shared/packages/flow-contracts/src/public-checkout.ts` (`ORDER_CONFIRMATION_TEMPLATE`, `orderConfirmationVariables`, `mailQueued` on the response), `tm-web/server/utils/orderMail.ts`, `tm-web/server/utils/checkoutService.ts` (`CheckoutResult`), `tm-web/server/api/public/checkout.post.ts`, `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/web-base/app/pages/thanh-toan.vue`, `tm-web/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`checkout.mailQueued`).
- Evidence (static): `tsx scripts/build/test-checkout.ts` → 12/12 — the response requires a boolean `mailQueued` (absent means false, `'yes'` refused), and the variables carry the order code, trimmed name, lowercased address, item count, VND-formatted lines and total, and an absolute lookup URL without a doubled slash; `tm-web nuxt typecheck` clean; `eslint --no-ignore` clean on the three server files.
- Evidence (runtime, disposable DB `tmweb_test_ordermail`, dev server :4007): checkout with an email → 201 and `mailQueued: true`, one `crm_mail_deliveries` row (`queued`, `order-confirmation`, dedupe `a@example.com:order-confirmation:WEB-06F90D2D`, subject and body rendered from the template with the real total); the same `Idempotency-Key` again → same order code and `mailQueued: true`, still one delivery; checkout without an email → 201 and `mailQueued: false`, no delivery; archiving the template and checking out again → 201, order written, `mailQueued: false`, no delivery.
- Evidence (browser, same DB, 375 px, vi): product page → add to cart → `/thanh-toan` → form filled → order `WEB-C595A58C` → success screen showed the confirmation line above the code, total and links, with no horizontal overflow; the queued row was `c@example.com:order-confirmation:WEB-C595A58C`.
- Limits: the mail is only confirmation of a written order — no shipping, cancellation or refund notices; no attachment/invoice; the template must exist and declare its variables or the order simply has no mail; delivery still depends on the CMS's `TM_MAIL_PROVIDER` drain, which the storefront does not run; the mail is always in the site's default locale (`vi`) because an order does not store the visitor's language.

### WEB-06 (order status mail) — a moved order tells the customer (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: the confirmation was the only mail an order ever produced. Once staff moved an order to `processing`, `shipped`, `completed` or `cancelled`, the buyer learned nothing, and the status only existed inside the CMS.
- The notification is queued by the CMS, where the status actually changes: `updateOrder` knows `statusChanged`, so after the order is saved and audited it calls `queueOrderStatusMail` (`tm-cms/server/services/orderStatusMail.ts`) with the mail models the admin route passes as `meta.notify`. Callers that pass no `notify` keep the plain update.
- Recipient: `crm_orders.customerEmail`, which checkout now stores for the address its confirmation went to, falling back to the linked `crm_customers.email` for an order placed in the CMS. An order with neither is simply not mailed.
- One message per transition: the dedupe key is `order-status:<status>:<order code>`, so a retried save, or an order that returns to a status it already had, cannot mail the same step twice. The template is `order-status` (locale `vi`) with `orderCode`, `name`, `status`, `statusLabel`, `itemCount`, `items`, `total`.
- A mail problem never fails the update: no address, an archived or missing template, or missing variables leave the order saved (`mailQueued` is not part of the admin response); a queue error is logged with the order code and swallowed.
- Files: `tm-cms/server/services/orderStatusMail.ts` (`ORDER_STATUS_TEMPLATE`, `orderStatusVariables`, `orderStatusDedupeKey`, `queueOrderStatusMail`), `tm-cms/server/services/orderService.ts` (`OrderUpdateMeta.notify`, the post-save hook), `tm-cms/server/models/order.ts` (`customerEmail`), `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/commerce/orders/[id].patch.ts` (passes `MailDelivery`/`MailTemplate`/`Customer`), `tm-web/server/utils/checkoutService.ts` (writes `customerEmail` on a guest order).
- Also this pass: the checkout success card on `/thanh-toan` is now `max-w-xl` instead of spanning the whole `max-w-5xl` container — at 1440 the 960 px card held a 448 px summary and half the box was empty. The form layout is untouched.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_commerce_service`): `pnpm test:commerce` → 22/22, of which 4 new — a status change queues one `order-status` row with the transition dedupe key, lowered address and rendered subject/lines; a note-only save queues nothing and a repeated transition keeps one message; an order without an address is not mailed; an archived template still lets the status change through without a delivery.
- Evidence (static): `tm-web nuxt typecheck` clean. `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still reports the two errors that predate this change (`admin/crm/customers/duplicates.get.ts` `groups`, `me/orders/index.get.ts` `customerId` against the shared list-response type) — re-checked with these edits stashed, unchanged.
- Evidence (browser, disposable DB `tmweb_test_cardw`, dev server :4007, 1440 px): checkout order `WEB-868DEB91` → success card measured 576×370 at x=232, summary 448 wide, both buttons on one row, `scrollWidth` 1423 ≤ 1440 (no overflow); the order document stored `customerEmail: cardw@example.com`.
- The shipment path tells them too (same day): `recomputeOrderStatusFromShipments` is the other place an order's status changes, so it takes the same optional `notify`, queues the same notice after its own audit row, and swallows the same failures. The three shipment routes pass `MailDelivery`/`MailTemplate`/`Customer` through `createShipment`/`updateShipment`/`softDeleteShipment`, so an order that reaches `shipped` or `completed` because a shipment moved mails the customer without anyone editing the order. Because the recompute can also step an order back (`completed → pending` when its last shipment is cancelled), that reverse move is a transition of its own and is mailed once; a status the order already mailed is not sent again, since the key is still `order-status:<status>:<code>`.
- Evidence (service, disposable DB `tm_test_shipments`): `pnpm test:shipments` → 15/15, two new — shipping every line from a shipment queues one `order-status` row under `ship@example.com:order-status:shipped:ORD-SHIPMAIL` with the `Đang giao hàng` label and lowered address; delivering it queues `completed`, cancelling returns the order to `pending` (its own mail), and re-shipping to `shipped` adds no second `shipped` message.
- Not run: the real send (still depends on the CMS `TM_MAIL_PROVIDER` drain and an `order-status` template the operator has to author).

### COM-01/02/03, INV-01/02, FIN-01/02 — the four remaining CMS module pages (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: the server slices for orders, inventory, finance and reports had landed on 2026-11-19, but all four CMS pages were still `<ModulePendingPage>` and the registry still told the reader to wait for a data model. The next session would have rebuilt services that already existed, and no editor could reach them.
- One client, four screens: `useOperationsAdmin` speaks to the existing admin routes (no new endpoint, no new collection) and the four pages are real editors:
  - `/orders`: cursor list with status/deleted filters and counts, create with catalog-picked lines and a shipping address, edit with the revision lock, status transition plus the order's own history, hide/restore, and the audit note.
  - `/inventory`: four tabs — balances (on hand / reserved / available, low-stock badge per warehouse), warehouses (create/edit/hide/restore), transfers (create from two warehouses, ship, receive full lines, cancel), stocktakes (create, complete which books the differences, cancel). A fifth **Serials / IMEI** tab landed on 2026-11-22 (see the narrative below).
  - `/finance`: payments (list, record against an order or purchase order by id with the direction enforced server-side, void with a reason) and payables (list, void).
  - `/reports`: finance summary with the per-method split, receivables/payables buckets and aging, and the ledger with state filter, load-more and the existing CSV route.
- Registry: `orders`, `inventory`, `finance` and `reports` are `dataSource: 'connected'` with no blocker and no pending hint; only `aiWorkspace` stays pending (Q15). The four stale `modules.*.pendingHint` strings were removed from both locales.
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (new), `.../pages/{orders,inventory,finance,reports}.vue`, `.../utils/module-registry.ts`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.orders`, `admin.inventory`, `admin.finance`, `admin.reports`, `common.confirm`), `scripts/build/verify-audit-reason-ui.ts` (the three new write screens are listed).
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 26/26 (a connected module is not the pending page and declares no hint/blocker) and `verify:audit-reason-ui` 55/55 (orders, inventory and finance render the note control and pass the typed note; the composable sends `x-audit-reason`). `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` reports only the two errors that predate this change (`admin/crm/customers/duplicates.get.ts` `groups`, `me/orders/index.get.ts` `customerId`).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 bound to `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1280 px): `/orders` rendered its list API (`200`) and the empty state; `/inventory` rendered all four tabs, its balance API and the *Add warehouse* dialog with code/name/phone/status/address/description and the audit note, dismissed with Escape; `/finance` rendered the payments list, filters and *Record payment* action; `/reports` rendered both date fields, the summary, the five aging buckets for receivables and payables, the ledger and the CSV link. Console clean on all four (only Vite and the Nuxt `<Suspense>` notice).
- Not run: no write was submitted in the browser — the write paths are covered by the existing service gates (`test:commerce` 22, `test:inventory` 17, `test:inventory-transfers` 13, `test:finance` 12, `test:finance-reports` 10) rather than by a UI submit; the local `sen-tuan` copy holds no orders, warehouses or payments, so only the empty states were seen; the 390 px layout and the `reports` CSV download itself were not exercised.

### COM-03 — shipments and returns from the order page (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: `shipmentService` and `orderReturnService` had routes and gates, but no CMS screen called them, so an operator could record an order and never say what left the warehouse or what came back; the `orders` row said "services exist" and nothing else.
- One modal, two tabs: a truck and an undo button on every `/orders` row open *Fulfillment · <order code>* with **Shipments** and **Returns** tabs. Both tabs list the rows for that order (`?orderId=`, deleted rows excluded) with status badges, carrier/tracking or refund amount/method, line count and the shipped date, plus the actions the service already models: shipment ship/deliver/cancel/hide and return approve/refund/cancel/hide.
- Remaining quantity is computed in the client from what it can see: a shipment line offers `ordered − returnedQuantity − already shipped (non-cancelled)`, and a return line offers `ordered − returnedQuantity`; the modal refetches both lists after every write, so the second shipment or return picks up the new numbers.
- Create forms: shipment (carrier, tracking, initial status, note, per-line quantity, address copied from the order) and return (status, refund method `cash|card|bank|credit`, refund amount, note, per-line quantity and the order line's unit price). The document codes are minted client-side as `SHP-<base36>` / `RTN-<base36>` because the schema accepts `^[A-Z0-9][A-Z0-9_-]{0,31}$`; edits reuse the row's `revision`, and every write form carries the audit note (`x-audit-reason`).
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (shipment/return types and the eight calls), `.../pages/orders.vue` (fulfillment modal, two create forms, two edit forms, delete confirm), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.orders.fulfillment`, `.shipments`, `.returns`, four new error strings).
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 26/26, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 55/55, both locale files parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still reports only the two pre-existing errors (`admin/crm/customers/duplicates.get.ts` `groups`, `me/orders/index.get.ts` `customerId`).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session): a probe order of 2 units was written through the page's own API and then driven entirely from the UI — *Shipments* tab on the row showed the empty state, the create form prefilled `2 left`, a 1-unit shipment with carrier `GHN` and a typed audit note returned `200` and appeared as `SHP-… / Shipped` while the order turned `Processing`; *Delivered* moved it to `Delivered` (and dropped the ship/cancel buttons); *Returns* create took 1 unit and `100.000 ₫` cash, then *Approve* → `Approved` and *Mark refunded* → `Refunded`. No console errors beyond the earlier wrong-database 503s from before the server restart. The probe order, its shipment, its return and its 7 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_orders 0 / crm_shipments 0 / crm_order_returns 0`).
- Harness note: the browser tab stayed `visibility: hidden`, so CSS leave animations froze and a closed dialog kept painting at opacity 1; the state did close (the form reset and the next list refreshed), so this is an automation artifact, not a UI defect — a visible window was not exercised.
- Not run: no carrier adapter or booking (still a follow-up slice), no partial return of a shipment line other than the order line it came from, no stock-effect assertions in the browser (covered by `test:shipments` 15 / `test:order-returns` 9), and no 390/1440 layout pass on the new modals.

### PUR-01 — purchasing gets a CMS screen (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: suppliers, purchase orders, receipts and supplier price history were server slices with `test:procurement` 26 behind them, but no CMS page reached any of it, and the registry had no purchasing entry at all — an operator could receive stock only through the API.
- New module, not a hidden page: `purchaseOrders` joins the module registry (`/purchasing`, `nav.purchaseOrders`, gated by the existing `purchaseOrders` entitlement) and the sidebar after *Inventory*, so `verify:modules` now checks its page like the other connected modules and the nav entry disappears for a customer that is not licensed for it.
- Two tabs on `/purchasing`:
  - *Suppliers*: cursor list with search, status filter, include-hidden and counts; create/edit (code, name, email, phone, status, rating, comma-separated items supplied, note), hide/restore.
  - *Purchase orders*: list with search, status, supplier filter and counts; create/edit with supplier and receiving warehouse, status, payment method, expected date, note and lines; a line can be linked to a catalog item (which fills the name, SKU and a default unit cost) or typed by hand, and the estimated total is computed in the form. Lines lock once the order is `received`/`cancelled` (the server already refuses that edit), and `/receive` takes a received and a rejected quantity per line against the remaining quantity.
- `listItemOptions` in `useOperationsAdmin` now takes a limit and clamps it to the catalog route's 50; the page asks for 50 so the picker is useful, and keeps the 20-item default for callers that do not care.
- Two honest dead-ends, surfaced instead of swallowed: the receive dialog explains that a receipt needs a warehouse on the order and a catalog item on every line, and a failed stock move (`PURCHASE_ORDER_RECEIPT_STOCK_FAILED`) says the item has no stock balance in that warehouse yet instead of pretending the order was received.
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/purchasing.vue` (new), `.../composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (supplier/purchase-order types and calls, bounded `listItemOptions`), `.../utils/module-registry.ts`, `.../layouts/default.vue`, `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`nav.purchaseOrders`, `modules.purchaseOrders`, `admin.purchasing`), `scripts/build/verify-audit-reason-ui.ts` (the page is a write screen with a note control).
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 29/29 (the new connected module has a real page, unique route and no hint/blocker), `verify:audit-reason-ui` 59/59, both locales parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still only the two pre-existing errors (`admin/crm/customers/duplicates.get.ts` `groups`, `me/orders/index.get.ts` `customerId`).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440 px): the sidebar shows *Purchasing & Suppliers*; a supplier (`PROBE-SUP-1`, rating 4, two supplied items) was created from the dialog and read back from the API; a purchase order with one 2 × 500.000 line was created from the form (server-generated code `PO-…`, total 1.000.000); the edit dialog pre-filled the order, the receive dialog showed `Ordered 2 · received 0 · rejected 0 · 2 left`, and recording 1 received moved the order to `Partial` with `receivedQuantity: 1` and the warehouse balance `1` through one `import` ledger row. Probe supplier, order, warehouse, balance, ledger row and 6 audit entries were deleted afterwards (`crm_suppliers 0 / crm_purchase_orders 0 / crm_warehouses 0 / balance 0 / transactions 0`).
- Harness note: the tab stayed `visibility: hidden`, so CSS leave animations froze and closed dialogs/backdrops kept painting; injecting a near-zero animation duration cleared them and the rest of the flow ran normally. The warehouse and catalog-item selects were verified by opening them (options and the selected label rendered) but the final warehouse/item link for the probe order was written through the page's own PATCH, not by two UI clicks.
- Not run: no supplier price-history screen (`/procurement/supplier-prices` is still API-only), no listing of payables raised by a receipt from this page, and a first receipt of a never-stocked item still needs an existing inventory balance row for that item and warehouse (the service books a movement against a balance, it does not create one).

### HR-01 — staff, attendance and payroll from one screen (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: HR-01 had models, services, routes and tests (`test:hr` 21, `test:hr-shifts` 15, `test:hr-self-service` 6) but no CMS surface, so a profile, a day's attendance or a payroll run could only be created through the API. The module is now a first-class sibling of orders/inventory/purchasing/finance/reports.
- Shape: one page at `/hr` (`hr`, entitlement `hr`), three tabs instead of three routes — *Staff*, *Attendance*, *Payroll* — because the three share one operator task and the payroll tab needs the same staff names the other two already load.
  - *Staff*: search, work-status filter, include-hidden switch and counts; create/edit (Hub subject, employee code, name, position, work status, joined/left dates, phone, job description, note), hide and restore. Compensation is deliberately absent from this form, so listing staff can never leak a salary.
  - *Attendance*: month + staff + status filters over the server's own summary; recording and correcting are the same dialog because `(staff, date)` is unique, and deleting a day states that the day stops counting for payroll.
  - *Payroll*: period + staff + status filters, counts and the period's net total, a calculate dialog (whole site or one person, with itemised adjustments), and the `draft → approved → paid` moves plus `cancel` with its mandatory reason. Only `content.write` can read this tab, matching the routes.
  - *Salary config*: opened from a staff row, shows the effective-dated history and posts a new config (base salary, standard work days, effective date, allowances, note). Rates are stored, never edited in place.
- Boundaries: every write goes through `AuditReasonField` (`x-audit-reason`), stale writes surface the server's `*_REVISION_CONFLICT`, and a locked payroll says so instead of retrying. No new server route, no change to the HR services.
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/hr.vue` (new), `.../composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (staff/attendance/salary/payroll types and calls), `.../utils/module-registry.ts`, `.../layouts/default.vue`, `tm-shared/customer-layers/cms/sentuan-cms/app/app.config.ts` (`hr: true`; the entitlement defaults to off), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`nav.hr`, `modules.hr`, `admin.hr`), `scripts/build/verify-audit-reason-ui.ts` (the page is a write screen with a note control).
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 32/32 (the connected module has a real page, a unique route and no hint/blocker), `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, both locales parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still only the two pre-existing errors (`admin/crm/customers/duplicates.get.ts` `groups`, `me/orders/index.get.ts` `customerId`).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session): the sidebar shows *People & Payroll*; a profile (`PROBE-HR-1`, staff/probation) was created from the dialog and listed with its counts; a salary config of 26.000.000 over 26 days saved and appeared in the history; 22/11 was recorded as present 08:00–17:30 and the summary moved to `Present 1`; calculating November produced a draft of 1.500.000 (1.000.000 for the day + a 500.000 adjustment), which was approved and then marked paid (`Draft 0 · Approved 0 · Paid 1`); hiding the profile removed it from the default list and restoring it brought it back. Probe profile, attendance, config, payroll and 5 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_staff_profiles 0 / crm_attendances 0 / crm_salary_configs 0 / crm_payrolls 0`).
- Fixed while verifying: the staff hide/restore calls sent `revision` in the query string while both routes parse it from the body, so hiding a profile answered `STAFF_REVISION_REQUIRED`; they now post `{ revision }`.
- Not run: no self-service view for a staff member at the time. (The roster gap named here was closed by the HR-02 screen below, the self-service view by `/my-work`, and the unpaginated attendance read by the paging slice below.)

### HR-02 — the roster gets a screen on `/hr` (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: shift templates and assignments had models, services, six routes and `test:hr-shifts` (15 checks), but the only way to plan a week was the API; `/hr` now carries the roster as its fourth tab rather than a fourth page, because a shift is assigned to the same people the staff tab lists.
- Shape — *Shifts* tab, roster first and templates under it:
  - *Roster*: a from/to range (default today → today + 6), optional staff filter and the server's own summary (`assignments · staff · days · worked hours`), rows grouped by date showing shift code, person, copied times, worked minutes and an overnight badge. Assigning asks for person, an **active** template, date and note; cancelling is a confirm dialog because the service deletes the plan row (a past day is refused by the server, and so is a second shift on the same person/day).
  - *Templates*: list shows code, name, times, worked and break minutes, capacity and active/inactive badges with counts; create/edit covers code, name, start/end (an end at or before the start means overnight), break minutes, capacity (0 means unlimited), in-use switch and note; archiving is a confirm and the server refuses it while someone is still scheduled from today on.
- Boundaries: no new route and no service change; assignment times are copies (the page never sends times), every write carries the audit note, and a template's revision travels with its PATCH so a concurrent edit answers `SHIFT_REVISION_CONFLICT`. The assign dialog explains the empty case instead of offering an empty select.
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/hr.vue` (Shifts tab, two dialogs, two confirm branches), `.../composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (shift types and six calls), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.hr.tabs.shifts`, `admin.hr.shifts`, two error strings). No new registry entry, route or gate row: the tab lives on the page the audit gate already lists.
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, both locales parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still only the two pre-existing errors.
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session): a shift `PROBE-CA-1` (08:00–17:00, 60 min break) was created from the dialog and rendered `480 min worked`; assigning it to `Nhân sự Ca Probe` on 22/11 moved the summary to `1 assignments · 1 staff · 1 days · 8h00` with the row under its date; cancelling that assignment returned the summary to zero and removed the row; archiving the template moved the list to `0 active · 0 inactive` and back to its empty state. Probe staff, template, assignment and 4 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_staff_profiles 0 / crm_shift_templates 0 / crm_shift_assignments 0`).
- Not run: no week-grid/drag-and-drop roster, no bulk "apply this shift to these days", no assignment edit (cancel then re-assign), and the roster read is capped at 62 days per range by the service.

### HR-01 self-service — every staff member reads their own month (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: `/api/v1/me/attendance` and `/api/v1/me/payroll` landed with `test:hr-self-service` (4 checks), but nothing called them, so the only way to see your own days or pay was to ask an administrator to open `/hr` — which needs `content.read` and shows everyone. `/my-work` is the missing surface.
- Shape: a profile header (employee code, name, position and work-status badges, joined date, phone) and one month control that drives both sections. **My attendance** lists the month's days with a status badge, check-in → check-out and the note, above the server's own `All/Present/Late/Leave/Absent` summary. **My payroll** lists the month's rows with status, net pay, worked/leave/absent days, adjustments, payment date and any cancellation reason. No totals are computed in the browser.
- Privacy by construction: the page sends no staff id. The two routes resolve the staff profile from the verified Hub subject and filter by that profile, so one person cannot ask for another person's rows, and the payload carries no `subjectId` of anyone else. Both responses are `no-store` and the page is not an entitlement — a staff account with no admin capability can open it, while `/hr` still needs `content.read`/`content.write` and keeps hiding pay behind write access for others.
- Boundaries: no new server route, service or model; the page reuses `AdminStaff`/`AdminAttendance`/`AdminPayroll` shapes and the existing `admin.hr.*` status labels, so vi/en copy stays in one place. Only new copy is the page itself plus `nav.myWork`.
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/my-work.vue` (new), `.../composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (`myAttendance`, `myPayroll`), `.../layouts/default.vue` (nav entry after People & Payroll), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`nav.myWork`, `admin.myWork`).
- Unlinked case: a 404 `STAFF_NOT_FOUND` is a state, not an error — the page explains that the sign-in has no staff profile yet and asks an HR administrator to link it, rather than showing a red failure.
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, both locales parse, `pnpm lint` 0, and `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still only the two pre-existing errors.
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440×900): a staff profile `PROBE-MW-1` for the mock subject, one present day (20/11, 08:00–17:30), a 26.000.000/26-day salary config and one calculated draft produced the page: profile card `PROBE-MW-1 · Probe My Work · Staff · Official · joined 2026-11-01`, `All 1 · Present 1 · Late 0 · Leave 0 · Absent 0`, the day row `2026-11-20 · Present · 08:00 → 17:30 · probe`, and `2026-11 · Draft · Net pay 1.000.000 · 1 worked · 0 leave · 0 absent`. Hiding the profile then reloaded into *No staff profile yet* with the explanatory copy. Probe staff, attendance, salary config, payroll and 5 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_staff_profiles 0 / crm_attendances 0 / crm_salary_configs 0 / crm_payrolls 0`).
- Not run: no request/correction flow (a staff member still asks an administrator to fix a day) and no payslip document or download. (The self-service roster named here was closed by the slice below.)

### HR-02 self-service — your own shifts on `/my-work` (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: `/my-work` could show a staff member's days and pay, but not the shifts they were expected to work — there was no `/api/v1/me/*` reader for the roster at all, so "when am I on?" still needed an administrator with `content.read` to open the HR roster and filter by name.
- Shape: a **My roster** section above attendance, driven by the same month control (first → last day), with the service's own summary (`{shifts} · {days} · {hours} worked`) and one row per shift: date, shift code, copied start → end, worked minutes, an overnight badge and the note. No totals are computed in the browser.
- New route: `GET /api/v1/me/shifts?from&to` resolves the staff profile from the verified Hub subject and passes **that** id to `listShiftAssignments`; there is no `staffId` parameter, so the route cannot be pointed at a colleague. The service's own rules still apply unchanged — a range longer than 62 days and a bad date are refused with the same codes the admin route returns — and the response is `no-store`.
- Boundaries: no service or model change, no new capability; the page reuses `AdminShiftAssignment` and the existing `admin.hr.shifts.*` labels (overnight, worked minutes), and only the section copy is new.
- Files: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/me/shifts.get.ts` (new), `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (`myShifts`), `.../pages/my-work.vue` (section, month-derived range), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.myWork.roster*`), `scripts/build/test-hr-self-service.ts` (two roster checks).
- Evidence (gate): `pnpm test:hr-self-service` → 6 checks (was 4): a template and one future assignment for the signed-in staff are listed for that staff with `workedMinutes 480`, and a colleague's separate assignment is returned only to that colleague. Gates `verify:modules` 32/32, `pnpm lint` 0, both locales parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` only the two pre-existing errors (a first pass put `range` in the response `meta`, which `successResponse` does not allow, and was narrowed to `totals`).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440×900): with `PROBE-MR-1` and a colleague `PROBE-MR-2` both assigned the same probe template (23/11 and 24/11, 60-minute break), the page showed `My roster · 1 shifts · 1 days · 8h00 worked` with the single row `2026-11-23 · PROBE-MR-AM · 08:00 → 17:00 · 480 min worked · probe own`; the colleague's 24/11 shift was absent, and attendance/payroll kept their empty-month copy. Probe staff, template, two assignments and 5 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_staff_profiles 0 / crm_shift_templates 0 / crm_shift_assignments 0`).
- Not run: no swap/cover request or acknowledgement from the staff side, no week-grid, and the section follows the month control rather than a rolling "next 14 days" window.

### HR-01 attendance — a month that no longer stops at a row limit (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: the attendance list read `limit(1000)` and returned whatever fit, with the summary counted from that page. A busy month (many staff × 31 days) would silently lose rows at the end of the list, and the caller had no way to ask for the rest — the page could not even tell that its totals described a partial month.
- Service: `listAttendances` takes `cursor` and `limit` (default 100, capped at 500). The list sorts by `date` then `_id`, so a single id cannot resume it without skipping or repeating rows that share a day: the cursor is the pair `date|_id`, and a malformed one is refused with `422 ATTENDANCE_CURSOR_INVALID`. The status summary is now an aggregation over the filter (month/staff/status) rather than a count of the page, so `All 62` means the month, not the screen.
- Route/UI: `GET /api/v1/admin/hr/attendances` passes `cursor`/`limit` through and returns `meta.nextCursor`/`meta.hasMore` beside the counts; the Attendance tab asks for 50 with a *Load more* button (the existing `admin.hr.loadMore` label) and any filter change restarts at the first page. `/my-work` keeps the default page, which covers a single person's month.
- Evidence (gate): `pnpm test:hr` → 21 checks (was 20). The new check seeds five October days, reads pages of two and asserts the page boundaries (`13,12` → `11,10` → `01`), that `hasMore`/`nextCursor` end correctly, that the five ids are distinct and complete, that the summary stays `All 5 · Present 4 · Late 1` on every page, and that a malformed cursor is a 422.
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440×900): two probe staff with every October day recorded (62 rows) showed `All 62 · Present 62 · Late 0 · Leave 0 · Absent 0` with exactly 50 rows on screen and a *Load more* button; clicking it produced 62 rows ending at `2026-10-01` and removed the button. Probe staff, 62 attendance rows and 64 audit entries were deleted afterwards (`crm_staff_profiles 0 / crm_attendances 0`).
- Not run: no page numbers or "jump to page" (deep pages are sequential only), no server-side CSV export of attendance, and the page does not de-duplicate rows if two operators append in different orders.

### PUR-01 (supplier prices) — the buyer can finally read them (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: `GET /api/v1/admin/procurement/supplier-prices` and its per-line `check` existed with `test:supplier-prices` (12 checks), but the only reader was the API, so the price a supplier charged could not be looked up while purchasing. `/purchasing` now has a third tab, **Supplier prices**.
- Shape: supplier filter, item filter, and an explicit from/to window (default: last 365 days), with the server's own summary (`suppliers · items · receipts`) and the range it actually read. Each row shows SKU + item name, supplier, the change against the previous distinct price (`+20%`, green when it fell), last cost with its date, the min/max spread, the quantity-weighted average and the receipt count; the history button expands the receipt lines (`date · purchase order · qty · unit cost · value`). No totals are computed in the browser — every number comes from the service.
- Boundaries: read-only (`content.read`), no write path, no new route and no service change. The `item` filter offers the 50 items the shared picker loads, because the catalog list route caps `limit` at 50; the window is the honest way to narrow a large history.
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/purchasing.vue` (tab, state, history expansion), `.../composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (`PriceObservation`/`SupplierItemPrice` types and `supplierPriceHistory`), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.purchasing.tabs.prices`, `admin.purchasing.prices`).
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, both locales parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still only the two pre-existing errors (a first pass declared a second `supplierName` and was renamed to `supplierLabel` before the clean run).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, seeded through the supplier/purchase-order services): two receipts of `Bộ HN-BO` (`PROBE-PO-P1` 2 × 100.000 and `PROBE-PO-P2` 6 × 120.000) produced the row `HN-BO · Bộ HN-BO · NCC probe giá · +20%`, `Last 120.000 · min 100.000 · max 120.000 · average 115.000 · 8 received · 2 receipts` and a two-line history with the matching order codes, quantities and values; the summary read `1 suppliers · 1 items · 2 receipts`. Probe orders, supplier, warehouse, inventory row, 2 ledger rows and 9 audit entries were deleted afterwards (all zero, `crm_inventory_transactions` back to 0).
- Harness note: the seed script first connected to a database literally named `sentuan` instead of the registry's `sen-tuan`; the empty database it created was dropped, and the real seed then ran against `sen-tuan`. The temporary script was removed.
- Check panel (2026-11-22, same day): the tab opens with **Check a price before ordering** — item, supplier, proposed unit cost, optional quantity and a threshold (default 10%). It calls the existing `GET /api/v1/admin/procurement/supplier-prices/check` and renders the verdict from `hasHistory`/`needsReview`/`changePercent`, so the copy stays vi/en and the service does not ship a Vietnamese `reason` string to an English screen.
- Not run: no price-spike alerting tied to recording a receipt (the check is an explicit control in the tab, not part of the receipt dialog), no CSV export of the price history, and the flagged/within verdict was not exercised in the browser — seeding a receipt for the probe supplier failed with `PURCHASE_ORDER_RECEIPT_STOCK_FAILED`; the logic behind it is covered by `test:supplier-prices` 12.

### CAT-02 (serials) — the serial/IMEI register gets a screen on `/inventory` (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: `crm_serial_numbers` had a service, eight routes and `test:serial-numbers` (10 checks) since 2026-11-19, but the only place a serial could be registered was a purchase-order receipt; no CMS screen could search by serial, mark a unit sold or returned, scrap it or move it between warehouses — the exact warranty question the model was built for.
- Shape — a fifth **Serials / IMEI** tab in `/inventory`, not a new page: a serial belongs to an item and a warehouse the inventory page already lists, and moving one is an inventory operation.
  - List: free-text search on the serial (debounced), status filter (in stock / sold / returned / scrapped / all), warehouse filter, *include hidden* switch and the server's own counts per status. Each row shows the serial, status badge, item (the catalog label when the picker has already resolved it, else the item id), warehouse, the order it was sold on, its revision and the note, plus a hidden badge.
  - Register dialog: catalog item picker, optional warehouse, note and one serial per line (commas and semicolons also split); the batch is posted as `serials[]`, so the service rejects a duplicate before writing any row.
  - Row actions follow the state machine: **mark sold** (asks for the order code, because the route requires `orderId`), **mark returned** (sold only), **mark scrapped** (not when already scrapped), **move** (in stock only, with the destination warehouse and the server's same-warehouse refusal) and **hide/restore** (restore is reachable with *include hidden*).
- Boundaries: no new route and no service change; every action is a modal carrying `AuditReasonField` (`x-audit-reason`), the row's revision travels with each write so a concurrent change surfaces `SERIAL_NUMBER_REVISION_CONFLICT`, and an empty destination warehouse is caught client-side before the request.
- Files: `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/pages/inventory.vue` (tab, list state, register and action modals), `.../composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (`AdminSerialNumber` and eight calls), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.inventory.tabs.serials`, `admin.inventory.serials`, three error strings). The page was already a row in `verify-audit-reason-ui`, so no gate change.
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, both locales parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still only the two pre-existing errors (`admin/crm/customers/duplicates.get.ts` `groups`, `me/orders/index.get.ts` `customerId`).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440 px): registered `PROBE-SN-001`/`PROBE-SN-002` against *Màn khung ngang* in a probe warehouse and saw `All 2 · In stock 2`; marking 001 sold with order `PROBE-ORD-1` moved it to `Sold` with the order in the row; marking it returned moved the counts to `Returned 1`; moving 002 to a second probe warehouse showed `PROBE-WH2 · Revision 2`; scrapping it showed `Scrapped 1`; hiding it removed the row from the default list, and *include hidden* brought it back with the *Hidden* badge; restoring cleared the badge (revision 5). The serial search narrowed the list to 001 and the status filter `Returned` kept only that row. Probe serials, both warehouses and 9 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_serial_numbers 0 / crm_warehouses 0`).
- Fixed while verifying: the register dialog's warehouse select used `value: ''` for "no warehouse", which the select primitive rejects (`A <SelectItem /> must have a value prop that is not an empty string`); it now uses a `__none__` sentinel converted to `null` on submit.
- Harness note: the tab stayed `visibility: hidden`, so clicks on popup options in the action dialogs landed off-viewport; the destination warehouse and the include-hidden switch were operated through the page DOM (focused option + Enter), which is why this run names that explicitly rather than claiming two mouse clicks.
- Not run: no bulk status change (one serial at a time), no barcode-scanner input, no automatic registration from an order (only the purchase-order receipt does that), no warranty/repair history and no CSV export.

### OPS-01 (imports) — one bulk-import dialog for three lists (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: `importCatalogItems`, `importSuppliers` and `importCustomers` were services with `test:catalog-import` 4, `test:supplier-import` 4 and `test:customer-import` 4 behind them, but no admin route and no screen reached any of them, so a legacy spreadsheet could only be loaded by a script.
- Four thin routes, same contract: `POST /api/v1/admin/catalog/items/import`, `/api/v1/admin/procurement/suppliers/import`, `/api/v1/admin/crm/customers/import` and `/api/v1/admin/serial-numbers/import` take `{ rows }`, pass `auditMetaOf` so every row is audited, and answer the service result unchanged; the catalog route also revalidates the storefront when at least one row was written. The row limit (500) and the per-row validation stay in each service, so its own `*_IMPORT_EMPTY` / `*_IMPORT_TOO_LARGE` / row codes are what the caller sees — the route only types `rows` as an array of objects.
- One shared component, `CmsImportDialog.vue`, used by `/catalog`, `/purchasing` (suppliers), `/customers` and `/inventory` (Serials / IMEI). It accepts a JSON array or CSV with a header row; parsing lives in `app/utils/import-rows.ts`: CSV is split with quote support, an empty cell is omitted rather than sent as `''`, a cell becomes a number only when it is a plain number (a leading zero stays text, so a phone number survives), and a cell that looks like JSON is parsed — that is how a translated title or a category list goes into a CSV cell. The dialog shows the parsed row count, blocks Import when the text does not parse, and lists the server's per-row result (row, key, created/updated/failed, error code and message).
- Boundaries: `content.write` only, every request carries the typed audit note, and nothing is written until the operator submits — the dialog never calls the API itself, the page does.
- Serial import reuses the same dialog inside the Serials / IMEI tab: a CSV with `serial,itemId` plus optional `warehouseId,variantId,purchaseOrderId,note`. `importSerialNumbers` only creates, so an already-registered serial (or a repeat inside the file) fails its own row with `SERIAL_NUMBER_DUPLICATE` while the rest are written; the composable maps the service's `serial` to the dialog's key column and reports `updated: 0`.
- Files: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/{catalog/items,procurement/suppliers,crm/customers,serial-numbers}/import.post.ts` (new), `tm-shared/nuxt-layers/cms-base/app/components/CmsImportDialog.vue` (new), `.../app/utils/import-rows.ts` (new), `.../app/composables/{useCatalogAdmin,useCrmAdmin,useOperationsAdmin}.ts` (`importItems`, `importCustomers`, `importSuppliers`, `importSerialNumbers`), `.../app/pages/{catalog,customers,purchasing,inventory}.vue` (button, state, example), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.import`, `admin.inventory.serials.importTitle/importHint`).
- Evidence (gates): `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, both locales parse, `tm-cms nuxt typecheck` still only the two pre-existing errors (`admin/crm/customers/duplicates.get.ts` `groups`, `me/orders/index.get.ts` `customerId`).
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440 px): a catalog JSON import of two rows answered `1 created · 0 updated · 1 failed` with `CATALOG_PAYLOAD_INVALID · A Vietnamese title is required` on the bad row, and the same key imported again answered `0 created · 1 updated · 0 failed`; a supplier CSV (`PROBE-SUP-IMP-1` with a leading-zero phone) answered `1 created` plus one `SUPPLIER_PAYLOAD_INVALID` row with an empty title; a customer CSV answered `1 created` and `CUSTOMER_SUBJECT_REQUIRED` for the row without a subject; typing `[{` showed the parse error and disabled Import. Probe catalog item, supplier, customer and 3 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`catalog_items` back to 241, `crm_suppliers 0`, `crm_customers` probe 0, legacy `suppliers`/`customers` untouched).
- Fixed while verifying: the first locale build failed with `Invalid token in placeholder: '"key":'` because the catalog example in `admin.import.examples.catalog` contained `{`, which `unplugin-vue-i18n` reads as a placeholder; the whole `en` bundle then failed to load and every label rendered its raw key. The examples now live in the page scripts and no message contains a literal brace.
- Also fixed: CSV coercion turned `0900000009` into a number, which the supplier route rejected because `phone` is a string; only plain numbers are coerced now.
- Gate gap closed: `scripts/build/test-serial-import.ts` had existed since the serial service landed but was declared in neither `package.json` nor `run-gates`, so no gate ever ran it — the service could have drifted while `verify:all` stayed green. It is now `test:serial-import` inside `verify:all` (4 checks, disposable `tm_test_serial_import`), and it passes.
- Evidence (browser, serial import, same session): the Serials / IMEI tab shows *Bulk import* next to *Register serials*; a pasted `serial,itemId,note` CSV of three rows answered `2 created · 0 updated · 1 failed`, the repeated serial listed `SERIAL_NUMBER_DUPLICATE · Serial number already registered` on its own row, and the serial list refreshed behind the dialog; `[{` showed the parse error and disabled Import. Called directly, the route answers `rows: []` → `SERIAL_NUMBER_IMPORT_EMPTY`, 501 rows → `SERIAL_NUMBER_IMPORT_TOO_LARGE`, a non-array → `SERIAL_NUMBER_IMPORT_PAYLOAD_INVALID`, i.e. the service's own codes reach the client. Probe serials and their 2 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_serial_numbers` 0).
- Review pass on the first three routes: each carried `min(1).max(500)` itself, so an empty or oversized body was refused by the route's zod schema and the service's specific `*_IMPORT_EMPTY` / `*_IMPORT_TOO_LARGE` codes were unreachable; all four routes now only type `rows` and leave limits and per-row validation to the service. `ImportRowResult.status` also lost a no-op `| string` union — the parser keeps its `empty` state because that is what stops the dialog warning before anything is typed.
- Failed-row export (2026-11-22): a run that fails rows now offers *Download failed rows ({count})* in the result box. The file is `import-failed-YYYY-MM-DD.csv` (UTF-8 with a BOM so Excel keeps the diacritics), and only the failed rows are written — re-importing a created row would either duplicate it or bump its revision for nothing. The header is the failed rows' own columns in first-seen order plus `error_code`/`error_message`, so the reason travels with the row instead of living only in the on-screen list; cells come from the rows the operator submitted (kept in the dialog after a run, `row` is 1-based into that array), not from the server's normalized payload. Quotes are doubled, commas and newlines quoted, and an object cell (a translated title) is written back as JSON text, so the export parses again with the same parser the dialog uses.
- Files (export): `.../app/utils/import-rows.ts` (`failedRowsCsv`, pure string logic), `.../app/components/CmsImportDialog.vue` (button, Blob download), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.import.downloadFailed`), `scripts/build/test-import-csv.ts` (new) declared as `test:import-csv` in `package.json` and in `run-gates` `DOMAIN_GATES`.
- Evidence (gate, export): `pnpm test:import-csv` 4 checks — only the failed row is written and the reason is appended while created/updated rows are absent; quotes, commas and diacritics survive a round trip through `parseImportRows`; an object cell is quoted JSON and a missing message is an empty cell rather than `null`; a run with no failures exports nothing and a result pointing outside the submitted rows is ignored.
- Evidence (browser, export, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session): a catalog import of `probe-csv-1` (valid) and `probe-csv-2` (basePrice -5) answered `1 created · 0 updated · 1 failed` with `CATALOG_PAYLOAD_INVALID · Too small: expected number to be >=0`, and the button produced `import-failed-2026-11-22.csv` (`text/csv;charset=utf-8`, 151 bytes with the BOM) whose body was the header plus exactly the `probe-csv-2` row, title as `"{ ""vi"" : ""Probe lỗi"" }"`. The created row was not in the file. Probe item and its audit row were deleted afterwards (`catalog_items` back to 241).
- Not run (export): the file was not opened in a spreadsheet (the BOM is inferred from the byte count, not from Excel), and the save was captured by intercepting the anchor rather than through the browser's download UI. Review pass (file picker, 2026-11-22): `CmsImportDialog.vue` now reads a `.csv`/`.json` file into the existing textarea via a hidden input (`accept=.csv,.json`) plus a drag-and-drop zone — `fileInput` ref, `dragging`/`dragDepth` counter (no flicker over child elements), `dropEffect = 'copy'`, `readFile` wrapped in try/catch, fileName shown, and state reset on reopen. Verified at 1440 px: selecting a file and dropping one both populated the textarea and showed the file name. Still open: no dry-run showing which rows would match before writing, and no supplier/customer import from a purchase receipt; only the mock session was used.

### HR-01 (attendance export) — the attendance sheet leaves the system as CSV (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: the Attendance tab could page through 62 rows but not hand the month to a bookkeeper, so payroll reconciliation still meant retyping what was on screen. `crm_attendances` had no export route.
- Shape: *Export CSV* next to the month/staff/status filters, a plain link (`UButton href`, write capability only) to `GET /api/v1/admin/hr/attendances/export.csv?month=&staffId=&status=`. It exports the **filter, not the loaded page** — a file truncated at the 50 rows on screen would look complete — by reusing the same `attendanceFilter` helper as `listAttendances`, so the table and the file can never disagree about which rows are in scope.
- Service: `exportAttendancesCsv` reads up to 5000 rows (`x-export-truncated` past the cap instead of a silent cut), resolves `employeeCode`/`fullName` for the page's staff ids in one query (an ObjectId means nothing outside the CMS), and writes `attendanceToCsv`: a `# Chấm công YYYY-MM (n dòng)` comment line, the header `Ngày,Mã nhân sự,Họ tên,Trạng thái,Giờ vào,Giờ ra,Kho,Ghi chú`, the status as the words the screen shows, and empty cells for a missing time/warehouse.
- Route headers: `text/csv; charset=utf-8`, `content-disposition: attachment; filename="cham-cong-YYYY-MM.csv"`, `x-export-rows`, `cache-control: no-store`, and a UTF-8 BOM so Excel keeps the diacritics. An invalid month is refused with `ATTENDANCE_MONTH_INVALID` (422) before any header is written.
- Shared `csvCell`: moved to `tm-cms/server/utils/csv.ts` and reused by the finance ledger and the audit export (they each carried a copy). It doubles quotes, quotes commas/newlines and prefixes `=+-@` cells with an apostrophe, so an operator's note like `=HYPERLINK(...)` stays text in every export, not only in finance.
- Files: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/hr/attendances/export.csv.get.ts` (new), `tm-cms/server/services/attendanceService.ts` (`attendanceFilter`, `attendanceToCsv`, `exportAttendancesCsv`), `tm-cms/server/utils/csv.ts` (new), `tm-cms/server/services/{financeReportService,auditService}.ts` (use the shared cell), `.../app/pages/hr.vue` (`attendanceExportHref`, button), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.hr.attendance.export`, `.exportHint`).
- Evidence (gate): `pnpm test:hr` 22 checks (new: the export answers the month filter with staff code and name, a status filter narrows it, another site gets none, an invalid month is 422, and a leading `=` plus a comma stay one quoted cell with the apostrophe prefix). `test:finance-reports` 10 and `test:audit` still pass on the shared cell. `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:scaffold` 234/234, `pnpm lint` 0.
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440 px): 62 probe rows (2 staff × 31 October days, one note `Nghỉ, "ốm" = ghi chú`) gave `All 62` with 50 rows plus *Load more*; *Export CSV* requested `/api/v1/admin/hr/attendances/export.csv?month=2026-10` and answered `200 text/csv`, `x-export-rows: 62`, `attachment; filename="cham-cong-2026-10.csv"`, 64 lines (comment + header + 62 rows), first three bytes `ef bb bf`, and the messy note as `"Nghỉ, ""ốm"" = ghi chú"`. Probe attendance, both probe staff and 66 audit rows were deleted afterwards (`crm_attendances` 0, `crm_staff_profiles` 0, `catalog_items` 241).
- Not run: the file was not opened in Excel (the BOM is checked as bytes, not as a rendered spreadsheet), there is no xlsx/column choice, and no payroll export.

### HR-01 (payroll export) — the period's payroll leaves the system as CSV (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: payroll rows could be calculated, approved and paid but never handed to the accountant; the tab read at most 100 rows and the numbers stayed inside the CMS, so paying people meant retyping a table.
- Shape: *Export CSV* in the Payroll filter row (the tab already requires `content.write`) as a plain link to `GET /api/v1/admin/hr/payrolls/export.csv?period=&staffId=&status=`. `payrollFilter` is now shared by `listPayrolls` and `exportPayrollsCsv`, so the sheet answers the filter rather than the page on screen; the export is bounded at 5000 rows and says `x-export-truncated` when it cuts.
- Columns: `Kỳ,Mã nhân sự,Trạng thái,Ngày công,Ngày phép,Ngày vắng,Lương tháng,Lương ngày,Lương công,Phụ cấp,Điều chỉnh,Thực lĩnh`, with a `# Bảng lương YYYY-MM (n dòng)` comment line and the status as words. The money is what is **frozen on the row** — an approved or paid month keeps the numbers it was approved with — and base salary, daily rate and base pay travel separately so a total can be checked by hand. No staff lookup is needed: `PayrollAdmin` already carries `employeeCode`.
- Route headers: `text/csv; charset=utf-8`, `content-disposition: attachment; filename="bang-luong-YYYY-MM.csv"`, `x-export-rows`, `cache-control: no-store` (a period of people's pay must not sit in a shared cache), and the UTF-8 BOM. Cells go through the same `csvCell`, so a code like `=CMD|calc` stays text.
- Files: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/admin/hr/payrolls/export.csv.get.ts` (new), `tm-cms/server/services/payrollService.ts` (`payrollFilter`, `payrollToCsv`, `exportPayrollsCsv`), `.../app/pages/hr.vue` (`payrollExportHref`, button), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.hr.payroll.export`, `.exportHint`).
- Evidence (gate): `pnpm test:hr` 23 checks (new: the exact October row of a paid month — `2026-10,NV-001,Đã trả,20,2,2,26000000,1000000,20000000,1000000,500000,21500000`; a cancelled September row still exported as `Đã huỷ`; the status filter; another site empty; an invalid period is `PAYROLL_PERIOD_INVALID` 422; a leading `=` in a code is prefixed). `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:scaffold` 234/234, `pnpm lint` 0.
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440 px): a probe staff with a 26.000.000 salary (26 standard days, 1.000.000 lunch allowance), 7 present and 1 leave day in November plus a 500.000 adjustment gave `All 1 · Draft 1 … Total 8.500.000`; *Export CSV* requested `?period=2026-11` and answered `200 text/csv`, `x-export-rows: 1`, `attachment; filename="bang-luong-2026-11.csv"`, three lines with `2026-11,PROBE-PAY-1,Nháp,7,1,0,26000000,1000000,7000000,1000000,500000,8500000`. Probe staff, config, 8 attendance rows, the payroll row and 13 audit rows were deleted afterwards (all 0, `catalog_items` 241).
- Not run: no payslip PDF or per-person sheet, no xlsx/column choice, and the file was not opened in a spreadsheet.

### HR-01 (self-service payslip) — an employee can print their own payslip (landed 2026-11-22)

- Why: `/my-work` listed one line per payroll row and nothing more. Paying an employee still meant HR emailing a screenshot, and the row could not be printed on its own. `crm_payrolls` already held every figure needed; only a read path and a sheet were missing.
- Shape: a hidden page `/payslip/:id` (no nav entry — it is reached from the payroll row) showing company name from the customer config, employee code/name/position/join date, and the row's own numbers: monthly salary, daily rate with the standard days it divides by, pay for days worked with the worked/leave/absent counts, allowances, each adjustment with its label (negative ones in red), total adjustments, and net pay. A status line follows the state — paid date, draft, approved-waiting, or the cancellation reason — plus a note that printing does not recalculate anything.
- API: `GET /api/v1/me/payroll/:id`. `getOwnPayroll` resolves the staff profile from the verified Hub subject (there is no `staffId` parameter) and returns `PAYROLL_NOT_FOUND` 404 — deliberately not `forbidden` — when the row belongs to someone else, so an employee cannot probe whether a colleague's payroll id exists. `cache-control: no-store`.
- Print: the page carries one non-scoped `@media print` block that hides the app chrome (`body *`), keeps `.payslip` visible and absolute at the top of the paper, drops the border/shadow, and sets `@page { size: A4; margin: 14mm }`; the toolbar (back, print) is `display: none` on paper.
- Files: `tm-cms/server/api/v1/me/payroll/[id].get.ts` (new), `tm-cms/server/services/payrollService.ts` (`getOwnPayroll`), `.../app/pages/payslip/[id].vue` (new), `.../app/pages/my-work.vue` (*Payslip* link), `.../app/composables/useOperationsAdmin.ts` (`myPayslip`), `tm-cms/i18n/locales/{vi,en}.json` (`admin.myWork.payslip.*`).
- Evidence (gate): `pnpm test:hr-self-service` 7 checks (new: the caller reads their own row by id with its employee code; a colleague's real payroll row is refused with `PAYROLL_NOT_FOUND`; a subject without a profile gets `STAFF_NOT_FOUND`). `verify:modules` 32/32, `verify:audit-reason-ui` 63/63, `verify:audit-meta` 4/4, `verify:scaffold` 234/234, `pnpm lint` 0, and typecheck still only the five pre-existing errors.
- Evidence (browser, local CMS :4005 on `sen-tuan`, mock session, 1440 px): a probe staff (26.000.000 monthly, 26 standard days, 1.300.000 allowances, 18 present + 2 leave days, +500.000 bonus and −2.000.000 advance, approved then paid) listed `Paid · Net pay 17.800.000`; the *Payslip* link pointed at `/payslip/<id>` and the page rendered `18.000.000 + 1.300.000 + 500.000 − 2.000.000 = 17.800.000` with `Paid on 2026-11-22`; a colleague's payroll id rendered the "no payslip with this id" state; the print rule is present in exactly one stylesheet. Probe staff, configs, 20 attendance rows, both payroll rows and 29 audit rows were deleted afterwards (all 0, `catalog_items` 241).
- Not run: the sheet itself was not printed or saved as PDF (only the rule was inspected), there is no signature/company stamp block, and no email delivery of the payslip.
- Review pass (error handling, 2026-11-22): `payslip/[id].vue` `load()` is status-aware — HTTP 404 (or `PAYROLL_NOT_FOUND`/`STAFF_NOT_FOUND`) reads as "no payslip" (`STAFF_NOT_FOUND` → `admin.myWork.noProfile`, otherwise `admin.myWork.payslip.notFound`); any other status keeps `describeError(e)` (401/403/503/network/generic) plus the retry button, so a transient failure is retryable instead of being misread as not-found. `statusOf` reads `statusCode`/`status`/`response.status`; `errorCode` reads `data.data.error.code || data.error.code`. Verified at 1440 px: a mock admin with no linked staff profile renders the not-found state with the retry button present.
